# Changelog and incident notes

Dated notes on behaviour changes and the incidents behind them, in reverse order. The
full session record is [`SESSION_LOGS.md`](SESSION_LOGS.md); the running engineering
state lives outside the repository (`RECODE_STATUS.md` in the operator's `roop-keep`
folder). Entries before 2026-09-21 were moved here from the README on 2026-09-22.

## 2026-10-26

- **React UI: the 3-stage pipeline controls and live telemetry exposed on #/faceswap, #/settings, #/processing and Diagnostics; the two CI failures fixed (every push since 10-25 had failed).** Rules in `react-ui/README.md` "3-stage pipeline controls and telemetry".
  *CI, both reproduced before touching anything.* (1) **`npm run typecheck`**: `react-ui/mock-server/server.ts:478-479` `Property 'kind' does not exist` (spreading a `Record<string, unknown>` drops its keys); `PipelineActive` interface added. (2) **Python light job**: `test_recognition_registry.py` -> `roop/recognition_registry.py` import `requests` at module top, which `app/requirements-ci.txt` lacked (installed locally, so no local run could see it); collection stopped at the first error and hid more. Reproduced with a fresh venv from `requirements-ci.txt` + `ROOP_TEST_LIGHT=1`; `requests` added, which exposed 4 `test_verify_ort.py` tests that copy the real `onnxruntime-gpu` distribution metadata and cannot run on a runner without it: skipped with that reason (not weakened). After: 2329 passed, 843 skipped, 0 failed locally (before: a collection error). **It took four more pushes to get GitHub green (run 40233300979, all four jobs), because each fix exposed the next layer that only a runner shows:** (3) `update_manifest.json` is stale whenever a hashed file changes (`react-ui/package.json`): regenerated; (4) ~30 tests drive a real `ffmpeg`/`ffprobe` that the runners lack (`test_finalize_output`, `test_segment_output`, `test_queue_segments`, `test_distributed_render`, stage-12 video I/O, and my own `test_qsv_encode`): ffmpeg is now installed in the job (apt / choco) rather than the tests skipped, since a skip says nothing about whether the mux/join code works; (5) `test_hardware_portability` assumed a 10+ core host (Settings caps `max_threads` at the logical core count; a 4-core runner turned every expectation into 4): the file now pins the core count like it pins the GPU; (6) `test_windows_runtime_compat` patched `os.add_dll_directory`, which exists only on Windows: `create=True`; (7) `test_stage10_pipeline_concurrency` compared two wall-clock samples of a sleep-based simulation with a 35% band and a Windows runner measured a 37% gap: it now compares the median of three sweeps (exact assertions still on every sweep). `requests` is still not a declared runtime dependency in `app/requirements.txt` (transitive).
  *Controls (`faceswap/PipelineControls.jsx`: one component on two tabs, editing the one settings object).* Swapper model (the five named models first; `realswap` and the whole `/api/meta` list kept as `<optgroup>`s, `ui.jsx` `Select` gained optional groups). **Restorer mode** = one choice over three real settings: *Off / Auto-Bypass >=256px* turns `auto_bypass_restorer_native_swap` on and leaves the restorer alone; *Laplacian Split RF++* = `Restore Ultra` + `restore_ultra_frequency_blend`; *Full RF++* = `Restoreformer++`; any other restorer (UltraMax is the shipped one) reads as a read-only **Custom**. Pre-pass optical-flow stride K 1..8 (`prepass_detect_interval`) and Temporal Smart-Skip. The stride / bypass / skip keys are in `LIVE_ENV_SETTINGS`, so they apply on the next render and the Face Swap placement is truthful. It replaces the Face Swap "Swap model" select and auto-bypass toggle and three Settings rows, and has no run button (the run bar stays the one Start). Rules are in `faceswap/pipelineRules.js`, named so it cannot collide by case with `PipelineControls.jsx` (on Windows `./pipelineControls` resolved to the component; found by the unreachable-modules test).
  *Telemetry.* Stage 1 in keyframes/s. Stage 2's tracked share is named for its engine only when `flow_backend` was reported (`nvof` -> "NVOFA tracked", otherwise plain "flow"; the live snapshot now carries `flow_backend`, `stage_telemetry.snapshot`). Stage 3 labels follow the backend's own backend names: "TRT Graph" only for a CUDA-graph replay, "Triton Blend" only for a fused kernel (today the blend is cv2 and says so). **Canary precision status** (`CanaryPrecisionStatus`): `PRECISION.canary_verdicts` + the live `engine_state` -> PASSED / FAILED (with reason) / RUNNING / NONE / UNKNOWN; no verdict is NONE, never PASSED. Shown in the Diagnostics HUD, the Settings "Last run report", and it now leads the PRECISION card of the 14-section structured report (the ten-row slice used to cut the verdicts off).
  *Mock server + offline.* `mock-server/pipelineTypes.ts`: typed `StageTelemetry` / `OpsReport` / `PrecisionValues` / `CanaryVerdict` and builders; the mock's `/ws/telemetry` carries `stage/stages/summary` while a job runs, `/api/runtime/state` carries the sections (it returned only `{state, initialized}`), `/api/meta` lists the native high-res swappers. `scripts/verify-offline-bundle.mjs` (+ self-test, `npm run verify:offline`, a CI step): dist/ names no http(s) host except `www.w3.org` (XML namespaces), `react.dev` (React error text) and `tailwindcss.com` (licence banner), and no external URL in any fetch position.
  *Verified (4070 box, quiet machine).* `npm run lint` clean; root `typecheck` 0 errors; `lint:unreachable` 136/136; `test:render-checks` all green, including new `render-pipeline-controls.mjs` (76 checks: every restorer mode round-trips from every starting state, handlers driven through the element tree, a skipped canary is UNKNOWN not verified); **Playwright + axe 132 passed, 0 failed, 38 skipped (the opt-in per-theme contrast run)**, including 13 new in `pipeline-controls.spec.js` (autosave POSTs read, keyboard-only stride/toggle, one Start control, canary states, axe on all of it); axe on the 9 screens still zero with an empty allowlist. Face Swap tab stops 258 -> 254 (ratcheted). Python: light CI profile 2329 passed / 0 failed; UI / settings / telemetry / standalone-install / smart-bypass subset 365 passed.
  *Not done, said plainly.* (a) **The full Python suite was NOT re-run**: the harness killed it at 71% for low memory. It showed the same 30 F / 15 E pattern as the pre-change full run (the known order-dependent and `test_inference_engine` set) plus two `test_smart_restorer_bypass.py` failures, which pass now: one was mine (the test requires `auto_bypass_restorer_native_swap` to stay catalogued, so it is kept and bound to the Restorer mode control), the other is order-sensitive. (b) The opt-in per-theme contrast run (`test:e2e:themes`) was not run; the new components use only existing colour tokens. (c) `trt_matrix` has no test of its own (`test_precommit_scratch_guard.py` pins the harness file and passes). (d) Nothing was run against a real backend: controls and telemetry are checked against the mock and recorded payload shapes. (e) The first baseline e2e run had one `long task` budget failure (`frame-budget`, job running on #/faceswap) while other suites ran on the same machine; it did not recur on a quiet machine. (f) The Restorer mode's `MOD` marker tracks `selected_enhancer` only; resetting it does not reset the other two settings the mode can write.

- **"Heterogeneous Intel + NVIDIA co-processing" brief (a re-issue of 10-22): the OpenVINO half already shipped and was re-verified end to end; the "split-GOP NVENC + QuickSync segments" half is MEASURED UNSAFE as specified (a mixed-encoder HEVC join exits 0 and loses a third of the frames), so QuickSync was built as a whole-render encoder and a job-start fallback instead. No QuickSync frame has been encoded: the bundled ffmpeg 8.1.2 has no `*_qsv` encoder and neither machine has an Intel GPU enabled.**
  *Part 1, already existed (named sites, tests run).* `roop/openvino_runner.py` (`OpenVinoAsyncSession`, one `AsyncInferQueue` per compiled shape, `hint.performance_mode=THROUGHPUT`, `NUM_STREAMS` read back, fail-closed INT8), seams `face_util.py:233` (`_offload_aux_models`: SCRFD + ArcFace, which is the Stage 1 pre-scan's detector), `retinaface.py:350` (`_build_one`), `processors/Mask_XSeg.py:84` (`Initialize`, Stage 3 mask). Off unless `ROOP_OPENVINO_NATIVE`. Re-run in a fresh scratch venv (`openvino` 2026.4.1 + `nncf`): `test_openvino_runner.py` 19 passed. Premise still wrong: `ov.properties.streams.Num.THROUGHPUT` does not exist in 2026.x.
  *New: the SCRFD and XSeg seams run end to end* (they were only exercised through the runner before). `tests/probe_openvino_seams_e2e.py`, 24 frames of Love.mp4, the user's TensorRT config vs `ROOP_OPENVINO_NATIVE=CPU` (OpenVINO's CPU device stands in for an Intel accelerator; `openvino` borrowed by `PYTHONPATH`, app venv untouched): both seams became `OpenVinoAsyncSession` and executed (runner calls xseg 24, scrfd 12 + 12 over the pool's two instances, 8 streams, 8 queue requests). XSeg mask: mean |d| 6e-6, IoU 1.0000, max |d| 0.10. SCRFD: 23 of 24 frames identical face sets, bbox diff up to 2.4 px; frame 14 gains one face scored exactly 0.500 against `det_thresh` 0.5 (borderline, not a miss). Numbers `docs/perf/openvino_seams/seams_e2e_cpu_device.json`. Still UNVERIFIED: any Intel NPU / iGPU / Arc, the 3060, speed on any of them.
  *Defect found by that probe, fixed:* `baseline_probe.log_session` printed `!! bound OpenVINOExecutionProvider but TensorrtExecutionProvider was requested first` (the alarm for a silent drop to CPU) for a session routed there ON PURPOSE. A `native_runner` session now logs `(routed to ... by ROOP_OPENVINO_NATIVE)`; test in `test_baseline_probe.py`.
  *Part 2, premise measured: stream-copy joining of two encoders' segments is not safe for HEVC.* `tests/probe_mixed_encoder_concat.py` -> `docs/perf/qsv_encode/mixed_encoder_concat.json` (ffmpeg 8.1.2): 60-frame segments `hevc_nvenc` + `libx265` + `hevc_nvenc` through the concat demuxer (what `SegmentedVideoWriter` and `distributed_render` use) exit 0 and read back **120 of 180 frames with 71 decoder-error lines**; the same-encoder control (NVENC + NVENC) is 120/120, 0 errors. Re-wrapping through Annex B into MP4 loses frames even in the control (no timestamps in a raw stream), so it is no repair; only the raw elementary stream decodes all 180 and nothing downstream takes one. H.264 is fine for that pair (`h264_nvenc` + `libx264` + `h264_nvenc` = 180/180). x265 stands in for QuickSync, so NVENC + QSV is UNMEASURED; the default codec is HEVC. `SegmentedVideoWriter`'s "refusing to concat mixed-codec segments" is therefore correct and now has a test. The distributed path would catch it only AFTER the join (`_count(joined) != expected`), because `_signature` compares codec/size/pix_fmt/colour and not parameter sets; not changed.
  *Built (whole-render, one encoder per file).* `roop/qsv_encode.py`: `rate_control_args` (`-global_quality` + `-preset`, `ROOP_QSV_PRESET`), NV12 output (QuickSync encoders reject a forced yuv420p), `choose_encoder`. `ffmpeg_writer._build_cmd`/`probe_encoder`, `util_ffmpeg._rate_control` handle `*_qsv`; before this a `hevc_qsv` writer was handed `-crf` and could never open. `core.ensure_video_encoder()` (the job-start pre-flight, `core.py` ~2001): a configured NVENC encoder that fails its probe now falls to its own-family QSV codec for THIS render only (nothing persisted), instead of aborting the job; a QSV failure keeps the old abort and says why. `api._VIDEO_CODECS` / `advanced_settings_manager` offer `h264_qsv`/`hevc_qsv`/`av1_qsv`, hidden by the existing `ffmpeg -encoders` filter where the build lacks them (all three are hidden here). `ROOP_QSV=0` removes it. Docs `docs/ENV_FLAGS.md`.
  *Tests.* `tests/test_qsv_encode.py` 25: exact argv for each QSV codec and unchanged NVENC / x265 argv, the real ffmpeg's answer for the missing `hevc_qsv`, the pre-flight decision table, `core.ensure_video_encoder` and `api._available_video_codecs` call sites, the mixed-codec guard. 12 of them fail with `ffmpeg_writer.py` / `core.py` / `api.py` / `util_ffmpeg.py` stashed.
  *Not a speed-up, said plainly.* `nvenc_quality.py` measured NVENC at ~440 fps at 720p against a 6-30 fps render, and the render is GPU-bound: moving encode to an Intel iGPU cannot shorten it. The gain is that a render which aborted at the pre-flight now runs. "NVENC sessions saturated" never happens inside one render (one writer, one session); it can at job start, which is where the fallback sits.
  *Found, NOT fixed: a hardware encoder that fails to open at segment 2 or later kills the render.* `tests/probe_segment_encoder_failure.py` (real ffmpeg; `docs/perf/qsv_encode/segment_encoder_failure.txt`): segment 1 on real `h264_nvenc`, segment 2's encoder dies at open -> `OSError: [Errno 32] Broken pipe` at frame 51, the software fallback never spawns. `FFMPEG_VideoWriter._retry_as_software` only retries while `_frames_written == 0`, but the first write into the dead pipe lands in the OS buffer and counts, so the death is seen one write too late. Even a retry would then hit the mixed-codec guard above (correctly). Committed segments survive for a resume. A real fix needs a same-encoder retry with the segment's first frames kept for replay; that is a design call, not done here.
  *Not done / unverified.* A QuickSync encode (the argv is tested, never run); NVENC + QSV segment compatibility; the Intel NPU/iGPU/Arc runs of Part 1; the 3060; no regression benchmark (no default-path code changed: the pre-flight differs only when the configured encoder fails its probe).

- **"Fused Stage 3 post-processing + manual brush + L2 persistence" brief (a re-issue of 10-22): most of it already existed; two missing pieces BUILT into the fused kernel (keep-original matte, low-band mix) plus a CPU/non-Triton fallback; the brief's brush sign is WRONG and was not implemented; the in-kernel inverse warp and the L2 window were priced, not built. Still not wired into a render.**
  *Already existed (named site, tests run: 23 passed in `test_fused_postprocess.py` before this change).* `app/core/fused_postprocess.py`: LAB Reinhard on the tile in registers (`DO_CT`), per-Face-ID EMA with scene-cut reset (`LabStatsEMA`, alpha 0.15 settable, EMA measured WORSE for tone pumping 10-22, `docs/perf/color_ema/`), frequency split (`detail_weight`), feather (`feather_sigma`), luminance grain from the target's noise floor (`estimate_noise_sigma`/`grain_deficit`), composite into a strided ROI view so the rest of the frame is untouched. `app/core/l2_persistence.py` (`pin_arena` default 16 MiB, `mark_streaming`).
  *Premise corrected: the brush.* The canvas Mask Brush reaches the render as `imagemask` JSON -> `ProcessMgr` `face_masks[faceset] = {exclude_mask, include_mask, ref_kps, is_canonical}` (`ProcessMgr.py:1438-1500`) and is applied in canonical crop space (`ProcessMgr.py:6734-6800`): `combined = exclude; combined *= (1 - include)`, then `fake = fake*(1-c) + aligned*c`. So painted = KEEP THE ORIGINAL, the include brush only cancels an exclusion, and nothing can switch the swap ON where XSeg refused it. The brief's `alpha = xseg*feather + paint - erase` makes paint ADD swap: the opposite. `test_the_briefs_sign_would_swap_where_production_keeps_the_original` pins it.
  *Built.* `keep_mask` on `fused_postprocess` / `reference_postprocess`: `alpha = blur(xseg) * (1 - keep)`, exactly the production composite when the original crop equals the plate (`test_keep_equals_production_crop_space_composite`, 1e-6; with a real warp round trip it is approximate, UNVERIFIED on a render). `manual_keep_mask(exclude, include, *overrides)` builds it in production order and UNIONS mouth/eye restore overrides after the include brush so the brush cannot cancel them. `low_weight` (share of the swapper's low band; 0.8 = "keep 80% of its lighting"), with `detail_weight=1.0` for 100% of the restorer's high band; default 1.0/0.75 is the old maths (`low=0, detail=1` returns the restorer exactly: test). `postprocess()` + `select_backend()`: Triton on an NVIDIA CUDA tensor, the eager torch reference everywhere else; a FORCED `backend="triton"` that cannot run raises instead of falling back. **No `torch.compile` rung:** Inductor needs a C++ compiler this install lacks, so it could not be tested.
  *Tests.* `tests/test_fused_postprocess.py` +12 (kernel == reference for keep fp32/fp16, keep=1 returns the plate bit for bit, keep=0 == no keep bit for bit, brushed region original / rest still swapped, low_weight grid, dispatcher runs the KERNEL not the fallback on CUDA); new `tests/test_fused_postprocess_fallback.py` (CPU, 11). 46 passed together (23 + 12 + 11); light suite 2322 passed.
  *Measured (4070, B=4, 512 px ROI, `tests/probe_fused_manual_mask.py`, `docs/perf/fused_postprocess/manual_mask_probe.json`; the GPU was shared at 37% utilisation, so the absolute 1.65-1.95 ms runs above 10-22's 1.49).* Null spread 0.10-0.14 ms (6-8%): keep_mask +2.2% / low-band mix 0.0% = not measurable. Bicubic warp of the kernel's 8 input channels 0.72 ms (30% of kernel + warp) vs bilinear 0.19 ms.
  *In-kernel inverse warp: priced, NOT built.* A fused warp must also resample each tile's blur halo: the same sampler at 1.78x the pixels costs 1.25 ms (+0.53 ms), against the only thing fusing saves, 64 MB of intermediate traffic (~0.13 ms at ~500 GB/s). Net about +0.4 ms WORSE as a compute lower bound. This is a proxy, not the fused kernel: UNVERIFIED by construction.
  *L2 window: not redone.* 10-22 measured the 16 MiB persisting window at 0.0 / -3.8 / -12.5% for 4 / 8 / 16 MiB on the real 6.3 MB working set (`docs/perf/fused_postprocess/`); the `.fsz` bank is a few tens of KB. Unchanged.
  *Not done, said plainly.* Not wired into any render (production swap/restore outputs are host numpy and the pipeline is GPU-bound; ~2 s of a 42 s render, see the Stage 3 entries); no end-to-end A/B; no pixel comparison against the real crop-space path on a rendered clip; no 3060; `Stream_Infer` pinning needs the (absent) 3-stream pipeline.

## 2026-10-25

- **Pre-pass session build, measured across jobs: the lead is confirmed. Every job rebuilds the `buffalo_l` analyser pool INSIDE its pre-pass: ~4.0 s of a ~7.0 s pre-pass (57-58%) on 150 frames, identical in every warm job.** Probe `app/tests/probe_prepass_session_build.py`, numbers `docs/perf/prepass_session_build/` (Weeds, K=1, 4070, HEAD 2a4a93f, one process, jobs back to back; changes nothing in the product).
  *Numbers.* Warm jobs 1-3 (job 0 is the cold one): pre-pass 6.96 / 7.09 / 7.14 s, of which `face_util._build_face_analyser` runs twice (pool of 2, strictly serial, at +0.3 s and +2.4 s into the pre-pass) for 4.03 / 4.03 / 4.17 s, leaving 2.93 / 3.06 / 2.98 s for detecting the 150 frames. A repeat run gave 3.96 s (job 1). First-inference warm-up inside the window is small (0.64-0.66 s total across 12 first runs). Phase 14's "~5 s before frame 4" = ~4.0 s analyser build + ~0.65 s first runs.
  *Why job 2 pays it again.* `core.release_resources()` runs at the START of every render (`core.py:1451`, `batch_process_with_options` `:1565`) as well as at its end (`api.py` `_run_swap`), and `release_face_analyser()` drops the pool; the pre-pass then builds it again. `release_resources` itself takes 1.5-2.6 s and returns 3.9 GB (1.4 GB in the cold job).
  *Scale.* The cost is fixed per job, so its share falls with clip length: 57% of this 150-frame pre-pass, ~23% of Phase 14's 17.4 s / 900-frame pre-pass, and ~6% of a ~65-70 s 600-frame render by arithmetic only (not an end-to-end measurement).
  *Found on the way, outside the pre-pass.* Every job also rebuilds the swapper session (`FaceSwapInsightFace.py:906`, built from bytes via `strict_session.py:330`): 5.0-5.4 s for the first one plus 1.3 / 0.9 / 0.85 s for the next three. That is ~8 s per job, larger than the pre-pass cost, and not looked at further. The ~33 s of "setup before the pre-pass" in each job is the harness's own (`init_pipeline`, first-face capture, faceset load) and was not decomposed: do not read it as app cost.
  *Not done, said plainly.* No fix: keeping the analyser across jobs trades against the 3.9 GB `release_resources` frees for the swapper/enhancer, and the VRAM governor headroom was not measured. Building the two pool instances in parallel would save up to ~2 s but ORT/TRT session creation contention is unmeasured. One clip, one GPU, no 3060, no null control beyond the three warm jobs agreeing within 0.14 s. My first run's "first face frame" marker was wrong (`smooth` first runs at the END of the pre-pass) and was removed before the numbers above were taken.

- **"VRAM-resident Stage 3" brief, re-issued with a Kornia fallback and `.fsz` v2 pose embeddings: the two kernels it asks for were MEASURED on the real engine; both work, neither is worth wiring; nothing in the product changed.** Probe `app/tests/probe_stage3_graph_and_warp.py` (not collected), numbers `docs/perf/stage3_resident_graph/` (4070, HEAD 2a4a93f, TF32 off, cv2 on one thread).
  *Bucketed CUDA graphs over a TensorRT swapper: capture WORKS, replay is bit-identical (max |replay - eager| = 0.0 at B = 1/2/4/8) on `.cache/trt_engines/hyperswap_1a_256_sm89_fp16_b8.engine`.* Replay saves 0.52 / 0.54 / 0.23 / 0.04 ms per call at B = 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 against 5.07 / 7.80 / 16.93 / 33.35 ms of engine time (10% -> 0.1%): it removes launch overhead, not GPU work. B = 16 is not available, the engine's optimisation profile ends at 8 (the `(1, 8, 16)` specs added in 2a4a93f are for the enhancers and XSeg). Per face the swapper costs 5.07 (B=1) / 3.90 (B=2) / 4.23 (B=4) / 4.17 ms (B=8): batching buys ~18% on this one model and flattens at 2, in line with 10-11 ("swap batching ON is slower", avg batch 1.5).
  *Batched GPU warp: `CudaAffineBatch.warp_frames` (in tree, bicubic `grid_sample`) is the arm to use; the Kornia arm is slower and, as called here, wrong.* 1080p, 256 px crops: cv2 loop 0.78 / 3.14 / 6.78 / 13.38 ms for B = 1 / 4 / 8 / 16 vs `CudaAffineBatch` 0.56 / 0.58 / 0.95 / 1.90 ms with the frames already in VRAM. Pixel error vs cv2: mean 0.284/255, max 3.4-4.4 (the OpenCV 1/32-px quantisation floor `CudaAffineBatch` documents). `kornia.warp_affine` (bicubic, border, `align_corners=False`) is 0.78-2.6 ms and sits mean 5.6-6.1 / max 39-57 levels off cv2: a half-pixel/normalisation convention that this probe did not chase, because the in-tree class already does the job. **CV-CUDA is still not installable** (`import cvcuda` fails, no Windows wheel).
  *The catch is the host boundary.* With HOST frames the GPU warp costs 2.0 / 6.3 / 12.3 / 23.5 ms (upload dominates, ~1.5 ms per 1080p frame), 3-4x WORSE than the CPU loop it replaces. It only wins when decode lands in VRAM (`NvZeroCopyReader`), and `video_io_hw.py` is still not on the render path (Phase 13).
  *Why not built.* Best case across both kernels is ~0.7 ms of warp CPU time plus ~0.5 ms of launch overhead per face, against a face that costs ~30 ms of GPU (swap 4.2 + RF++ ~25 + masks) in a GPU-bound pipeline where thread-time savings have measured neutral three times. The production swapper and XSeg are ORT sessions, so graphs would first mean replacing them with raw TensorRT contexts (single-input `NativeEngine` handles RF++ only) and re-proving identity. "Eliminate ALL `cv2.warpAffine/resize/GaussianBlur` from Stage 3" is 392 call sites in 88 files under `app/roop/` (`procmgr_masking.py` 29, `face_swapper.py` 38, `ProcessMgr.py` 22 ...); mouth/eye/expression/lip-sync restores run on host frames after the paste.
  *Not done, said plainly.* No end-to-end A/B (nothing is wired); no 3060 (TensorRT is not admitted there); XSeg and RF++ graph capture not measured (only the swapper); the 3-stream ring was priced on 10-19 (`stage12`) and not re-run. `pose_embedding_for_target` (500dfca) calls `get_pose_matched_embedding` per face with a GPU->CPU copy and rebuilds the bank vectors on every call; its cost per frame is UNMEASURED. **Still the one defensible slice:** ROI-bounded GPU paste behind `paste_upscale` (`CudaAffineBatch.paste_faces_roi` exists, unwired), pixel-parity test first, then a counterbalanced 600-frame A/B with a null.

- **"NVOFA box propagation + every-frame landmarks + 1-Euro + trajectory cache" brief (a re-issue of Phase 14): all four parts already exist; the headline "7-8x Stage 2" is not reachable; nothing changed in the product, one harness arm added.**
  *Named sites, each with a passing test (169 + 91 passed, run alone).* Interval + flow between detections: `roop/prepass_policy.py`, `roop/flow_coast.py`, `roop/flow_backend.py` (`prepass_detect_interval`, default 1; `test_prepass_interval*.py`, `test_flow_*.py`). Neural landmarks on the flow box, every flow-placed frame: `roop/landmark_refresh.py` (`ROOP_PREPASS_LANDMARK_REFRESH`, default OFF; `test_landmark_refresh.py`). Vectorized zero-lag 1-euro on the 5 / 106 / 68 sets: `roop/one_euro_vec.py` (`prepass_landmark_filter=one_euro`, default `adaptive`; `test_one_euro_vec.py`). M / M_inv / pose per face per frame, memory-mapped, saved with the project checkpoint, read by the HUD: `roop/trajectory_cache.py`, `routes_trajectory.py`, `app/stage_checkpoint.py`, `react-ui/.../HeadPoseHud.jsx` (`test_trajectory_cache.py`, `test_stage_cache.py`).
  *Why 7-8x cannot happen (Weeds, 900 frames, 4070, HEAD 500dfca, `docs/perf/phase14/recheck_2026-10-25/`).* Pre-pass wall time K=1 17.4 s (two bit-identical runs) / K=4 13.9 s (-20%) / K=8 12.7 s (-27%), at 1.00 / 0.26 / 0.13 detector calls per frame. Every pre-pass spends ~5 s before frame 4 (ten `Applied providers` session-creation lines are logged inside that window; not timed, so the cause is unproven) and then runs ~85 fps at K=1, ~165 fps at K>=4, so even zero detector cost leaves >= ~5 s of 17.4 (3.5x at the very best), and the pre-pass is ~15-25% of a job. End to end (600 frames, counterbalanced `base,k4,k4r,k4r,k4,base,base`, realswap + GPEN Realistic, 20 threads; the first arm paid a 451 s TensorRT build and is excluded): base 69.1 / 71.1 s, K=4 65.5 / 70.0 s, K=4 + refresh 67.1 / 68.9 s = **-3%, inside the +-5% noise: not measurable**.
  *The 10-21 swap-loss verdict no longer reproduces, but the replacement is not a win either.* Phase 14 measured K=4 at -9.6% swapped faces. At HEAD the audit reads base 442 / K=4 478 (+8.1%) and plan frames 429 -> 473 (the audit counts INTENT). Judged on pixels against the ORIGINAL clip (`swapflags.py`; base vs base and K=4 vs K=4 differ in 0 of 600 frames): base marks 278 frames as replaced, K=4 287; 19 frames change status, K=4 loses 5 (24, 287-289, 351) and gains 14 (18, 25, 30, 175, 177-179, 189, 216-217, 220-222, 357). The gained frames are turned-away / frame-edge heads (178, 221, 351 inspected by eye), the class this project has repeatedly found swapped wrongly; not judged correct. `prepass_detect_interval` stays 1.
  *Landmark refresh runs and costs ~0.7 s per 900 frames* (831 of 887 flow-placed faces re-measured, 56 refused) but cannot show up in the probe's NME, which reads the 5-point `kps` while the refresh rewrites the 106-point landmarks; K=4 vs K=4 + refresh differ in 2 of 600 frames end to end. Still no evidence it improves a swap.
  *Not met, said plainly.* (1) NVIDIA optical flow (`cv2.cuda_NvidiaOpticalFlow_2_0`): this OpenCV has no CUDA, so the NVOFA rung is dispatch-only against a fake engine (UNVERIFIED on hardware); the shipped rung is CPU `cv2` LK at 0.14-0.23 ms per call. (2) The cache's `bbox` is the DETECTION box; a tight destination ROI `[x1, y1, x2, y2]` for paste-back is NOT stored (it depends on the swapper crop, template and restorer sizes; Stage 3 does not read the cache, `test_stage3_does_not_read_it`). (3) Every-frame landmark net is opt-in and un-A/B'd for swap quality. (4) RTX 3060 unmeasured.
  *Lead, not built:* the ~5 s session build inside every pre-pass. `core.release_resources()` -> `face_util.release_face_analyser()` runs at job end, so a second job probably pays it again; cause and per-job repeat not measured here. *Harness:* `ab_phase14_e2e.py` gained a `k4r` arm and now sets `ROOP_PREPASS_LANDMARK_REFRESH` explicitly on every arm.

- **"Dual-path hardware video I/O + segment overwrite" brief: most of it already existed; three real defects found and fixed, two premises corrected, one item deliberately not done.**
  *Already existed (named site + test).* `NvZeroCopyReader` (`ThreadedDecoder`, `useDeviceMemory=True`, `RGBP`, `bufferSize=16`, `torch.from_dlpack`) and `NvZeroCopyWriter` (`CreateEncoder`, NV12 from VRAM) are in `app/roop/video_io_hw.py` (not `app/core/`: `app/roop/core.py` is a module and a sibling named `core` is the shadowing trap of 5609800). `start_frame` seeks to the preceding IDR and discards the pre-trim pictures inside the decoder: bit-identical to a full decode at start 0/30/40/45/46/59/60 (`test_start_frame_seek_lands_on_the_requested_frame`, `test_trim_window_is_bit_exact_...`). A per-split output folder per brief item 3 did NOT exist (see below). The distributed CLI (`roop/distributed_render.py`) already renders into a private `<output>.chunks` workspace and refuses a non-empty one; it cannot overwrite.
  *Premise corrected: NVENC options.* The brief's `enableAQ=True` / `enableTemporalAQ=True` are accepted and IGNORED by `PyNvVideoCodec.CreateEncoder` (byte-identical to not passing them); the spellings that change the stream are `multipass="fullres"`, `aq=1`, `temporalaq=1` (`roop/nvenc_quality.py`, `test_nvenc_quality.py`, `docs/perf/nvenc_quality/`). Measured 10-22: spatial AQ is a consistent LOSS (+12..16% bytes at equal face PSNR) and P6+fullres+both AQs encodes ~40% slower than P5 with no gain beyond the +-3% scatter. So the writer's defaults stay P4 / off and the bundle stays `ROOP_NVENC_QUALITY_FIRST=1`. NOT changed; the measurement is the deliverable.
  *Defect 1 - `NvZeroCopyReader.read_batch` returned a WRONG FIRST FRAME.* The frame fetched at open (the "peek") sits in a decoder surface that the next `get_batch_frames` call recycles. The first frame of the first batch differed from a full decode at every start offset; single `read()` and every other frame were exact. Not hit in production (the reader is not on the render path), found by the new trim test. Fixed by copying the peeked frame on the device before completing the batch; `test_the_first_batch_after_open_is_exact`.
  *Built: trim window.* `end_frame` (exclusive) on `NvZeroCopyReader` and `LegacyCaptureReader`; `trim_range()` / `timecode_to_frame()` convert `[trim_in, trim_out)` seconds; `open_reader(trim_in=, trim_out=)`. `read`/`read_batch` hand out nothing past `trim_out`. CFR conversion; VFR UNVERIFIED.
  *Defect 2 - an interactive frame request could wait for the full 15 s API deadline.* `/api/target/preview`, the filmstrip and `/api/preview` decoded through one shared `cv2.VideoCapture` behind `_capture_lock`, and `capturer._extract_fallback_frame` ran ffmpeg with NO timeout, so one slow seek or a hung ffmpeg queued every later request until the client gave up ("Frame unavailable - Retry"). New `roop/preview_provider.py` `InteractiveFramePreviewProvider`: 2 s deadline (`PreviewTimeout` -> `503 {busy, retry_after_ms}`, which `retryPolicy.busyDelay` already treats as "ask again", not a failure), its own daemon decode thread, newest-request-first, abandoned/superseded requests dropped without decoding, identical requests share one decode, a decode that outlives its request lands in the cache so the retry is a hit, 32-frame LRU under a byte budget (`ROOP_PREVIEW_CACHE_MB`, default 256) with copy-in/copy-out. The filmstrip has ONE 8 s budget for up to 64 stills. `_extract_fallback_frame` now times out at 10 s. `tests/test_preview_provider.py` (stalled decoder answers inside the deadline; the retry is a cache hit; a held real `capturer._capture_lock` costs the caller the deadline, not 15 s; route-level 503s).
  *What this is NOT.* It does not own a decoder - it bounds the wait on `capturer.get_video_frame`. Stage 3 does not take `_capture_lock` (grep: only `capturer.py`), so a scrub never queued behind the render's decoder and the render never queued behind a scrub DECODE. One coupling remains: `core.release_resources()` (render teardown) calls `release_video()`, which takes `_capture_lock`, so teardown can wait for a preview seek in flight (bounded now by the 10 s ffmpeg timeout). The live websocket PLAY stream (`_ws_frame_source`) and `preview_seq` still call `get_video_frame` directly (sequential, not random access). UNMEASURED: a real scrub against a live render (the render at hand finished before the change could be exercised).
  *Defect 3 - segment overwrite, the part that was still open.* The 10-18 fix named slices `{stem}_segment{idx}_{start}_{end}.mp4` in the output folder. Two splits of clips that share a file name, or two splits of one clip with different settings, still wrote the same paths; and an ordinary job queued between two slices, with `clear_output` on, wiped the finished slices. Slices now go to `{output_dir}/.segments/{group}/seg_{idx:04d}_{start}_{end}.mp4` (`roop/segment_output.py`; `{group}` is the queue's split id, i.e. the brief's `job_id` - one split render, stamped on every slice; unsafe ids are sanitised + digest-suffixed), `api._clear_output_folder` keeps `.segments`, and the join is unchanged (`<stem>_joined.mp4` + `<stem>_concat_list.txt` beside the folder, verified before and after, segments kept). Regression: `test_two_splits_of_the_same_ranges_retain_all_six_and_each_joins_cleanly`, `test_every_slice_survives_and_the_join_is_added_beside_them` (real ffmpeg through the real queue runner), `test_an_ordinary_run_clears_everything_except_finished_slices`. Old-layout files already on disk are left alone and no longer parsed. Finished splits are kept (not cleaned up), as before; `.segments` grows until the user deletes it.
  *Suite.* `pytest app/tests tests` (58 min, 4070, the app server idle): 6840 passed, 29 failed, 15 errors, 28 skipped. One failure was mine and is fixed (`test_exception_visibility` flagged a silent broad `except` in `preview_provider._execute`; it now reports through `roop.degrade.swallowed`); another was a test stand-in (`read(filename, frame)`) that did not take the new `deadline_s`. Of the rest, 30 ids (`test_inference_engine` 15 errors + 7 failed, `test_execution_providers` 4, `test_new_swap_models`, `test_occlusion_wiring`, `test_phase14_profile`, `test_small_card_prepass`) fail identically at clean HEAD when those files are run alone. 13 ids (`test_cuda_affine_roi` x12, `test_gpu_filmstrip_and_scene_cuts::test_sparse_pre_scan_hardware_execution`) fail only inside the full suite (numeric tolerance 0.03-0.08 - the TF32-at-import order dependence) and PASS alone with these changes; a full-suite run at clean HEAD to prove the same was NOT done (it is another hour). Touched-area files run together after the last edit: 192 passed, 1 failed (the pre-existing video_io_hw -> lockout order dependence below; 2 files that the numbers above leave out, `test_exception_visibility` and `test_preview_provider`, are among the 192).
  *Pre-existing, not touched.* `test_video_io_hw.py` followed by `test_preview_render_lockout.py::test_cache_only_endpoints_keep_answering_during_a_render` fails at clean HEAD (`/api/progress` -> `TypeError: unhashable type: 'list'` in `jsonable_encoder`, some global left by the video_io_hw tests); each passes alone.

## 2026-10-22

- **"Fused SRAM post-processing kernel + L2 cache persistence" brief: the kernel is BUILT and 7-20x faster than the unfused GPU chain it replaces; the L2 carve-out works as an API but measures ~0% on the working set it was meant for; NOT wired into the render.
  *Premises.* `triton` and `cuda-python` were not installed (installed `triton-windows` 3.3.1 and `cuda-python` 12.9, declared in `app/requirements.txt` with platform markers); `torch.compile(max-autotune)` cannot run here (no `cl` for Inductor); torch has only `L2_cache_size` (persisting maximum = `cudaDeviceGetAttribute`, 24.75 MiB of 36 MiB); `cudaCtxSetLimit` does not exist (`cudaDeviceSetLimit`); the stream-attribute enum is spelled `cudaLaunchAttributeAccessPolicyWindow` in cuda-python 12.9; the driver granted 18 MiB for 16 MiB asked. Production's swap / restore outputs are host numpy, so there are no device "TensorRT output tensors" to fuse over.
  *Built.* `app/core/fused_postprocess.py`: one Triton launch does Reinhard colour transfer in CIE L*a*b* (per-Face-ID stats supplied, `LabStatsEMA` outside), Laplacian-split blend, mask feather, luminance grain and the composite into a (strided) destination; Gaussians are banded-matrix `tl.dot`s over a reflect-indexed haloed tile, so colour transfer precedes the blur with nothing written between stages. 32x32 tile (64x64 needs 161 KB of shared memory, Ada allows 99 KB), halo <= 8 px. `app/core/l2_persistence.py`: `PersistentArena` (one allocation so one window covers the embedding and ROI buffers), `pin_arena`, `mark_streaming`, `clear`. 30 tests; 1 level from the unfused reference; LAB within OpenCV's own LUT approximation (0.36 units).
  *Measured (4070, whole batch).* 4 faces x 512: fused 1.49 ms, unfused torch 10.5 ms (287 kernels), fused including host upload + download 2.00 ms, CPU production chain 186 ms; 1 x 256: 0.13 / 2.81 / 0.21 / 6.6 ms. L2: a persisting window changed a read after a 1 GiB flood by 0.0% (4 MiB), -3.8% (8 MiB), -12.5% (16 MiB, ~6 us): nil for the 6.3 MB `[4,3,512,512]` fp16 ROI buffer.
  *Not done / unverified.* Wiring (host-array inputs, ROI resample, GPU-bound-pipeline A/B); direct proof of L2 residency (the no-flood control was confounded by GPU clocks; the first probe, with the flood on a second stream, could not resolve anything); WDDM vs TCC; the RTX 3060; parity with production's 8-bit LAB paths. Details `docs/perf/fused_postprocess/`.

- **"Four GPU visual-quality guards" brief (NVOFA box tracking + per-frame landmarks; sub-box bicubic/Lanczos warp; temporal-EMA colour transfer + frequency split; sensor-noise match + quality-first NVENC): most of it already existed or measured negative; three things built, one of them opt-in; the end-to-end speed effect of the one real saving is UNMEASURED.
  *Premises, with sites.* (1) `GpuFaceAligner` does not exist; `optimized_processor.CudaAffineBatch` is the GPU aligner (dormant, full-frame paste). (2) Bicubic ROI inverse warp + matte composite already existed (`procmgr_masking.paste_upscale`, `ROOP_BLEND_ROI_WARP`); the cubic crop round trip is lossless (mean 0.02-0.41 level, detail kept 99-101%) and Lanczos4 is indistinguishable from it. (3) The frequency split is `enhance_blend.frequency_blend` (swap low band + 0.75 x restorer high band), called from `ProcessMgr:3650`, `Enhance_UltraMax:1277`, `Enhance_GPEN256Pro:720`; on 72 real faces the restorer at full strength is 1.58x the plate's texture (over-textured, not plastic), the 0.75 split 1.09x. (4) Grain matching existed: `procmgr_merger.apply_grain_match`, `merger_grain_match` live at 0.2. (5) `NvZeroCopyWriter` is not on the render path (`NVHardwareVideoWriter` / `FFMPEG_VideoWriter` are). (6) NVOFA cannot run here: the OpenCV wheel has no CUDA (`flow_backend.nvof_unavailable_reason()`); `2d106det` / `1k3d68` are FP32 by override because mixed precision drifts them 1.2-8.8 px (`PRECISION_POLICY.md`), so the brief's mixed-precision landmark extractor contradicts a measured policy and was not used.
  *Built.* (a) `roop/nvenc_quality.py`: multipass / spatial AQ / temporal AQ for both production writers and `NvZeroCopyWriter`, OFF unless `ROOP_NVENC_QUALITY_FIRST` or the three per-option variables are set. **`PyNvVideoCodec.CreateEncoder` silently ignores unknown kwargs**: only `multipass="fullres"`, `aq=1`, `temporalaq=1` change the bitstream; the brief's `enableAQ` / `enableTemporalAQ` (and `spatial_aq`, `FULL_RESOLUTION`, ...) produce a byte-identical stream. Pinned by `test_nvenc_quality.py` on a real NVENC. (b) `paste_upscale` matte on a face-neighbourhood canvas (`_matte_roi`, `ROOP_BLEND_ROI_MATTE=0` to disable): 15.4 / 25.6 / 74.6 ms per face at 720p / 1080p / 4K became 10.4 / 10.5 / 10.5 ms; 135 real faces differ from the old path by at most 1 level, median 0 pixels. (c) `roop/grain_match.py`: a post-warp, variance-aware closer for `merger_grain_match` (adds sqrt(max(s_plate^2 - s_paste^2, 0))), self-gating, counted in the swap audit; `ROOP_GRAIN_MATCH=0` disables it. (d) `CudaAffineBatch.paste_faces_roi` (not wired) and the `ROOP_PASTE_INTERP=lanczos4` opt-in.
  *Measured negatives.* **Temporal EMA colour transfer** (`temporal_color.py`, `probe_color_ema.py`, 402 frames, 4 clips): pumping lct 0.141 -> 0.154 / 0.157 with the EMA, rct 0.138 -> 0.165; accuracy unchanged; NOT wired. **NVENC bundle** (Love + Weeds, CQ 19-31, bytes at equal face quality): spatial AQ +12..16% PSNR / +4.5..15% LPIPS, whole bundle +10..12% and ~40% slower; nothing else beats p5 by more than the +-3% scatter. Visible blocking in flat skin is unmeasured. **Grain:** the face region of 16 clips sits at 0.25-0.74 levels (8-bit floor 0.29); on Weeds the swapped face is 2.22 against the plate's 0.49 (4.5x NOISIER) and on d1 `0/127 faces got grain`; the stage is a no-op on this footage and unverified on grainy footage.
  *Measured positive, then BUILT (opt-in).* Re-running `landmark_2d_106` on the flow box halves the error of raw flow-placed landmarks (`probe_flow_vs_neural_landmarks.py`, Weeds K=4: median 1.21 -> 0.58, p95 5.58 -> 2.87, better on 92% of frames), because `FlowCoaster` moves the KEY FRAME's landmarks rigidly. Love's tail is a harness artifact (cuts). `roop/landmark_refresh.py`, hooked in `procmgr_tracking._flow_miss` and `_flow_bridge_geometry` (`ROOP_PREPASS_LANDMARK_REFRESH=1`, default OFF, 13 tests, mutation-checked). Through the REAL pre-pass (`probe_landmark_refresh_prepass.py`, Weeds, 300 frames, K=4, 217 flow-placed faces vs the K=1 run): median 1.24 -> 0.93, p90 4.11 -> 2.68, p95 6.22 -> 4.07 (the bridge already corrects toward the closing detection, so the gain is smaller than the isolated probe's); p99/max unchanged; the pre-pass printed `244/271 re-measured, 27 refused, 0 errors`. No swap-level A/B (renders killed for memory), and K>1 still loses swaps for binding reasons landmarks do not touch.
  *Defects found.* My first end-to-end A/B compared ENCODED files: with a VBR encoder a few +-1 pixels early in a clip changed 87% of later pixels by up to 12 levels, so encoded diffs cannot test a paste change (`probe_matte_roi_real_faces.py` compares the paste). Two A/B schedules failed: the 8-render 4K one was killed for low system memory; the 4-render 1080p one ran at 0.2-1.0 fps (void, null control 107% apart).
  *Not done / unverified.* The effect of the canvas matte on a render's fps; NVOFA on hardware; the RTX 3060; grain on grainy footage; the NVENC options at `video_quality: 14` and on H.264 / AV1; the regression benchmark (see RECODE_STATUS). Light suite: the failure set is identical to clean HEAD (35: 30 `test_stage_cache`, `reswapper`, the unmanaged-session test from `dc6bc7d`'s surgeon, 3 update-manifest tests).

- **Native OpenVINO aux offload + per-stage HUD telemetry + "Benchmark Pipeline Providers": BUILT and tested; OpenVINO is OFF by default and has run only on OpenVINO's CPU device (no Intel NPU / iGPU / Arc exists on either machine); INT8 passes for SCRFD only.**
  *Code.* `roop/openvino_runner.py` (`OpenVinoAsyncSession`: per-shape compiled model + `AsyncInferQueue`, `hint.performance_mode=THROUGHPUT`; `resolve_device`; `adopt()` seam; calibration recorder; fail-closed `quantize_int8`), seams at `face_util._offload_aux_models` (det_10g + w600k_r50), `retinaface._build_one`, `Mask_XSeg.Initialize/_run_session` (all no-ops unless `ROOP_OPENVINO_NATIVE`; flags in `docs/ENV_FLAGS.md`). Telemetry: `stage_telemetry` now carries S1 `keyframes_per_sec` / `scene_cuts`, S2 `neural_detected_frames` / `flow_tracked_frames` / `flow_ratio_pct` / `fps`, S3 `ops` (decode / warp / model / restore / blend / encode ms per call + backend label, fed by `procmgr_runtime._prof`), live per-stage `fps`, and `aux_offload`; React `PipelineReport.jsx` / `reportFormat.js` print them. `bench.py` profile `providers` + `measure_provider_matrix`; `ProviderBenchmark.jsx` mounts under the HUD on `#/processing` and drives the existing `/api/settings/benchmark_*` endpoints (report-only: the worker passes `apply_to_config=False`).
  *Premises that were wrong.* `ov.properties.streams.Num.THROUGHPUT` does not exist in OpenVINO 2026.4.1 (`hint.performance_mode` is used and `NUM_STREAMS` read back); CV-CUDA has no wheel here; this OpenCV has no CUDA, so NVOFA is unavailable. Warp / blend are labelled `cv2 (CPU)`: `CudaAffineBatch` has no production caller.
  *Defects found in my own earlier work (fixed, tests added).* (1) `_backends()` mislabelled the TensorRT model column: `execution_providers` holds `(name, options)` pairs and `str()` of one never reads as TensorRT. (2) The provider matrix silently dropped a stage whose per-stage measurement failed (XSeg, "invalid throughput: 0.0" after a cold-cache 3 min engine build) and then summed the rest as the "frame cost"; failed stages are now listed under `unmeasured`, a serial arm needs EVERY stage, and a failed stage is retried once. (3) The OpenVINO calls/s arm read `throughput()`'s tuple as a number (caught by the new matrix test; would have errored on the first real Intel box). (4) Six silent broad handlers in the new runner now go through `degrade.swallowed` (one propagating handler registered in `ACCEPTED_SILENT`).
  *Measured (4070, idle).* `providers` profile, warm engine cache: 70 s; cold: 631 s (engine builds). TensorRT serial per frame 54.7 ms (detect 3.0, recognize 1.2, landmark 0.7, swap 7.4, UltraMax 27.5, XSeg 2.7, BiSeNet 12.3); CPU per call: detect 87.5, recognize 41.4, landmark 3.1, swap 141.7, XSeg 56.5, BiSeNet 30.0. The CPU ARM is unavailable: FP16 `codeformer.fp16.onnx` will not initialise on ORT CPU (reported with the error, no number). Host warp+blend 10.6 ms / face, cv2 LK 2.7 ms vs torch LK 24.7 ms for 106 points. Numbers: `docs/perf/providers/`. Serial per-frame figures are an arm comparison, NOT a pipeline fps.
  *OpenVINO correctness.* 19 tests pass in a scratch venv with `openvino` 2026.4.1 (real-model parity vs ORT CPU, 16-thread isolation, error recovery, INT8 fail-closed, recorder); end to end on Love.mp4 (24 frames, `ROOP_OPENVINO_NATIVE=CPU`) ArcFace ran through the runner, 35 faces, bbox diff 0.000 px, embedding cosine min 0.99989. `openvino` / `nncf` are deliberately NOT in the app venv (`hardware_probe` would start listing OpenVINO devices including the NVIDIA card). SCRFD / XSeg seams were not exercised end to end.
  *INT8 on real recorded inputs (NNCF PTQ, gated on a held-out slice).* SCRFD PASSES (anchor F1 0.969). ArcFace and XSeg FAIL even with accuracy control: plain PTQ min-cosine 0.76 / mean IoU 0.57; `quantize_with_accuracy_control` bounded at 20 reverted layers (an unbounded run was stopped by hand after ~10 min and 7 reverts with the drop still 0.06-0.08 vs the 0.02 target; not run to completion) reached 0.927 (gate: min >= 0.95 and mean >= 0.98) and 0.651 (gate: mean >= 0.97). Both stay FP16/FP32; sidecars in `docs/perf/openvino_int8/`. More reverts might pass; that was not tried.
  *Not done / unverified.* Any run on an Intel NPU / iGPU / Arc or on the RTX 3060; the `trt_openvino_aux` and `trt_cvcuda_nvofa` arms (unavailable here, with reasons); the HUD in a real browser against a live render (checked by render-check on fixtures made by the real writers, `tools/gen_hud_fixture.py`, plus the production build); no regression benchmark (no default-path pipeline code changed; the `_prof` hook costs 0.76 us/op). Pre-existing: `npm run lint` reports `no-undef onUpdateSetting` in `BatchSwap.jsx`.

- **"VRAM-resident Stage 3 (CV-CUDA + CUDA-graph TRT + 3-stream ring)" brief: priced, NOT built. The blend step is 9-14x cheaper on the GPU in isolation (13.1 -> 1.6 ms per face) but that step is ~2 s of thread time in a 42 s render, the GPU arm is not pixel-equivalent, CV-CUDA cannot be installed on this platform, and the architecture the brief describes already exists, dormant, and measured 3.4x SLOWER end to end. Nothing in the render changed; one probe added.**
  *Premises, with sites.* (1) **CV-CUDA is not installable here:** `pip download cvcuda-cu12` in the app venv (Windows, Python 3.10.20, torch 2.7.0+cu128) -> `No matching distribution found`; `import cvcuda` / `nvcv` fail. The `cvcuda.warp_affine` arm of the brief could not be written or tested on either machine; the Kornia/torch arm is the only one that would run. (2) **Requirement 4 (decode / infer / encode on three CUDA streams with `torch.cuda.Event` hand-offs, VRAM ring) exists as `face_engine/core/cuda_streams.py` (`CUDAStreamPipeline`)**, and the batched GPU warp / paste as `roop/optimized_processor.py:1732` (`CudaAffineBatch.warp_frames` `:1978`, `.paste_faces` `:2006`) driven by `roop/vectorized_pipeline.py:837,857`. `vectorized_pipeline` has no production caller (`grep`: only `tests/test_env_flags.py`; `docs/perf/sustained_throughput_2026-10-11/architecture_map.md:43` agrees), and `GpuFaceSwapProcessor` / `MemoryStreamingProcessor` are instantiated nowhere (entry of 2026-10-19 above). **These sites are NAMED, their tests were not driven for this brief: UNVERIFIED, not "shipped".** (3) Requirement 2 (bound TRT + CUDA graph) exists as `roop/trt_bound_session.py`, reachable only through `ROOP_TRT_BOUND` (`processors/Mask_XSeg.py:50-70`); measured earlier as correct and unmeasurable end to end (bound XSeg ABBA d4 +1.0% / s7 -1.7%, noise). (4) The one-CUDA-owner pipeline was measured 2026-09-24: 3.88 / 3.88 fps vs 13.38 / 13.03 for the 20-worker path (2026-10-11 entry below, "Not changed, contradicted by the record": ABBA, d4, 600 frames).
  *Measured (`app/tests/probe_stage3_blend_gpu_vs_cpu.py`, 4070, 1280x720 `Love.mp4` frame 120, 30 reps, cv2 single-threaded; JSON in `docs/perf/stage3_gpu_blend/`).* CPU arm is the REAL `MaskingMixin.paste_upscale` driven face by face; GPU arm is `CudaAffineBatch.paste_faces`.

  | faces | CPU `paste_upscale` | GPU incl. upload + download of the BGR frame | GPU, frame stays in VRAM |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | 13.12 ms | 1.58 ms | 0.90 ms |
  | 2 | 25.94 ms | 2.27 ms | 1.67 ms |
  | 4 | 51.29 ms | 3.75 ms | 3.17 ms |

  *What the table does and does not say.* The GPU arm does LESS than production (no landmark hull, no `blur_area` adaptive feather, no temporal matte EMA, no overlap trim, no `refine_alpha`/multiband, no rim illumination), so it is a ceiling on the step, not a port, and no pixel comparison was made. Idle GPU; under a real render the GPU is shared with swap + restorer + XSeg. Scaling the CPU number by Phase 11's face count (156 faces in a 41.7 s, 600-frame render) gives ~2.0 s of thread time spread over 20 workers: far below the ~5% spread the 4070 resolves, and the pipeline is GPU-bound (`AGENTS.md`), so moving CPU work onto it can only add GPU work. Everything after the paste (`apply_mouth_area`, `apply_eyes_area`, expression restore, lip-sync, occluder) also works on the host frame, so "100% VRAM-resident" means porting those too, or paying the round trip they force.
  *Not done / what would be.* No end-to-end A/B (nothing wired); RTX 3060 unmeasured (TensorRT is not admitted there, so the CUDA-graph arm is inert). The one defensible follow-up is narrow: an ROI-bounded GPU paste behind `paste_upscale`, with a pixel-parity test against the CPU arm first, then the counterbalanced 600-frame A/B. Requires an owner decision because the expected end-to-end effect is inside the noise.

## 2026-10-21

- **"Phase 14: interval detection with optical flow, 1-euro landmark filter, pre-computed affine cache" brief: all three built and MEASURED on real clips; the detection interval and the filter are opt-in because neither passes the swap-rate guard end to end. Four defects found on the way, each with a failing test. NVOF is unverified (no CUDA OpenCV here); the torch GPU Lucas-Kanade is ~90x slower than the CPU call on the chain's workload. Evidence: `docs/perf/phase14/README.md`.**
  *Premises, with sites.* (1) **Interval detection already existed in two other forms**: `ROOP_TEMPORAL_STEP` skips frames and linearly interpolates (measured 6x worse on turned heads), and `TemporalFaceTracker` already does a full detection every 8 frames with an ROI detection on every frame between (`roi_interval` is the opt-in coast cadence). What did not exist is "detect every K, carry by flow, bridge between detections" as one setting. (2) `optimized_prepass.py` is not the production pre-pass (`procmgr_tracking._precompute_tracks` is); the brief's Hungarian IoU + centroid matching is `linear_sum_assignment` over `association_cost.composite_cost` there, already. (3) The One-Euro filter in `one_euro.py` is causal and per-scalar, and the default smoother is `AdaptiveLandmarkSmoother` (Phase 7 added a zero-phase EMA, OFF); a vectorized zero-phase 1-euro did not exist. (4) **There was no 3-D head-pose HUD in the React UI** (`routes_angle_scan.py` has no UI caller); it is new. (5) `cv2.cuda_NvidiaOpticalFlow_2_0` is wrapped for the high-frequency stabilizer (`face_swapper._flow_nvof`) but this venv's OpenCV has no CUDA (`getCudaEnabledDeviceCount() == 0`).
  *Built.* `roop/prepass_policy.py` (K clamp 1-8, `max_misses` grows with K, filter kind); setting `prepass_detect_interval` (slider 1-8, `ROOP_PREPASS_DETECT_INTERVAL`, live) and `prepass_landmark_filter` (`adaptive` / `zero_phase` / `one_euro`, `ROOP_PREPASS_FILTER`, live), generated schema/catalog updated, defaults 1 and `adaptive`. `TemporalFaceTracker.from_env` reads K; the pre-pass takes Stage 1's bitstream scene cuts (`_active_ranges_stats['scene_cuts']`, rebased to the scan) as a HINT that forces a full detection (not recorded as a shot boundary), refines the association prior with the flow-measured box, anchors the flow chain on one observation at K>1 and carries a face up to K-1 frames past a track's last detection by flow. `roop/one_euro_vec.py`: zero-phase 1-euro as a parallel scan (ceil(log2 T) vectorized passes on numpy or CUDA torch; defaults 0.6 Hz / 25 / 3 Hz chosen on the fixture AND three real clips). `roop/flow_backend.py`: `CvLK` (the call `FlowCoaster` always made, default), `TorchLK`, guarded `NvofPointTracker`; `FlowCoaster(tracker=...)`. `roop/trajectory_cache.py`: one memory-mappable structured `.npy` per job (frame, track, flags, smoothed kps, bbox, yaw/pitch/roll, `M`, `M_inv`), closed-form matrices, active-job registry; `<id>.trajectory.npy` sidecar in the project checkpoint (derived from Stage 2: re-recording stage 1 or 2 drops it, completion deletes it, restore maps it only if it matches the restored faces bit for bit). `routes_trajectory.py` (`/api/trajectory/{summary,pose,series}`); React `HeadPoseHud` in the Processing tab's live panels and Face Swap's diagnostics section; Stage 2 of the per-job report now carries `detect_interval`, `detector_calls`, `scan_frames`, `coast_frames`, `flow_backend`, `landmark_filter`, `trajectory_faces` (None when Stage 2 was restored).
  *Measured (4070; `docs/perf/phase14/`).* **K=1 is bit-identical to `HEAD`** (all 12 captured arrays, same 16.9 s). Three 900-frame clips, K=2 / 4 / 8: pre-pass -11..-14% / -21..-26% / ~-30%; faces found 0..-5% / -3..-10% / -9..-20%; frames matched to a source -0..-5% / -1..-25% / -0..-31% (a track's source binding needs a mean-embedding gate or 3 matching frames, and a hard-pose target loses it when seen every Kth frame); flow-placed faces median 0.8-1.4% of an eye distance against 1.2-12.7% for linear interpolation, p95 9-18%; faces placed WITHOUT flow median 13-40% off. End to end (Weeds, 600 frames): K=4 64.8 s vs 70.5 s but **424 vs 469 swapped faces (-9.6%)**, pixels 0.55/255 from the baseline against a null of 0.05-0.07; `one_euro` 69.5 s, 469 swapped, pixels 0.07-0.08 (the null). Real tracks, `one_euro` vs shipped `adaptive`: still jitter equal or lower on all three, 106-landmark jitter lower, lag +0.02..-0.11% of an eye distance against -1.1..-4.8%, deviation in fast motion 1.3-2.0% against 1.6-11.3%. Back ends (ms/call at 70 points, <=192 px): cv 0.23, torch-CPU 26.5, torch-CUDA 20.3; no crossover below 3000 points at 768 px. 1-euro scan: CPU 0.1-12 ms per typical track, CUDA 14x faster at 60,000 frames.
  *Defects found (kept, not hidden).* (1) **The interval was ignored under the pool's lag**: a result is consumed up to pool_workers + 2 frames after its frame was planned, and consuming frame t-4 moved "the last detection" back to t-4, so K=4 ran 900 detector calls over 900 frames with 0 coast frames; fixed for K>1 only (`_note_requested` / `_note_full`), K=1 byte-identical. (2) The last K-1 frames of every track (and of the clip) had no face: gap-fill bridges only between two detections. (3) A track of K>=5 was declared lost between key frames (`max_misses` 3). (4) The flow chain waited for 3 observations (2K frames at K=4). And one wrong claim of my own, caught by a test: the closed-form matrices agree with `face_util.estimate_norm` to 5e-5 px, not 1e-9 (`estimate_norm` moves by 2.1e-5 between a float32 and a float64 call of the same keypoints).
  *Why the defaults stay.* Neither K>1 nor `one_euro` survives the project's own guard (swap rate, then pixels against the null). K>1 costs identity binding; `one_euro` changes nothing measurable end to end (Phase 7's zero-phase result again). The slider's help text quotes the measured costs.
  *Tests.* `test_prepass_interval.py` (26: policy, schedule under lag with an old-scheduler subclass that fails the test, settings wiring), `test_prepass_interval_pipeline.py` (23: the real `_precompute_temporal` on real frames, direct and pooled; hints, tail, first gap), `test_one_euro_vec.py` (23: scan vs loop, fixture claims, exact invariances, bounded runs, GPU parity, pre-pass wiring), `test_flow_backend.py`, `test_trajectory_cache.py` (matrices vs skimage / `estimate_norm` / `cv2`, file, window match, checkpoint through the real project record, route), `test_prepass_report.py`; React `render-head-pose-hud.mjs`, `e2e/head-pose-hud.spec.js`, report fixture. 25 mutants of the new code were applied (18 Python, 7 React) and 24 were killed; the one survivor is equivalent (`face_from_flow` also refuses an inconsistent step, so the tail's own check is defence in depth). Two survivors on the first pass were weak tests, now tightened.
  *Not done / unverified.* RTX 3060; NVOF on an OFA; the regression benchmark PASSES for the default configuration (swap 300/300, face SSIM 0.9906, min 0.9868) and was not run with K>1 or `one_euro`; the full suite is green except `test_gpu_filmstrip_and_scene_cuts::test_sparse_pre_scan_hardware_execution`, which already fails in a full run on `HEAD` (`test_7_boost_fixes` leaves `g_desired_face_analysis = 'all'`; it passes alone); no resumed render through `/api/swap`; **Stage 3 does not read the cache's matrices** (its alignment depends on the swapper's crop size, template and SAR, and Phase 11 measured the affine math at under 0.5% of a render); no fix for the identity loss at K>1 (it means changing the assignment gate). The open follow-up: re-detect, in a second mini-pass, only the bridged frames whose flow chain was refused.
  *Files:* `app/roop/{prepass_policy,one_euro_vec,flow_backend,trajectory_cache}.py`, `app/routes_trajectory.py`, `app/roop/{temporal_tracker,flow_coast,procmgr_tracking,procmgr_batch,stage_telemetry}.py`, `app/{settings,project_checkpoint,stage_checkpoint,api}.py`; `react-ui/src/components/faceswap/{HeadPoseHud.jsx,headPoseModel.js,reportFormat.js}`, `Processing.jsx`, `FaceSwap.jsx`, `Settings.jsx`; tests above plus `app/tests/{probe_phase14_prepass,ab_phase14_e2e}.py`; `docs/perf/phase14/`.

- **"Zero-copy hardware video I/O" brief: `roop/video_io_hw.py` built (NVDEC-DLPack reader, true-IDR keyframe reader, NVENC-Direct writer, Intel QSV rung, fallback ladders) with decode/encode backend, PCIe-saved and decode-queue telemetry in the runtime report, the job report and the HUD. NOT wired into the render: every consumer takes host BGR arrays, so this is not a speedup claim. QSV decode is UNVERIFIED.**
  *Premises, with sites.* (1) **`app/core/video_io_hw.py` is the wrong place**: `roop/core.py` is a module and a package named `core` already shadowed it once (5609800); the module is `app/roop/video_io_hw.py`. (2) **PyNvVideoCodec was not installed** (`nvdec_reader.py` / `hardware_streamer.py` mention it only in a docstring); 2.2.3 installed into the venv, `app/requirements.txt` carries it with win32/linux x86_64 markers, and every rung degrades without it. (3) The "14-section" report is `runtime_state.SECTION_NAMES`; the GOP-aligned worker is `distributed_render.py` (untouched). (4) `ProcessMgr`, ORT and cv2 consume host `[H, W, 3]` BGR arrays: nothing in the render reads a CUDA tensor, so only the telemetry labels were wired (`nvdec_reader.wrap_capture`, `procmgr_batch`, `segment_writer`, which now say what they really chose: `NVDEC-Pipe`, `NVENC-Pipe`, `CPU-Fallback`, `HDR-Pipe`).
  *Measured (4070, PyNvVideoCodec 2.2.3, idle machine; d1.mp4 1080p 418 frames / Love.mp4 720p 3456):* cv2 346 / 796 fps; existing ffmpeg NVDEC pipe to host BGR 150 / 490 fps; NVDEC-DLPack to a CUDA tensor 899 / 1889 fps (the same with a `bgr()` host copy per frame). Encode, convert + NVENC + mux, 1080p: H.264 401, HEVC 523, AV1 567 fps. Per `AGENTS.md` decode is ~0.7% of thread time and the pipeline is GPU-bound, so these do not predict a render speedup and none is claimed; an A/B at 600 frames is the precondition for wiring a consumer.
  *Defects found while building (each has a test).* (1) **Decoded surfaces recycle**: the ring holds 2 surfaces whatever `buffer_size` is; 39 of 40 held DLPack tensors were overwritten. `HwFrame` raises `StaleFrameError` once the reader advances; `.detach()` clones on the device. A raw tensor held past `close()` is an illegal memory access that poisons the CUDA context (my own test did it and every later test failed). (2) **The container key flag is not IDR**: Love.mp4 flags 36 sync samples, 29 are IDR; decoding all 36 returned 6 wrong pictures (up to 232/255). Packets are classified by NAL (H.264 5, HEVC 19/20); IDR-only + an empty-packet flush matched a full decode bit for bit on 3 clips (29, 6, 220 keyframes), and decodes 29 of 3456 frames. (3) **NVENC raced torch**: the NV12 conversion runs on torch's stream, NVENC reads on its own; unordered, ~30% of 30-frame clips encoded garbage (4 dB, identical on every decode of the file). `CreateEncoder(cudastream=...)` did not fix it (8/60 bad); a `current_stream().synchronize()` before `Encode` did (0/150). Found only because a test was flaky 1 run in 3. (4) **Muxer**: moov is written when the object is released (`Finalize(); del mux; gc.collect()`), and only the timebase denominator is honoured (1001/24000 gave a 24000/1 fps file; 1/(fps_num*k) is exact and Stage 4's A/V check then passes). (5) **Matrix**: BT.601 below 720p, BT.709 from 720p: 640x360 re-decoded 45 dB vs 27 dB the other way. (6) **`-skip_frame nokey` is not the sync-sample list**: on an open-GOP clip ffmpeg emits 3 pictures where ffprobe flags 5, in decoder order (60, 30, 0); indices come from `ffprobe -skip_frame nokey`, not packet flags, so the software rung can be DENSER than the IDR-only NVDEC rung.
  *Telemetry.* `/ws/telemetry` frames carry `io_decode`, `io_encode`, `io_pcie_saved_mb`, `io_queue_sat` (coarse, change-only); `runtime_state` HARDWARE has `video_io`; the job report has `video_io`; the Face Swap HUD prints "Video I/O: NVDEC-DLPack -> NVENC-Direct, PCIe saved ~N MB (est.), decode queue P%". PCIe saved is a labelled ESTIMATE against a host-staged pipeline (frames handed out or encoded as device tensors); `pcie_moved_bytes` is what `bgr()`/uploads actually copied. Queue saturation = share of reads served without waiting on the decoder.
  *Tests.* `app/tests/test_video_io_hw.py` (37; 20 need no GPU; hardware class marked `gpu`): `cuda:0` uint8 `[3, H, W]`, colours vs cv2 (and a channel-swap control), stale/detach, seek, sparse bit-exact, round trip H.264/HEVC/AV1, SD matrix, NTSC rate, audio finalize + verify, VRAM-only decode-to-encode moves 0 PCIe bytes, every fallback rung, the Love.mp4 reproduction (skips without the file). `test_standalone_install.py` passes. Full suite 6406 passed, 16 skipped, 7 xfailed (the only failure, `test_exception_visibility`, was two silent handlers of mine, fixed).
  *Not done / unverified.* **QSV decode** (bundled ffmpeg has no `*_qsv` decoder, no Intel iGPU in use): command, probe and fallback are tested, a QSV decode is not. `BackgroundKeyframeScan` (QSV/NVDEC prescan while TensorRT engines build) exists and is tested but is NOT called by `ProcessMgr`. `start_frame` seek is exact on CFR (tested) and untested on VFR (see the Stage 2 VFR note). No RTX 3060 run. The writer needs even dimensions >= 64 (odd falls back to the ffmpeg writer). Output is untagged (no VUI colour tags); players apply the resolution rule. No render was driven through it.

- **"Phase 12: React UI and settings streamlining" brief: the Identity Blender shows only the one dial the render validation proves, the sparse pre-scan / disk-frame / Skip-audio / Video-method controls and their config keys are gone (the pipeline streams in memory with the 3-stage scan and stream-copied audio for every video), the global precision offers mixed and fp32 only, and the run bars carry a 3-step Pipeline Pill driven by `/ws/telemetry`. Several of the brief's premises were not true as written, and the verification found a contrast failure in the run bar that no existing test could see.**
  *Premises, with sites.* (1) **All eight cited commits resolve.** (2) **The three Identity Blender sliders were not "disabled"**: they rendered dimmed (`opacity-40 pointer-events-none`) when `/api/identity/blend` reported the direction unavailable, because `identity_render_validation.json` marks `age`, `gender`, `expression` `writable: false` and `identity_algebra.dial_steps` refuses them. Removed from the panel anyway; `jawline` (`step_per_dial` 0.75, guard cosine 0.80) is the one left. A recipe that comes back from the server (`BlendRecipe.from_payload` fills all four names) or an older save can still hold a value for a removed dial, with no slider to clear it, so `identityBlend.normalizeBlend` drops them at both entry points. (3) **There is no Stage 1 stride or detector-worker input anywhere in the UI** (`grep` of `react-ui/src`): only the one toggle, in `#/settings`, not `#/faceswap` or `#/batch`. (4) **"Temp Frame Format PNG/JPG" is not a setting**: the only hits are a benchmark RECOMMENDATION row (`benchmark/ui_dashboard.py`) that writes `output_image_format`, the real image-output format, which stays. (5) **The global precision does not conflict with the per-model pins**: `precision_policy.providers_for` for xseg, 2d106det, 1k3d68, resnet18 and RestoreFormer++ resolves FP32 under global mixed, fp32 AND fp16 (`test_per_model_pins_win_over_every_global_value`). What a global `fp16` DOES do is reach every row the policy calls `fp16=candidate / mixed=safe` (the GPEN family, detection, recognition, masks) at FP16, precision `PRECISION_POLICY.md` says still needs its quality gate - so that one choice was removed, not the select. (6) **No angle-HUD state or config key is left** in `react-ui/src`, `app/settings.py`, `config.yaml` or the schema (`c59eea7` removed the UI only; the remaining "angle" hits are unrelated features). What remains is backend: `routes_angle_scan.py` (`/ws/angle-scan`, `/api/angle-scan/*`) has no UI caller; not removed (outside the brief's state/config scope, and `source_portfolio` routing in the render path consumes what it builds). (7) **The legacy Gradio UI (`start_legacy.js`) reads `CFG.keep_frames`, `skip_audio`, `video_swapping_method` and `wait_after_extraction` while it builds its widgets**, so deleting the attributes would have stopped it launching: they are now CLASS constants on `Settings` (never loaded, saved or in the schema). `core.batch_process`' disk branch is therefore untouched; it is unreachable from the React app and `POST /api/swap`, and still serves Gradio.
  *Built.* **Settings:** `sparse_auto_range`, `keep_frames`, `skip_audio`, `wait_after_extraction`, `video_swapping_method` removed from `Settings._load`/`save`, `UI_SETTINGS`, `settings.schema.json`, `settingsCatalog.js` (regenerated), `default_config.yaml` and the local `config.yaml`; an old config that still holds them is read and the keys are dropped on the next save. `roop/render_policy.py` holds the fixed choices (in-memory streaming, 3-stage scan on, audio kept) and `api._run_swap` takes them from it. `skip_audio` and `sparse_auto_range` stay readable from the request PAYLOAD only: `distributed_render` renders audio-less chunks through it, and the harnesses build the dense 1-stage baseline with it. Audio was already a stream copy (`restore_audio`, `-c:a copy`), verified by Stage 4. All 54 saved projects hold `video_method: In-Memory processing`, `keep_frames: false`, `skip_audio: false`. **Precision:** `TRT_PRECISION_CHOICES = ('mixed', 'fp32')`; the Settings select, `/api/meta` and the mock agree; a `trt_precision: fp16` in an older `config.yaml` reads as `mixed` (printed once). `precision_policy.resolve(requested='fp16')` is untouched, so harnesses that name FP16 per session still get it. **UI:** Skip audio / Keep frames / Wait before creating video / Video method removed from Face Swap, its defaults and request; `video_method` from the Batch Matrix payload; the sparse toggle from `#/settings`; "TensorRT FP16" removed from the Max Turbo copy. **Pipeline Pill** (`faceswap/PipelinePill.jsx`, model `pillModel.js`): 1 Pre-Scan, 2 Range Lock, 3 Render & Mux from `stages`; Stage 4 is not in `stages`, so step 3 stays active as "Muxing" until `summary` arrives and fails if the report failed; culled share is `skipped_frames_pct` (hidden when unmeasured, never 0); fps is `fps_now` beside step 3. Mounted under the `#/processing` run bar and in the `#/faceswap` run bar while a job runs. Socket-only (no REST call, no timer: the 15 s API deadline cannot apply), the two live numbers are `LiveText` leaves capped at `UI_HZ` = 10, state is a glyph AND a word, one `aria-current="step"`, one polite live region that changes only at a step change.
  *Proof it executes.* `test_phase12_settings_streamlining.py` (15): the real `api._run_swap`, run up to the render call with the renderer stubbed, hands it `use_new_method=True` and `keep_frames/wait_after_extraction=False` when the payload asks for the disk route, and carries the two internal channels through (a mutant that honours the payload's `keep_frames` fails it); an old config holding every retired key loads without them in `__dict__` and does not write them back. Render-checks `render-pipeline-pill.mjs` and `render-retired-controls.mjs` (the blender check first proved its own section was open - a collapsed section made the first version's "Age Shift is not on the panel" pass for the wrong reason; mutants for Muxing, `aria-current` and the label are caught). `e2e/pipeline-pill.spec.js` (real Chromium): the pill walks Range Lock -> Render -> Muxing -> done from scripted `/ws/telemetry` frames, no pill when idle, axe clean on both tabs with the pill showing, and a CONTROL ARM (same socket, stage tables emptied) shows the pill adds no REST request.
  *Defects found on the way (kept, not hidden).* (1) **The run bar's own controls failed axe color-contrast, but only while a run was in flight**: "Pause Render" white on amber-600 3.19:1 and "Resume Render" on emerald-600 3.7:1. `a11y.spec.js` audits idle views, so `3d9d5d6`'s "zero violations" never covered the running or paused state. Both moved one shade (amber-700, emerald-700); the running and paused states are now audited in the pill spec. (2) `pipelinePill.js` next to `PipelinePill.jsx` resolved to itself on this case-insensitive filesystem (the Phase 9 trap again; `lint:unreachable` and the build caught it): renamed `pillModel.js`. (3) My REST check first used a hand-kept allow-list and tripped on an existing `GET /api/models/integrity`; the control arm replaced it. (4) The scripted run-state needed `/api/progress` answered consistently with the socket, or the mock's "idle" flipped `processing` between frames. (5) Four contract tests encoded the old `['fp32','fp16','mixed']` list and `video_methods` (`test_provider_initialization`, `test_fresh_install_tensorrt`, `test_ui_preset_recipes`); updated to the new contract. One further full-suite failure, `test_windows_runtime_compat`, is `WinError 145` in a temp-directory cleanup (the class Phase 9 recorded); it passes alone and on a stashed tree.
  *Finding outside the brief (pre-existing; not fixed, not traced to a component).* The running-job frame budget in `frame-budget.spec.js` ("React commits 4.2-4.7/s") is measured against the MOCK server's old-shape telemetry (`status`, no `processing`, no `started_at`), so it never starts the run clocks. A frame in the REAL backend's shape (`api._telemetry_snapshot`: `processing: true`, a `started_at`) makes `#/processing` commit about **70 React commits/s**, and `#/faceswap` about 70/s while the stage table is still empty (the model-load phase before Stage 1), whatever else the frame carries (a bisect with every field static still gave 51/s). Measured on a build of HEAD with none of Phase 12's code (70.7/s), so it is not the pill. The nodes that mutate are run-clock text (`font-mono text-display`, 14-27 updates/s); frame time stays at 59.5+ fps and script time is about 3% of the main thread, so it is a waste, not a jank. It does mean `BUDGET.job.commitsPerSec` (10) describes the mock, not a real run, so it is not applied to the new variant. The pill's own cost is measured against a control arm in `frame-budget.spec.js` ("pill vs control", a real reload between the arms): +3 to +5 commits/s on `#/processing` (budget 6), none on `#/faceswap`, 0 long tasks, >20 ms frames <= 0.4%. A first version of that test measured 67/s "with the pill" and 66/s without it: the number was the scripted frame, not the component, which is why the control arm exists.
  *Not done.* `swap_quantization` (off / auto / fp8 / int8 / fp16) is still in `#/settings`: it is not a global FP16/FP32 toggle, but `PRECISION_POLICY.md`'s own measurements call INT8 unsafe (59% of faces lose identity) and FP8 unsupported on Ada, so it is the next dead option to remove if you want it gone. `routes_angle_scan.py` stays. The Gradio UI still shows its old controls (frozen). The regression benchmark was not re-run (it does not go through `_run_swap`, and the `roop.globals.sparse_auto_range` default stays False). No real render was driven through the HTTP API with the new default; the executing test stubs the renderer.
  *Files:* `app/roop/render_policy.py`, `app/settings.py`, `app/api.py`, `app/default_config.yaml`, `app/settings.schema.json`; `react-ui/src/components/{Settings,FaceSwap,Processing}.jsx`, `faceswap/{IdentityBlender,PipelinePill}.jsx`, `faceswap/{identityBlend,pillModel,defaults,batchMatrix}.js`, `faceswap/ProcessingDock.jsx`; tests above plus `react-ui/e2e/pipeline-pill.spec.js`.

- **"Phase 11: terminal interconnectivity and fast video generation" brief: MEASURED on a real render, nothing built in the pipeline - four of the five items cost under 1% of a render, the fifth (unloading the Stage 1/2 models) already exists and frees 1.96 GB; one new regression test pins it.**
  *Premises, with sites.* (1) Commits `3ec3906`, `b2e580d`, `c01d767`, `a7eb4cf`, `e87af52` all resolve in this repo. (2) **Neither `PersistentVideoContext`, `AsyncVideoEncoderPipe`, `VerifiedTargetFace`, `M_align` nor `M_inv` exist** and `stage2_frame_plan` is a Face-free frame index, not the Stage 2 -> 3 hand-off (`stage_cache.py` says so), so there is no record to attach a matrix to. (3) **Item 3 already exists**: `ProcessMgr._release_replayed_analysis` (`ProcessMgr.py:974`, called at `procmgr_batch.py:952`) -> `face_util.release_face_analyser_aux` (recognition = w600k_r50, `landmark_2d_106`, `landmark_3d_68`, `genderage`, `lm68_model`) + `shrink_analysis_pools(1)`. The detector stays on purpose (verification, autorotation, rescue). (4) **Item 4's moving parts exist**: `run_batch_inmem` writes through `SegmentedVideoWriter` -> `NVHardwareVideoWriter` / `FFMPEG_VideoWriter` (rawvideo bgr24 into an ffmpeg stdin pipe; h264/hevc/av1_nvenc, `_SW_FALLBACK` to libx264/libx265), fed by `write_frames_thread` from bounded per-worker `processed_queue`s (depth <= 4). The disk temp-frame extraction survives only for Keep Frames / per-frame masks (`core.py:1764`, the Frame Editor). Audio is muxed by Stage 4 (`finalize_output`), which deliberately re-reads the delivered file.
  *Measured (`tests/probe_phase11_interconnect.py`; Phase 5 clip, 600 frames, user's live config UltraMax / realswap / RealityUX / 20 threads, 4070; two renders after a warm-up: 41.7 s / 14.4 fps and 44.0 s / 13.7 fps, 156 of 156 target frames swapped in both; `docs/perf/phase11/probe_phase11_interconnect.json`).*
  | item | what it would save | measured cost today | share |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 1 shared probe / demuxer | container opens | 6 `cv2.VideoCapture` opens x ~25 ms = 150 ms (Stages 1, 2 and 3 use different decoders: cv2 workers, cv2 seeker, an NVDEC ffmpeg pipe - one handle cannot serve them); the 7 ffprobe spawns (0.23-0.34 s) are ALL Stage 4's, and are the independent verification | 0.4% of the render |
  | 2 precomputed `M_align` / `M_inv` | CPU affine math in Stage 3 | `estimate_norm` 312 calls = 0.15-0.16 s, `invertAffineTransform` 624 calls = 0.02-0.04 s, `align_crop` 150 = 0.22-0.27 s (thread time summed over 20 threads; it includes the `warpAffine` it wraps). `warpAffine` itself (688 calls, 0.7-0.9 s) is the crop extraction and stays whatever is cached | < 0.5% of the job, < 1.7% of Stage 3 even if serial |
  | 3 unload Stage 1/2 models | VRAM | already freed: 7384 -> 5422 MiB (**-1962**), pool models `[landmark_2d_106, landmark_3d_68, recognition]` x2 -> `[]`; what remains is one detector instance (~60 MiB per `[Provider]` log line) | built |
  | 4 async encoder pipe, audio in the same pass | write time, mux pass | `write_frame` 0.41-0.48 s over 600 frames on the dedicated write thread (not on the critical path); Stage 4 `finalize_render` 0.27-0.38 s total incl. the verification ffprobes, of which the two ffmpeg mux processes are ~0.1 s | < 1% |
  | 5 10 Hz progress throttle | terminal / telemetry I/O | terminal is already throttled to 2 Hz (`ChunkedProgress.DISPLAY_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 0.5`, redraw gated by tqdm `mininterval`), gradio/`_progress` callback 2 Hz, `/ws/telemetry` sampled by its OWN asyncio task at 4 Hz (`routes_telemetry.SAMPLE_HZ`) and sending only on change - the pipeline never touches a socket. `update_progress` in isolation is 5-86 us per call (`probe_phase11_progress_cost.py`); the 0.3-0.5 ms inside a render is GIL / lock wait | a 10 Hz gate would be FASTER output than today |
  *Why nothing was built.* Per `AGENTS.md` the pipeline is GPU-bound and only work removed from the GPU moves the render clock; every item here redistributes CPU/IO time that the 4070 resolves only above ~5% (null spread 4-5%).
