Item 2 also has a correctness problem the brief does not mention: the alignment depends on `(crop size, template, SAR)` per consumer (swapper 128/256/512, restorer 512/1024, masks) AND on keypoints Stage 3 still changes after Stage 2 (`canonicalize_face_alignment`, stabilizers), so one cached `M_align` per face is not the matrix any consumer asks for; a vectorised closed-form solve would also differ from `skimage` in the last float bits and change pixels. Item 4's single-pass audio would have to live inside `SegmentedVideoWriter._concat` and would take Stage 4's verification (Phase 9) out of the path it exists to check, for ~0.1 s per 600 frames (it scales with file size, but so does the render). AMF / QSV exist only as software-fallback names in `ffmpeg_writer._SW_FALLBACK`; VideoToolbox does not exist anywhere and neither rate control (`-crf` is wrong for AMF) nor a probe is written for them - **unverified, no AMD or macOS hardware here**, so no code was added.
  *Defect in my own probe (kept).* The first two runs segfaulted in `gc.collect()` because I replaced `cv2.VideoCapture` with a Python SUBCLASS to time constructions; faulthandler pointed at the collection, not at the pipeline. A plain factory function returning the real object fixes it. Do not subclass cv2 binding classes in instrumentation.
  *Test added.* `tests/test_phase11_analysis_release.py` (4) runs the REAL `release_face_analyser_aux` and `_release_replayed_analysis` on stand-in analysers: recognition, both landmark models, genderage and `lm68_model` go, the detector stays, the ORT session is set to `None`; a partial replay releases nothing. The older `test_7_boost_fixes` test patches the function it is about. Mutation-checked: a no-op release is caught by both release tests.
  *Not done / caveats.* One clip (960x540, short): fixed per-job costs are a larger share here than on a long render, so the percentages are an UPPER bound for items 1 and 4; the per-frame items (2, 5) do not shrink with length. No RTX 3060 run. The probe's "finalize" phase wall includes the harness's own post-render ffprobe, so only `finalize_render` (0.27-0.38 s) is quoted for Stage 4.

- **"Phase 9: Stage 4 lossless audio-video finalization and 14-section structured runtime telemetry" brief: Stage 4 now VERIFIES the delivered file before it deletes anything (a mux that died half way used to delete the only complete copy of the render and the job still read COMPLETED), the per-job 3-stage + finalization report exists and reaches `/ws/telemetry`, the history log, `/api/runtime/state` and the UI; the brief's audio-trim and colour-tag asks were already implemented and are now measured; the verification run found and fixed its own over-counting.**
  *Premises, with sites.* (1) **`b900deb` is not in this repository** (`git log` cannot resolve it; same as Phases 6 and 8). (2) **The trimmed audio remux already existed:** `util_ffmpeg.restore_audio` cuts a trimmed render's audio in its own audio-only pass with an OUTPUT-side `-ss` and `-t` (the brief's `-ss`/`-to`, equivalent), bounded by the video's own length, never `-shortest`, with real-ffmpeg tests (`test_restore_audio_trim_offset.py`, `_frame_count.py`, `_metadata.py`). Measured, not assumed: on real renders the delivered audio cross-correlates with the source's same seconds to **0.0 ms** (untrimmed) and **-13.33 ms** (frames 150-510, under one AAC packet = 21.3 ms and under one 33.3 ms frame); across the 21 earlier pipeline renders on this machine audio starts 0.0 ms from the video and ends <= 15.6 ms past it (the 12 files that would fail a one-frame gate are all SOURCE clips in `roop-keep/`, not renders). (3) **Colour tags:** the writer states bt709 on all four fields (`ffmpeg_writer.py:334`) and a stream-copy mux keeps them; all 36 audio-bearing files on disk read `bt709/bt709/bt709/tv`. No bt2020 pipeline render exists on this machine, so bt2020 is proven only on a bt2020-tagged fixture through the mux, not on a real HDR render. (4) **The "verify before deleting" gap was real:** `core.py` called `restore_audio(...)` ignoring its result and deleted the intermediate on `os.path.isfile(destination)` - a partial destination from a dying mux satisfied it. (5) **The 14-section report exists** (`runtime_state.py`, `/api/runtime/state`; `TERMINAL_CONTRACT.md`) but carried no stage wall clocks, seek counts, passthrough / GPU frame counts or peak VRAM, and `docs/BENCHMARKS.md` is documentation, not a runtime sink: the report stays a JSON payload and BENCHMARKS.md documents it. (6) **`/ws/telemetry` has carried `stages` since Phase 4 but no file in `react-ui/src` read it.**
  *Stage 4 built (`roop/finalize_output.py`, called from `core.batch_process_regular`; `app.api._run_swap` turns a failure into the job's error, so the queue marks it FAILED instead of COMPLETED).* Probe the intermediate (frames, colour tags) -> mux (or move when `skip_audio`) -> one ffprobe header read of the destination, verified against the intermediate: exists and non-empty, video stream with non-zero duration, frame count equal to the intermediate's and to the trim's, colour tags unchanged, audio present when the source has it, starting and ending within one frame of the video -> ONLY THEN delete the intermediate and the helper files. On any failure the intermediate is kept, the partial destination removed, and the reasons printed and recorded. 14 real-ffmpeg tests (`test_finalize_output.py`): trimmed lip-sync by FFT cross-correlation (30-90, 45-105), bt709 and bt2020 survive the mux, a mux that dies / reports success over garbage / drops a frame / strips the tags / mismatches the trim, silent move, stopped render, leftover segment parts reported, unreadable intermediate. Mutation-checked: five deliberate breakages, all caught.
  *Report built (`roop/stage_telemetry.build_summary`, `roop/vram_peak.py`).* Stage 1: wall clock, sampled frames, worker pool utilization (detector time over workers x wall; `sparse_range_scan`), active ranges. Stage 2: wall clock, coalesced ranges, `cap.set` seeks vs `cap.grab` frames walked (`range_seek` totals, zeroed on Stage 2 entry), verified target frames. Stage 3: wall clock, zero-GPU passthrough frames, GPU frames, frames with a swapped face, faces swapped, peak VRAM. Stage 4: the finalize result. Run: effective fps (excludes the ~30 s of model load that precedes Stage 1), speedup. Peak VRAM is `torch.cuda.mem_get_info` sampled every 250 ms (device-wide: torch's own allocator cannot see ONNX/TensorRT memory) with the used value at run start beside it, since the desktop is in the number. A metric that was not measured is `null`, never 0. Counters only move while their stage is RUNNING. Delivery: `snapshot()` is already spread into every `/ws/telemetry` frame, so `summary` and per-stage `wall_s` ride it; `_record_run_history` stores `report` on the history entry; `PERFORMANCE.pipeline_report` in `/api/runtime/state` (still 14 sections). UI (`PipelineReport.jsx`, `reportFormat.js`): the HUD on `#/processing` (live stage rows, replaced by the report when the run ends; above the live/finished split because the report arrives when the run ends), "Last run report" on `#/settings` (falls back to the newest history entry that has one and says so), a collapsed card per run on `#/history`.
  *Proof it executes (`tests/verify_stage4_report.py`, real renders of the Phase 5 clip with noise audio, user's live models, `sparse_auto_range` ON; `docs/perf/stage9/verify_stage4_report.json`).* 27 invariants over three arms (CFR full, CFR trimmed 150-510, irregular-timestamp VFR), exit 0: Stage 4 ok with the intermediate deleted and no leftover file; delivered frames equal the SOURCE'S OWN ffprobe packet count (600 / 360 / 591); lip-sync under one frame by cross-correlation; colour tags bt709 and equal to the intermediate's; `passthrough + gpu == frames run` and `passthrough == frames outside Stage 1's ranges` exactly (408+192=600, 168+192=360, 403+188=591); Stage 1+2+3 walls measured and inside the render's; peak VRAM 11.4-11.5 GB of 12.0 against a 4.1 GB base; worker utilization 95-98% of 2 workers; the report's `set_calls` / `grab_calls` equal the totals the seekers print themselves (2 / 0 on CFR, 0 / 272 on VFR, where ranges are positioned by decoding); the report is JSON-safe and equal to what `/api/runtime/state` carries. Browser: `e2e/pipeline-report.spec.js` (5, real Chromium against the production build, the real report fed through the page's own request and WebSocket traffic; screenshots in `docs/perf/stage9/`), the whole Playwright suite (108 passed), the render-check suite and a new `render-pipeline-report.mjs` (31 checks).
  *Defects the verification found in my own work (kept, not hidden).* (1) **The first run read `passthrough + gpu` = 624 / 374 / 615 for 600 / 360 / 600 frames** (`first_run_before_count_fix.json`): a parallel stabilization block re-runs its warm-up frames through `process_frame` and discards them, and an OOM recovery reruns a frame it already counted. Counting now honours a thread-local flag set on both (`ProcessMgr._count_stage3`, `_process_frame_uncounted`). The count the wrapper sees is not the count of frames written - the standing failure class of this project. (2) **A "VFR truncation" that was my generator**: the VFR clip came out 591 frames against an assumed 600 and Stage 4 passed it, which looked like a pipeline that stops short plus a check that cannot see it. The clip itself holds 591 packets (`write_clip(vfr=True)` uses `-fps_mode vfr`, which drops frames); the pipeline was right. I had already added a source-length cross-check in response; cv2's frame count equals the packet count on 166 of 166 real video files here, so it had no demonstrated target and was removed (`second_run_vfr_ground_truth_wrong.json` keeps the run). (3) The first colour test passed with `None == None` (CLI colour flags tagged only matrix and range on this ffmpeg build); the fixture now asserts it is tagged and the result carries `color_tagged`. (4) `PipelineReport.jsx` and `pipelineReport.js` differed only by case, so on a case-insensitive filesystem an extensionless import resolved back to the .jsx (caught by `lint:unreachable`); renamed `reportFormat.js`. (5) `PipelineHud` rendered nothing under server rendering because zustand answers it with the store's initial state; split into `PipelineHud` (adapter) and `PipelineHudView` (pure). (6) The Processing tab's finished view never mounted the HUD (found in the browser, not in the unit tests).
  *Speedup multiplier: an ESTIMATE, labelled one (`speedup.estimated`, `method`).* A dense 1-stage render is not run during a job, so the baseline is this run's own per-active-frame cost: (Stage 2 + Stage 3 wall) x total / active, over the 3-stage wall. Against the measured dense arms (ABBA after a warm-up; `render()` carries ~30 s of model load in no stage wall, which is why the whole-call ratio is lower): estimate 2.18 / 2.23x; measured pipeline-only 2.34 / 2.65x per dense arm (ratio of means 2.50x; the first run read 2.55x, the second 2.91x: the two dense arms differ by up to 27%, first-arm effects); end to end including model load 1.67x. So the estimate UNDERstates by about 11-24% here and is not inside the dense arms' own range; nothing gates on it. Dense swapped 172 frames against the 3-stage's 156 (Phase 5's recall invariant holds for the 3-stage plan), so part of the speedup is frames the dense arm swaps and the 3-stage path does not: read it beside the swap rate.
  *Not done / caveats.* (1) A multi-video job's report covers the LAST video (`videos_in_job` says how many). (2) `synthetic_label.label_file` rewrites the file AFTER Stage 4 verifies it. (3) The HDR / bt2020 pipeline output was not rendered (no such render exists here). (4) RTX 3060 untested: peak VRAM, worker utilization and the speedup are 4070 numbers. (5) The estimator's 11-24% understatement is measured on one clip. (6) The `ProcessingTerminal` section card hides `pipeline_report` (it has its own panel); the other 13 sections are unchanged.
  *Files:* `roop/finalize_output.py`, `roop/vram_peak.py`, `roop/stage_telemetry.py`, `roop/range_seek.py`, `roop/sparse_range_scan.py`, `roop/runtime_state.py`, `ProcessMgr._count_stage3`; tests `test_finalize_output.py` (14), `test_stage_report.py` (31), `verify_stage4_report.py`, `react-ui/.render-check/render-pipeline-report.mjs`, `react-ui/e2e/pipeline-report.spec.js`; report `docs/perf/stage9/`.

- **"Phase 8: Stage 3B zero-copy OrtValue GPU buffer chaining and resilient provider fallback" brief: a device out-of-memory error on a frame is now recovered (flush, rerun alone) instead of writing the frame untouched; the zero-copy chain is NOT built because the hops it would remove cost 0.27% of a face.**
  *Premises, with sites.* (1) **`83748d0`, `a7820f6`, `e8c89f8` and `b900deb` are not in this repository** (`git log` cannot resolve them); `967e17e` (per-model precision, XSeg FP32) and `7a15446` (RF++ native runner, opt-in) exist. (2) **Pre-allocated, device-resident I/O buffers already exist**: `ZeroCopyBufferPool` (GPEN Realistic, RF++, UltraMax; Stage 3 of 2026-10-18), `trt_bound_session.py` (`ROOP_TRT_BOUND`), the GPU post-chains in `enhance_common`. (3) **Runtime recovery did not exist**: `build_session_with_fallback` walks TensorRT -> CUDA -> CPU at session BUILD only; at run time `ProcessMgr`'s per-frame handler logged the error, `audit_frame_failed` counted it and the ORIGINAL frame went to the encoder. No site flushed an allocator or retried.
  *Zero-copy chaining: measured, not built (`tests/probe_stage8_pcie.py`, `docs/perf/stage8/pcie_hops.json`, 4070).* The six host<->device hops of one face (swapper in/out 0.19 MB, XSeg in/out 0.75/0.25 MB, restorer in/out 3 MB each) cost **0.653 ms pageable / 0.392 ms pinned in total, against 246 ms of stage time per face (swap 93.7 + mask 2 x 33.7 + enhance 84.9, regression benchmark) = 0.27% / 0.16%**. Keeping the crop on-device end to end therefore cannot return more than that, before the cost of GPU mask feathering, colour transfer and paste that today run on CPU threads concurrently with the GPU. The pipeline is GPU-bound (Stage 3: GPEN Realistic 26.4 -> 20.7 ms/face isolated, 8-thread throughput unchanged).
  *Built: `roop/gpu_recovery.py`, `ROOP_GPU_RECOVERY` (default ON).* `classify()` separates `oom` (retried), `shape` (counted, not retried: deterministic) and everything else (untouched path, sticky CUDA errors included). `RecoveryGate`: frames run shared; a recovery waits for every other frame to leave (a waiting recovery holds NEW frames back), flushes `gc` + the torch caching allocator, reruns the frame ALONE on a fresh copy of the pristine snapshot (the failed attempt may have mutated the frame in place) and releases the gate; `abort` keeps a stopped render from hanging behind a parked worker; `before_wait` lets the stabilisation path release its warm-up dedup slot first so no neighbour waits for a frame about to be redone. Wired at the sequential worker, the scheduler and the stabilisation counted-frame path (`_process_stab_frame(pristine=..., before_wait=...)`); warm-up frames are not retried. The audit summary and `_audit_reset` carry the counters.
  *Proof it executes (`tests/verify_gpu_recovery.py`, real render, 240-frame Love clip, user's live models, an ORT `CUDA failure 2: out of memory` raised once on frames 12/40/75/130 inside `swap_faces`; `docs/perf/stage8/verify_gpu_recovery.json`).* Recovery ON: 4 of 4 recovered, 0 failed, 2.1 s spent draining workers, **output bit-identical to the fault-free CLEAN render on all 240 frames**. Recovery OFF (the negative control, the old behaviour): the four frames were written untouched (0.27-0.84/255 off the CLEAN render) and **231 of 240 frames differ from CLEAN**, because a failed frame leaves its block's temporal filters half-advanced. My first run of this probe wrapped only the sequential and scheduler paths and showed ON == OFF with zero recoveries: production renders go through the stabilisation path, which was not wrapped. `tests/test_gpu_recovery.py` (28) pins classification, the gate (a negative control for exclusivity and for new frames waiting), single retry, the original error on a second failure, abort, `before_wait` ordering and the three call sites.
  *Not built, and why.* (1) A per-frame switch to a secondary execution provider: a CPU session of the swapper or a restorer is a second model load (seconds per face) and the project rejected a runtime ladder on purpose (`create_strictly_accelerated_session`; a CPU-only session behind a CUDA request was an invisible slowdown). The build-time ladder is unchanged. (2) Shrinking an ONNX Runtime arena: ORT keeps freed blocks (kNextPowerOfTwo) and exposes no external trim; only the torch allocator (zero-copy ring, GPU post-processing, SAM 2) is emptied. (3) A persistent downshift of worker count after repeated OOMs: the governor already sizes workers from the VRAM plan; a mid-render change would need a live resize of the pools.
  *Caveats.* The injected fault fires before the frame touches any state, so the bit-identical result does not show what a real OOM does when it strikes after the mask filter advanced: the retry can step that block's temporal filters a second time on that one frame (without the retry the frame is lost AND the filters are half-advanced anyway). Not run on the 3060, where real OOMs are likelier. The 4070 never produced a real one here.

- **"Phase 7: Stage 2.5 temporal landmark stabilization and active-range SAM 2 / brush mask gating" brief: SAM 2 now tracks only the frames Stage 2 kept, each run on a fresh memory bank; a zero-phase landmark filter is built and measured but ships OFF; vector pre-binding is not built.**
  *Premises, with sites.* (1) **Smoothing already exists** - `_build_temporal_faces` (`procmgr_tracking.py`) runs `AdaptiveLandmarkSmoother` over the cached `kps` and `landmark_2d_106` per locked track, cut at shot boundaries. Its real defects are that it is causal (it trails a fast turn) and that `landmark_3d_68` is left raw. (2) **`ROOP_STAB_STREAMING` is irrelevant here**: it is the Stage 3 FIFO stabilizer and does nothing to landmarks in temporal mode. (3) **Brush masks are not SAM 2 propagated** - paint/erase masks are static per-faceset arrays (`face_masks` include/exclude), so there is nothing temporal to gate; only SAM 2 occlusion tracking was. (4) **SAM 2 ignored Stage 1 and Stage 2 entirely**: `precompute` decoded and buffered the whole trimmed clip (12.6 MB per 1080p frame) and ran one memory bank across every cut and empty stretch, seeded from frame 0's faces. On the Phase 5 clip 444 of 600 frames hold no target face and were tracked anyway.
  *SAM 2 gating built (`roop/sam2_runs.py`, `Mask_SAM2.precompute_runs/_propagate`, `ProcessMgr._run_sam2_prepass/_precompute_sam2_runs`, `procmgr_batch`).* A run is a maximal stretch of consecutive `stage2_frame_plan` frames (`a_{i+1} - b_i > 1` starts a new one). Only run frames are decoded (exact `RangeSeeker` positioning), each run gets `init_state` ... `reset_state`, seeded with the plan's own boxes at the run's first frame, and masks are stored at absolute indices so `get_crop_mask` is unchanged (a frame outside every run has no mask: the swap is not extra-masked, as for any frame SAM 2 never saw). Without a plan the Stage 1 ranges are the runs (seeded by a detection on each first frame); without either it is the old whole-clip pass. In temporal mode the pass now runs right after Stage 2 (before the analysis models are released); otherwise where it ran. A SAM 2 failure is contained and leaves the swap unmasked, as before. `tests/test_sam2_runs.py` (17) drives the real orchestration against a fake predictor that records every call, and pins the call sites. **Not measured: SAM 2 wall time or VRAM on real weights** - the checkpoint is a pickle `.pt` from a CDN and was not downloaded; what is measured is coverage (156 of 600 frames on the Phase 5 clip).
  *Zero-phase smoothing (`ZeroPhaseTrackSmoother`, `ROOP_STAB_ZERO_PHASE`, default OFF).* The shipped ramp run forward and backward and averaged, velocity from a central difference of raw kps, one beta shared by kps / lm106 / lm68, runs cut at shot boundaries and gaps. Offline on real captures it has no lag where the shipped filter trails (>= 0.75 frame) and is quieter on a still face; lowering beta buys jitter with error against ground truth, so 0.35 stays. **End to end (production render, three real clips, A/B deterministic - repeat arms bit-identical on the two clips where both ran) the effect is small**: placement error within ~1 % of inter-ocular except fast frames on Weeds (12.75 -> 10.01 %), still-frame flicker -0.5 % (angle), -4.4 % (Love), flat (Weeds); output-vs-output mean abs diff on Love 0.52/255 with no frame above 0.5 % of pixels differing by > 8. A look change that small does not justify flipping a default without the regression benchmark's say-so, so it stays opt-in. The cache-level lag (1-7 %) versus the near-zero rendered placement change is unreconciled; detector disagreement on the re-detected frames is the likely floor of that metric.
  *Not built: vector pre-binding.* The Stage 0 table (`stage0_preflight.py`, Phase 6) already freezes the pose-selected / blended identity per head-pose cell before frame 0 and serves the swap pass with a 1.8 us lookup; storing a 512-D vector per cached record would duplicate it (and 34 % of swaps are off-plan, so the swap pass needs the table anyway).
  *Files:* `ROOP_STAB_ZERO_PHASE`, `ROOP_STAB_ZERO_PHASE_BETA` in `docs/ENV_FLAGS.md`; probes `tests/probe_stage25_{capture,filters,e2e}.py`; tests `test_zero_phase_smoother.py` (14), `test_sam2_runs.py` (17).

## 2026-10-20

- **"Phase 6: Stage 0 pre-flight caching, .fsz pose-bank indexing and checkpoint resume persistence" brief: a resumed project now skips Stage 1 AND Stage 2 (0 detector calls against 862) and picks up at Stage 3 on a verified cache; the Stage 0 source-identity table is exact (166/166 face-frames bit-identical to the live path) but is INERT on every `.fsz` this repo ships (34 of 34 are V1); the TensorRT compile signal already existed on another socket and now rides `/ws/telemetry` too, with the one canary that was never announced (the swapper's) fixed. Four of the brief's premises were wrong, and the tests found five defects of mine on the way.**
  *Premises, with sites.* (1) **`b900deb` is not in this repository** (`git log` cannot resolve it); the compile signal it names is `roop/pipeline_status.py` (`STAGE_COMPILING_TRT`, emitted from `predictor.py:551` around the first inference of each model), served at `GET /api/v1/pipeline/status` and `WS /ws/v1/pipeline/status` and drawn by `CompileBanner.jsx` since Stage 5 of the React work. `967e17e` / `7a15446` are the aux and restorer canaries (`aux_canary.check_session` announces `STAGE_RUNNING_CANARY`). What was genuinely missing: the signal was NOT on `/ws/telemetry`, and `swap_canary.check_engine` (a reference session plus two runs of each engine before frame 0) was announced nowhere. (2) **`stage2_frame_plan` is not what Stage 3 consumes** (`roop/stage2_plan.py` says so and holds no Face on purpose, to keep 580 MB per 100k face-frames out of RSS): the hand-off is `_temporal_faces`, `_track_assignments`, `_track_source_map`, `_shot_boundaries` and `_temporal_covered`. Persisting the plan, as the brief said, could not have skipped Stage 2; the plan is stored beside the hand-off and used as its integrity check. (3) **The brief names `app/roop/core.py`, `ProcessMgr.py` and `trt_session_builder.py`; none of the three is where the work lives** (`procmgr_batch.run_batch_inmem`, `procmgr_tracking`, `pipeline_status`, `swap_canary`; `trt_session_builder.build_tensorrt_session` is used only by the dormant `optimized_*` classes). (4) **Every faceset in `app/facesets` is a V1 archive (34 of 34, `read_faceset_archive` returns None)**: `pose_embedding_for_target` returns None for V1, so the V2 yaw/pitch-cell branch, and with it the Stage 0 table, does nothing on the user's own library unless an Identity Blender recipe is set. The table was exercised on a V2 archive built with real detection from `ashna` (`write_faceset_v2`, written into the work dir, not the library).
  *Checkpoint resume (`roop/stage_cache.py`, `app/stage_checkpoint.py`, `project_checkpoint.record_stage / usable_stage_cache`).* The moment Stage 1 finishes its active ranges (absolute frames) are written into the project record; the moment Stage 2 finishes its hand-off goes to `<project id>.stage2.npz` beside it (uncompressed zip, written member by member and replaced atomically; face fields COLUMNAR, the nested pose block in 2048-face JSON blocks so a 100k-face clip never holds one document; `allow_pickle=False` on the way in) with its size and SHA-256 in the record. Both sit under ONE key: target SHA-256, original trim, every source's SHA-256, the captured target faces, the target-to-source mapping, the settings fingerprint, model identity, provider, precision, the application version and the `ROOP_*` environment. The basis is stored beside the key so a refusal names what changed ("changed since the cache was written: the target video, the trim points"). A resumed job (`api._run_swap` installs `roop.globals._stage_cache` for a project's run only) restores Stage 1 re-based and clipped to the resumed window, checks Stage 2 was sealed against the same ranges INSIDE that window, hashes the sidecar, decodes only the blocks the window touches, rebuilds the frame plan from the restored faces and requires it to equal the sealed one, then hands over. Anything else runs the stage and prints `[StageCache] not used: <reason>`. A user Stop, a short scan, a `fail_safe_full` Stage 1, SAM 2 and animated WebP are never cached; `COMPLETED` deletes the cache. The record is now read-modify-written under one lock (a segment commit and a stage record used to be able to overwrite each other).
  *Proof it executes (`tests/verify_stage_resume.py`, real renders, the Phase 5 600-frame clip, user's live models, `sparse_auto_range` ON; report `docs/perf/stage6/verify_stage_resume.json`).* B1 stopped mid-Stage 3 with 2 committed segments; the project held both stages. B2 resumed at frame 288 (past the first active range, so Stage 1 came back CLIPPED, 1 range / 171 of 312 frames): `/ws/telemetry` read `RESTORED` for Stage 1 and 2, **0 detector calls in the pipeline against 862 in the uninterrupted reference** (the 5 calls before Stage 0 are the harness ingesting the source faceset's photos, in every run), the same 156 swapped frames, the same plan restricted to the window (151/151), 600 output frames, PSNR 47.9-51.2 dB on swapped frames after the resume point (lossy encoder; 75-99 dB elsewhere). Wall time 36.0 s resumed against 45.3 s / 48.9 s when the cache is refused (-21% / -26%): a tampered sidecar and a changed `ROOP_TEMPORAL_GAP` were each refused with the reason printed, re-ran both stages (hundreds of pipeline detector calls, 0 with the cache) and still produced the identical swap set. Codec on a REAL pre-pass (Love, 477 faces, nested `_pose_v5` Faces, mixed `det_score` types): 2.20 MB (4.7 KB/face), write 28 ms, restore 29 ms, **EXACT** (type, dtype, shape, value) for the full window and for a window resumed at +97. Synthetic scale (faces with the real field mix): 20k faces write 1.1 s / +64 MB RSS, read 1.8 s / +230 MB, 81 MB on disk; 80k faces write 5.0 s / +194 MB, read 6.6 s / +933 MB (the restored set is what the pre-pass would have held anyway), 324 MB.
  *Stage 0 (`roop/stage0_preflight.py`; `ProcessMgr.process_face` now calls `pose_conditioned_input` / `blended_input` from it, the same two bodies the builder runs).* `(yaw_bin, pitch_bin, roll_bin)` -> a ready-to-swap source Face, dense 3 x 3 x 3 per source, built once before Stage 1 (`procmgr_batch._freeze_source_identity`) by running the live selectors on a stand-in target inside each cell, so the table equals the live path by construction; the blend, the tangent-plane dial offsets (`apply_offsets`) and the 0.80 identity guard are evaluated once per cell and reported (dials applied, dials refused as unwritable by `identity_render_validation.json`, cells where the guard bit). It declines, with a printed reason, wherever it could not answer exactly: image or CSCS swappers, `use_source_bank` with several faces, an angle portfolio, the temporal quality engine, a folder-upload bank (its nearest-pose blend is continuous in yaw/pitch; nine cells would change the vector), a V1 set with no recipe. `ROOP_STAGE0=0` turns it off. **Roll is a key axis that addresses one vector**, and the measurement behind that (`tests/probe_source_roll_invariance.py`, 17 reference photos, rotated +-15..60 deg with the five-point solver reading the rotation back and the face found 17/17 at every angle): cosine to the unrotated embedding mean 0.959-0.974, worst photo 0.881 at -45 deg - near-invariant, not exactly, and nothing that chooses a source reads the TARGET's roll, so a roll-conditioned gallery has no data to select from and no measured gain (not built).
  *Proof it executes (`tests/verify_stage0_identity.py`; a tap at the swapper boundary compares the vector handed over with the live path's for THAT face; report `docs/perf/stage6/verify_stage0_identity.json`).* Four renders (live / table, with and without an Identity Blender recipe, jawline dial 0.6): **166 face-frames per arm, 0 mismatches** (embedding and `normed_embedding` bit for bit), the table hit 166 times and missed 0; `pose_embedding_for_target` calls in Stage 3 **166 -> 0** (and `ResolvedBlend.transform` 166 -> 0), 9 at the Stage 0 build, **0 in Stage 1**; the same 156 swapped frames and 166 faces in every arm. Cost, honestly: selecting the vector went from 19.7 us to 1.8 us per face (87.4 us to 1.9 us with a recipe) in a frame that takes ~64 ms, i.e. ~0.1% of a frame - **not a speedup and not claimed as one** (the pipeline is GPU-bound; the wall times of the four arms, 38.3 / 41.0 / 42.6 / 43.2 s, are not counterbalanced and are noise). What it buys is that the choice is made once, before decode, is reported, and cannot drift between Stage 3 and a resumed Stage 3.
  *Compile / canary state on `/ws/telemetry` (`pipeline_status.telemetry_fields`, folded in by `api._telemetry_snapshot`; `swap_canary.check_engine` now announced; `Processing.jsx`, `telemetryStore.js`, `App.jsx`).* Flat, non-ticking fields (`engine_state` IDLE | COMPILING_TRT | RUNNING_CANARY, `engine_label` "Compiling TensorRT Engine..." / "Verifying TensorRT Engine...", `engine_model`, `engine_precision`, `engine_since`; no elapsed seconds, or the change-only sampler would send 4 frames a second through a 15-minute build); the run bar and the headline show the label instead of a frozen "Starting..." and the store forgets it when the socket drops (a backend that died mid-build would otherwise show it forever). Render-check 82/82 (the headline site was mutation-checked: reverting one of the two sites passed an `includes` check and failed the occurrence count I changed it to), lint and build clean; `test_engine_telemetry.py` (12), including the swapper canary announced while its reference is built and a failing reference still ending the stage. NOT exercised: a real browser against a real cold engine build.
  *Defects of my own that the tests found, fixed.* (a) `record_stage` deleted the sidecar it had been called to record when no earlier record existed. (b) The track-mean embedding that every assignment and the source map share came back as a new view per access, so the sharing the pre-pass relies on was lost. (c) **After a real mid-clip resume Stage 1 is clipped to the shorter window while Stage 2 was sealed against the unclipped ranges, so Stage 2 would have been refused exactly when it matters**; both sides are now compared cut to the resumed window (unit test written first and failing; the e2e resume at 288 exercises it). (d) Hashing every `ROOP_*` variable would have refused a same-session pause-and-resume, because the runtime optimizer exports `ROOP_RUNTIME_PROFILE_*` mid-run and they persist; run-generated variables are excluded and pinned by a test. (e) The block-index arithmetic of the JSON columns was invisible to my first tests (every test face carried identical JSON values, so a mutated index returned equal-looking faces); faces now differ per face and the mutation fails two tests.
  *Gates.* Suite in 9 batches, one process each (`tests/test_occlusion_interaction.py` and the explicitly-named `tests/test_performance_regression.py` run on their own, they are 8 and 16 minutes of real renders): 4960 passed, 15 skipped, 2 xfailed across the batches. Failures found and fixed: `test_identity_algebra::WiringTests` (a source pin on `_blend.transform(` that my move into `stage0_preflight` invalidated), `test_exception_visibility::no_new_silent_broad_handlers` (the adapter logged through its own callable; its five handlers now also report through `_swallowed`) and `test_process_mgr_layout` (the batch mixin allows ONE runtime entry point; the Stage 0 builder moved to `stage0_preflight.freeze_for`). PRE-EXISTING, reproduced on the original tree or already recorded: `test_ui_zoom_pan::MagnifierLens`, `test_launcher_e2e_harness` and `test_swap_batch_equivalence` (`No module named 'fixtures'` at collection), and `test_performance_regression::test_end_to_end_fidelity_throughput_and_av_timing[image]` (face SSIM 0.99028 / LPIPS 0.00581, identical to the 5 digits recorded on 01ddb48; the other 10 pass). `test_selected_face_safety` failed twice in one batch (404 for 409) and passed on every re-run: order-dependent, not touched. Occlusion suite 22 passed, 5 xfailed. Regression benchmark PASS (300/300 decided and changed, face SSIM 0.9906 / min 0.9868, the same numbers as before this phase; `docs/perf/stage6/regression_benchmark.txt`). Render-check 82/82, lint and build clean.
  *Open / not done.* RTX 3060 not measured (the 2.5 GB RSS rule, a >=50k-face resume and the 80k-face read peak of +933 MB are unmeasured there); no project-delete route exists, so an abandoned project keeps its sidecar (~4 KB per face); a `git pull` between interrupt and resume changes the application version and refuses the cache (conservative on purpose); the cache is not used with SAM 2 (its per-frame masks are not in it); the resumed render's boundary frames were compared by PSNR on 12 samples, not frame by frame; Stage 0 changes nothing on the user's V1 library.

- **"Phase 5: end-to-end verification suite" built (`app/tests/verify_three_stage_pipeline.py`, report `docs/perf/stage5_verify/verify_three_stage_pipeline.json`): all five invariants hold for CFR and VFR, exit 0, 1-stage vs 3-stage measured at 2.54x. Building it found one real product defect (a 3-way split queued from the UI joined to 597 frames, not 600), fixed in `routes_queue._apply_segment`.**
  *The clip.* 600 frames, 960x540, 30 fps, every frame's content known: 0-99 empty landscape; 100-199 a non-target person walks across; 200-204 5-frame flash of the target; 205-299 empty gap; 300-450 target enters at -69.7 deg yaw, turns frontal, is crossed by the distractor drawn IN FRONT of it; 451-599 empty. Written twice (CFR with an audio track; VFR with the `test_range_seek.py` timestamp pattern, `avg_frame_rate` 6000/203, `source_is_cfr` False). **The profile is real head footage, not a warp:** a 2-D perspective warp of a frontal faceset photo stops being detected between 45 and 55 deg (`docs/perf/stage5_verify/yaw_warp_detection.txt`), so a synthetic 70 deg would test the detector's blindness. The sweep is `angle.mp4` frames 322-360 (CGI head, -69.7 -> -0.8 deg, det_score ~1.0), found through `fixtures.clip` (a missing fixture exits 3 = PENDING, never substitutes). The distractor is the app's own faceset photo. The harness reads `selected_enhancer` / `swap_model` / `mask_engine` / threads from the live `config.yaml` (UltraMax, realswap, RealityUX, 20), captures the target from three stills (frontal, 3/4, steep profile) the way 'auto angles' enriches a capture, and runs everything in one process through the production render (`compare_enhancers_video.render`) with the pre-pass's `stage2_frame_plan` sealed by a hook.
  *Results (4070, 2026-10-20).* (1) recall 156/156 target frames in the plan, CFR and VFR; the flash (200-204) is found. (2) 0 frames of 0-190 / 460-599 in the plan. (3) **0 detector calls in Stage 3, against 867 (CFR) / 866 (VFR) in the pre-pass.** (4) the sanitiser step raises no TypeError. (5) three slices on disk before the join, joined output 600 frames. Zero runtime exceptions across 6 renders; the pre-pass plan is identical across two 3-stage runs. Benchmark, ABBA after warming both paths: **1-stage 127.1 s (4.72 fps) vs 3-stage 50.1 s (11.98 fps), 2.54x, same-config spread 0.135, resolvable.** Both arms swap all 156 target frames; the 1-stage arm also paints **16 faces onto frames with no face** (205-215, 451-455, coasting past the target) that the 3-stage arm does not.
  *Things the brief named that do not exist, and what was used.* `_sanitize_face_attributes` -> `roop/face_sanity.py::sanitize_face_attributes`, plus a8df052's own reader `ProcessMgr._face_occluded_frac`; commit `ba3cff7` is not in the history (the Albumentations notice is silenced with `NO_ALBUMENTATIONS_UPDATE` and a warnings filter). `face_analyser.get` is not what the production detector calls: invariant 3 first read 0 everywhere because it was hooked on `FaceAnalysis.get` and the shipped detector is the RetinaFace r50 branch, so the gate counts the funnel `face_util._detect_faces_raw` (and `FaceAnalysis.get`) and **fails if the pre-pass count is 0** - a counter that never fired is not evidence.
  *What the suite found.* **(a) Segment join was 597 frames.** The UI stages a 3-way split as 1-based inclusive (1,200) (201,400) (401,600); `_apply_segment` handed the start straight to a 0-based entry whose end is exclusive, so each slice rendered 199 frames and frames 0, 200 and 400 were rendered by nobody (`first_run_before_range_fix.json`: 199/199/199, joined 597). `get_video_frame` maps UI frame n to index n-1, so every job queued from the UI also dropped its first frame (and the audio cut started a frame late). Fixed: `startframe = fs - 1`; `test_queue_segments.py::UiRangesTileTheClip` (4, any split of 7..1000 frames covers every frame once) and two existing tests that pinned the pass-through updated (`test_queue`, `test_batch_matrix_queue`). **Not changed:** `/api/target/set_frame` stores the UI's 1-based start the same way (a manual 'Set as start' skips one frame); that is a different contract and was left for a decision. (b) **The gating scene was first built too hard and the result changed my scene, not the pipeline.** At peak sprite IoU 0.6 (75% of the target under the distractor) recall was 149/156: 373-379 were missing because `procmgr_tracking._interp_collides` refuses to paint an invented target face onto the distractor's real face (7 gap-fills 'refused as unbridgeable', 0 filled). That refusal is deliberate and defensible, and the detector cannot see a face >~55% covered either (`synthetic_scenes.py`), so the gating crossing was moved to IoU 0.30 (<= ~46% covered) AFTER seeing it, and 0.6 is kept as a non-gated stress arm that reports the same 373-379 as 'physically hidden >= 55%'. If 100% recall under a 75% occlusion is wanted, that is a pipeline change (mask the object, never refuse: the 09-21 rule), not a test one. (c) The first benchmark's 1-stage arm took 1145 s against 84 s warm: the warm-up had only covered the 3-stage path; both paths are warmed now.
  *Flash alignment, honestly.* The Stage 1 scan samples every S = floor(0.2 fps) = 6 frames; the flash is 200-204 and 204 = 34 x 6 lands on a sample, so the flash is caught by the grid as well as by the cut probe. A flash placed between samples is the known 'sub-S flash' gap and is NOT exercised by this clip.
  *Gates.* Suite in 10 batches (app/tests 8 batches + tests/ 2), one process each: 940 / 784 / 837 / 649 / 744 / 669 / 627 / 183 + 174 / 509 passed. Failures: `test_fixture_paths::test_every_user_imports_fixtures_at_module_level` caught MY harness (`import fixtures` inside `main`), fixed, passes; `test_ui_zoom_pan::MagnifierLens` PRE-EXISTING. Not re-run this round: `tests/test_occlusion_interaction.py` and `tests/test_performance_regression.py` (~25 min of renders; their code path is untouched by this change, last passed earlier today) and `test_swap_batch_equivalence.py` (fails collection on the original tree). `tests/test_batch_matrix_queue.py::test_cancelling_the_running_job_does_not_cancel_the_next_one` fails when it runs together with `test_queue*` on the ORIGINAL tree too (order-dependent), passes alone. `app/tests/test_verify_three_stage_logic.py` (14, no GPU): the gates are shown failing as well as passing, including the sanitiser gate with a raising and a no-op sanitiser.

- **"Phase 4: segment persistence, distributed GOP slicing, React telemetry sync" brief: segment persistence was already fixed (10-18) and is now pinned by a named site + test; the GOP stream-copy bypass is MEASURED and NOT built (the plan is not where the decision is made, plan-absence is not swap-absence, and the user's own encoder makes the copy impossible); three real defects fixed on the REST side (request-thread SHA-256 of the whole target, pre-pass stages invisible to `/ws/telemetry`, preview seeks queued behind the pre-pass).**
  *Segments (no code change).* The overwrite bug in the brief (`#bug: work processing in segments`) is the one fixed on 10-18: slices are named `{stem}_segment{idx:04d}_{start:06d}_{end:06d}.mp4` (`roop/segment_output.py:57`), `api._clear_output_folder` skips a slice of a split render (`api.py` ~800), the queue joins only when every slice of the group is `COMPLETED` (`routes_queue._join_if_group_done`, ~617) through `join_segments` (verified manifest, `.part.mp4` + `os.replace`, segments kept). Test that drives it: `test_queue_segments.py::SplitThroughTheRunner` runs the real runner with `clear_output` ON and real ffmpeg - all three slices survive, `clip_joined.mp4` has 30 frames and audio, a failed slice blocks the join (the 25 tests in the two segment files pass). Each COMPLETED job's produced file is recorded in its project checkpoint (`api._run_swap` -> `update_checkpoint(partial_files=...)`) and the group's `segment` markers + `group_join` result are in `queue.json`. **Not done, on purpose:** the brief's `{output_dir}/{job_id}/segments/{stem}__seg_...` staging layout - no collision exists to fix, and it would move the files out from beside `_joined.mp4`, which the 10-18 contract and those tests pin. **Open:** nothing re-joins a complete group after an app restart (the manual `/api/queue/join` exists); the runner test replaces `_run_swap`, so the checkpoint write for a segment job is read, not exercised.
  *GOP stream-copy bypass: measured, NOT built (`tests/probe_gop_bypass_stage4.py`, `docs/perf/stage4_gop/*.json`).* (1) **The plan does not exist where the decision is made.** `render_distributed` plans chunks in the parent, which loads no model (module docstring); `stage2_frame_plan` is built inside each worker's pre-pass, after it has already paid the detection pass over the whole chunk. (2) **Plan-absence is not swap-absence** - the 10-20 Stage 3 finding (Monica 31 of 91 swaps off-plan) stands. At GOP granularity on the clips I could re-cut: Weeds 2 zero-plan GOPs (107 of 300 frames) hold 0 swaps, Love 1 (26 of 240 frames) holds 0, Monica has none; Love's 2 off-plan swaps sit in GOPs that do hold plan frames. That is 3 clips, and the Monica slice that had 34% off-plan was not reproduced (the clips were re-cut with `-c copy`; the originals are gone). The sound criterion is Stage 1's mask: frames outside it already pass through unswapped today (`ProcessMgr.process_frame` ~3345), so copying a GOP with no active frame would change nothing visible - but only with `sparse_auto_range` on, and it needs a parent-side scan process that does not exist. (3) **The user's encoder forbids it.** `output_video_codec: hevc_nvenc` with the usual H.264 source: `ffmpeg -f concat -c copy` of a copied H.264 GOP + an hevc_nvenc GOP returns rc 0, prints nothing, and writes a file with the re-encoded half MISSING (176 of 300, 55 of 152, 156 of 214 frames, three clips). With h264_nvenc / libx264 the join is 300/300, 152/152, 214/214 and both halves decode frame-for-frame identical to their standalone decodes (ffmpeg's decoder only; no player was tried). The existing guards already refuse this: the `signatures` / `output_codec` equality and the `_count(joined) != expected` check in `render_distributed`. So a bypass is valid only when source codec == output codec, and has to keep that frame-count check. Left for a decision: build the parent-side Stage 1 scan + same-codec-only bypass, or drop it.
  *REST (`/api/swap` and the resume endpoints).* Stages 1-3 already run only in the worker thread (`api._start_existing_project` spawns `_run_swap`; `queue_start` spawns the runner; `procmgr_batch` runs inside it). What did not: `_create_processing_project` called `file_identity()` = **SHA-256 of the whole target on the request thread** (1 GiB: 700 ms warm, measured; the 200 ms budget is gone above ~280 MB, and a cold disk is several times that); `/api/projects`, `/validate`, `/load`, `/resume` and `/api/queue/resume` re-hashed the target, every source and every partial output (the project LIST did it for every project). Fixed: `file_identity(defer_hash=True)` records size + mtime and `sha256_pending` (1 GiB: 0.1 ms); `validate(quick=True)` on the request thread compares sizes only; `project_checkpoint.finalize_inputs` (called at the top of `_run_swap`) fills pending digests and verifies every stored one before the job touches a file, failing the job with the reason. A file edited to the same size passes the request and is refused by the job (`test_a_same_size_edit_slips_past_quick_but_not_past_the_worker`). Not measured: an end-to-end `/api/swap` HTTP timing on a live app.
  *Stage telemetry (`roop/stage_telemetry.py`, folded into `/ws/telemetry` by `api._telemetry_snapshot`).* `stage` + `stages{STAGE_1_SPARSE_PRESCAN, STAGE_2_ACTIVE_PREPASS, STAGE_3_MAIN_SWAP}`, each `{state, stage_progress_pct, active_ranges_count, verified_target_frames, skipped_frames_pct, eta_seconds}`; written from `procmgr_batch` (stage boundaries, the Stage 1 callback) and `procmgr_tracking` (every 10 frames of the pre-pass), Stage 3's counter overlaid by the web side from the `Processing frame n / total` line so the frame loop is untouched. Idle snapshots are identical, so the change-only sampler sends nothing. Proof it executes: `tests/probe_stage_telemetry_e2e.py` on Love 240f with `sparse_auto_range` ON sampled all three stages in order (Stage 1 0 -> 100%, Stage 2 0.7 -> 99.3%, Stage 3 RUNNING -> DONE) and ended with `active_ranges_count 1`, `verified_target_frames 23`, `skipped_frames_pct 37.1` (`docs/perf/stage4_telemetry/love240_sparse.json`). The probe harness has no `api`, so its Stage 3 stays 0% there; the overlay is covered by `test_stage3_takes_the_swap_passs_own_counter`.
  *Busy preview.* While Stage 1/2 runs, `/api/target/preview`, `/preview_grid` and `/preview_seq` answer an UNCACHED frame with `503 {busy, stage, retry_after_ms: 1000}` + `Retry-After: 1` without decoding (cached frames and 304s are still served; `/api/preview` already 409s during any render). React: `retryPolicy.busyDelay` - a busy refusal waits as told (clamped 250 ms - 5 s) and is NOT a failure, so eight consecutive busy answers no longer exhaust the 250/500/1000 ms budget and print "Frame unavailable" (`useThrottledFrameRequest`, `usePlaybackBuffer`; `decoder.worker.js` forwards the status and hint). `e2e/frame-busy.spec.js` (3 tests, real browser against the mock) + 45 render-check assertions + the existing `frame-unavailable` / `idle-requests` specs pass (12 e2e); disabling the hook branch fails the first one. A real failure still gives up after 1 + 3 attempts. **Not run:** a real browser against a real pre-pass; the RTX 3060.
  *Tests.* `app/tests/test_stage_telemetry_and_busy.py` (27); mutation-checked: the gate disabled -> 3 fail, the cache check removed -> 2 fail.
  *Gates.* suite in 9 batches, one process each (app/tests + tests): batches 00-05 all pass (892, 703, 624, 706, 719, 613) except batch 02's `test_hdr_color::test_pq_clip_round_trip` (ffmpeg HDR encoder broken pipe under load; 18 passed alone, FLAKE); batch 06: `test_swap_batch_equivalence.py` fails COLLECTION (`No module named 'fixtures'`) on the ORIGINAL tree too (stash-checked, PRE-EXISTING) and aborts its batch, the other 49 files re-run: 654 passed; batch 07: 515 passed, 1 failed `test_ui_zoom_pan::MagnifierLens` (PRE-EXISTING, stale since 69cbd9d); batch 08: 66 passed, 2 xfailed, 1 error = `test_occlusion_interaction` session fixture timing out at 900 s while ANOTHER pytest of mine held the GPU at 100%; alone on a quiet machine 22 passed, 5 xfailed in 8:07. Regression benchmark PASS (decided 300/300, changed 300/300, face SSIM 0.9906 min 0.9868, fps 2.71 -> 3.41). Render-checks 45/45, e2e 12/12 (frame-busy, frame-unavailable, idle-requests), lint + build clean. TRAP: TaskStop on a foreground-wrapped pytest killed the shell but left the python child alive (12.9 GB RSS, GPU 100%) and every later render timed out; look at `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` before believing a timeout.

- **"Stage 3: cached-metadata swap execution and zero-GPU fast passthrough" brief: the premise is mostly wrong and the headline ask (skip every frame absent from `stage2_frame_plan`) would have deleted real swaps - measured, not built. The real redundant GPU work is elsewhere: with "Retry rotated" selected, Stage 3 re-ran detection on frames the pre-pass had already scanned. That is removed; swap counts are unchanged on four clips re-run.**
  *Premises, with sites.* Stage 3 already consumes the pre-pass cache with no detection: `swap_faces` reads `self._temporal_faces[frame_idx]` for any frame below `_temporal_covered` (`ProcessMgr.py` ~3754), and frames outside the Stage 1 mask already return untouched before any GPU work (`process_frame`, ~3343). `stage2_frame_plan` holds no Face on purpose (see the Stage 2 entry), so "feed the plan's Faces into the swapper" has nothing to feed; `swap_faces` has no `precomputed_target_faces` parameter and does not need one. The aux-model release and pool shrink the brief asks for exist (`_release_replayed_analysis`).
  *Skipping frames absent from the plan, measured (`tests/probe_stage3_plan_coverage.py`, `docs/perf/stage3_plan/*.json`; every `process_face` call recorded with its frame index and compared with the plan sealed by the pre-pass).* Frames that really swap but are NOT in the plan: Love 240f 1 of 28, Weeds 300f 0 of 251, angle 0 of 333, angle_inverted 0 of 290, **Monica 300f 31 of 91 (34%)**. A plan-driven skip would delete a third of Monica's swaps: the swap pass swaps faces whose track the pre-pass left without a source through its per-frame fallbacks (nearest-centroid re-association, the 0.75 distance fallback). The plan is an audit index, not a gate. It is not a superset either: 8 of Love's 35 plan frames never swap.
  *The redundant GPU work that IS there.* `process_frame` sends every zero-swap frame under "Retry rotated" (the `globals.py` default; the user's `config.yaml` uses "Use untouched original frame", where this is zero) to `retry_rotated`: up to two more full detections plus swap attempts. In temporal mode the frame was already detected by the pre-pass (upright, roll-aware ROI, rescue ladder). Detector traffic after the pre-pass sealed the plan, "Retry rotated" on: **Love 221 retries / 179 `get_all_faces`; Monica 217 / 10; Weeds 50 / 120; angle_inverted 95 / 175.** Swapped-frame counts with the retry on vs off were identical on all five clips measured (Love 28, Monica 91, Weeds 251, angle 333, angle_inverted 290), so it recovered nothing.
  *Built.* `TrackingMixin._prepass_scanned(frame_idx)` (the test `swap_faces` already applies before trusting the cache: temporal mode, a cache, frame below `_temporal_covered`) and a branch in `process_frame` that returns the untouched frame instead of calling `retry_rotated` for such frames, printing `[Stage3] "Retry rotated" is skipped...` once. Frames past the scan, temporal mode off and every other no-face policy are untouched. After, re-run on Love, Monica, Weeds and angle_inverted: retries and Stage 3 `get_all_faces` 0; swapped frames/faces identical (28/28, 91/95, 251/262, 290/301). `test_stage3_retry_skip.py` 8 (drives the real `process_frame`; 3 fail when the skip is disabled).
  *Teardown, measured (Love 240f, VRAM free).* Enhancers load first (`processors-ready`, 6.31 GB free), the pre-pass drops it to 2.36 GB, `release_face_analyser_aux` brings it to 3.85 GB and `shrink_analysis_pools(1)` to 5.06 GB before main processing. The brief's ordering (release before loading the heavy engines) is not how it is built - the engines are resident during the pre-pass - but free VRAM never goes below 2.2 GB on the 4070. Reordering initialisation would be a large change with no measured defect here; the RTX 3060 (6 GB) is where it could matter and is unmeasured. Not done.
  *Precision and geometry, measured (`tests/probe_stage3_geometry.py`).* Every cached array is float32, detected and gap-filled alike (bbox, kps, landmark_2d_106, landmark_3d_68, pose, embedding): 232 + 34 faces on 1280x720, 78 + 12 on a 9:16 crop (404x720). Against a fresh `get_all_faces` on the same frame the cached landmarks sit 0.75-0.92 px away on average (max 3.6-8.4 px; kps on the 9:16 crop max 28 px on one face, mean 2.8 - not an axis swap, which would be hundreds); nothing lies outside the frame. `test_stage3_cache_precision.py` pins float32 and the straight-line position of gap-filled faces on 1280x720, 404x720 and 720x720. The occlusion/anti-carving mask intersection (`affa102`, `e66d777`) lives in the swap-time compositor and is untouched here; it has its own tests (`tests/test_interacting_faces_and_occlusions.py`).
  *Checked:* new tests green (`test_stage3_retry_skip` 8, `test_stage3_cache_precision` 3) and the four-clip re-measurement above. **Gates, completed after the first commit** (two single-process full runs were killed by memory pressure, so the suite ran in 8 batches): everything passes except `test_ui_zoom_pan` (pre-existing) and `tests/test_performance_regression.py::test_end_to_end_fidelity_throughput_and_av_timing[image]` (face SSIM 0.99028 vs a 0.995 floor, LPIPS 0.00581 vs 0.005) which is PRE-EXISTING - identical numbers on `01ddb48`, before Stage 2; a bulk run deselects it. A flaky `test_aux_canary` failure did not repeat. Regression benchmark PASS (300/300, face SSIM 0.9906). Details in `RECODE_STATUS.md`. *Not done:* the plan-fed `swap_faces` parameter and any plan-driven frame skip (measured to regress); reordering engine load after the pre-pass; RTX 3060; the timing effect of the retry skip (not counterbalanced, so no fps claim).

- **"Stage 2: active-range pre-pass, GOP-safe seeking, track-lock flush, entry recovery, frame plan" brief: the premise is mostly wrong (the code is `procmgr_tracking.py`, not `ProcessMgr.py`; the lock flush and the entry recovery already exist and are now pinned by tests that fail without them), but the seeking half hid a REAL defect - range seeks land ONE FRAME LATE on variable-frame-rate sources - and the brief's fix would not have caught it. Fixed differently; the validator and the plan are built, the plan as a read-only index.**
  *Seeking, measured (`tests/probe_gop_seek_stage2.py`, `docs/perf/stage2_seek/*.json`; frame identity = 32x18 luma thumbnail against a sequential decode of the same reader, and for the test fixtures the frame number drawn into the picture).* Constant-frame-rate H.264/HEVC, four real clips and two synthetic, `cv2` and the ffmpeg pipe: `cap.set(POS_FRAMES)` landed on the right frame **160/160** (real) and 72/72 (synthetic) at 10-30 ms (cv2) / 70-88 ms (pipe) whatever the distance; walking there costs the gap (cv2 ~0.1 ms/frame, pipe ~3 ms/frame) and is cheaper only below ~100 frames (cv2) / ~25 (pipe) - **at the brief's 250 it is 2x (cv2) and 10x (pipe) slower**, and Stage 1 coalesces ranges closer than 45 frames, so that regime barely occurs. Variable-frame-rate H.264 (timestamp jumps + jitter): **both readers land +1 frame late on 30-35% of seeks** (40 random targets: cv2 14 late, pipe 12 late, every miss exactly +1, `docs/perf/stage2_seek/vfr_landing_offsets.txt`; 3 of 8 ranges on the frame-numbered fixture), because the seek is a TIME, `(frame - 0.5) / fps`. A walk that starts from a seek inherits the error, so "seek if gap > 250, else grab" is not exact either. The brief's landing check cannot see it: `cap.get(POS_FRAMES)` echoes the requested frame on cv2 (0 error on every trial) and `FFmpegVideoReader.get` returned a constant 0.0 - "step forward while actual < a_i" would have decoded `a_i` frames on the pipe reader. (My first probe run reported sequential misses; that was a sampler bug - a target index smaller than the gap parked on a negative frame - found by decoding which frame each arm really landed on, and fixed before any number above.)
  *Built.* `roop/range_seek.py`: `source_is_cfr` reads every packet pts once per file (cached by path/mtime/size) and `timestamps_on_grid` asks whether a time seek can be exact (every pts within 0.2 of a frame period of `pts0 + k/fps`); `RangeSeeker` tracks its own position (it never asks the capture), seeks on CFR and positions by DECODING on VFR (exact by construction - a frame's index is how many frames precede it, the render pass's definition). Wired into the three range loops of `procmgr_tracking.py` (`_read_loop`, `_sync_read_gen`, `_precompute_stabilized_kps`); the scan prints `[Track] positioned N active range(s): S seek(s), W walk(s) over F frame(s)`. `FFmpegVideoReader` now reports a true `get(POS_FRAMES)` and has `grab()`. `ROOP_RANGE_SEEK_SEQ_GAP` (default 0 = always seek on CFR) is the walk threshold if a machine measures otherwise. Only the `sparse_auto_range` path seeks, so this is inert while that setting is OFF.
  *The track-lock asks, with sites.* **Flush on a gap: exists, stricter than asked.** `procmgr_tracking.py` `idx > prev_idx + 1` marks ANY skipped stretch a shot boundary, and `_consume` then retires every live track and clears `vel`, `prev_bbox` and the flow/occlusion chains (embeddings kept, so the same person is re-acquired on appearance at the tighter `REID_MAX`). Track ages (`first_seen`/`last_seen`) and `_track_assignments` keys are true clip indices. `tests/test_stage2_range_tracking.py` drives the real `_precompute_tracks` with `_active_ranges`: a stranger in the same box gets its own track and no source at gaps of 1, 15, 16, 40 and 200 frames; the same person on the far side of the frame is one track; a moving face's velocity does not cross the gap. Mutation-checked: with the gap-as-cut line removed the stranger inherits at gaps 1 and 15 (the existing `STALE = 15` retirement covers only 16+). The relaxed forward-face lock floor (`b1ed764`) is a SWAP-time floor on a face whose track the pre-pass already bound; it cannot reach across a range because no track does.
  *Backward entry pass: exists as `_stitch_tracks`.* An entry whose appearance has collapsed (distance 0.85 from the target) followed by the target in the same box is chained and gets the target's source on all six entry frames (test). **Open finding, not fixed:** the same chain gives the target's source to a STRANGER (orthogonal embedding, distance 1.0) in the same box immediately before the target, and to one 113 px away - the appearance veto `_TRACK_STITCH_EMB = 1.05` is above the distance between unrelated faces, so geometry alone decides. The brief's `cos >= tau` rule would refuse the stranger but would also refuse the 0.85 profile entry; the right bar needs a measured distribution on real footage (`AGENTS.md`: five gates were implemented and reverted for skipping that), so it is recorded here and in `RECODE_STATUS.md`, not changed.
  *`roop/face_sanity.py`.* One validator at the end of `face_util._enrich_detected_faces` (the exit shared by `get_all_faces`, the rescue chain, `get_all_faces_hires`, and so the ROI detector): `det_score` None/NaN/inf -> 0.0; an unusable `bbox` or `kps` DROPS the face; unusable `landmark_2d_106`/`landmark_3d_68`/`pose`/`embedding` become None; everything counted and printed once as `[FaceSanity]`. Reproduced first: a face with `embedding = None` raised `TypeError` out of the whole pre-pass (the temporal pre-pass lost for the clip); a NaN or zero embedding left a one-frame NO-SOURCE fragment where the target was. The tracker now leaves an embedding-less detection out of association and reports the count. InsightFace's `normed_embedding` is a read-only property of `embedding` (setting it raises); the validator only touches a stored one. Behaviour change worth knowing: a `det_score` of None used to pass the bystander filter and the lock floor, 0.0 does not - only for a face the detector itself returned without a score, which it never does.
  *`stage2_frame_plan` (`roop/stage2_plan.py`), as a read-only index.* `{frame: [VerifiedTargetFace(frame, track, source, det_score, bbox, pose, synthetic)]}` for frames with a face matched to a source, built in `_precompute_temporal` and reported as `[Stage2] plan: ...` with an outside-the-mask / past-the-scan check. It deliberately holds no Face and no landmark arrays: the swap pass already hands over `_temporal_faces` + `_track_source_map` and evicts faces as it consumes them, and a plan that referenced them would pin all of them (~5.8 KB per face: 106x2 + 68x3 float32 landmarks, two 512-float embeddings; ~580 MB per 100k face-frames, against a project that holds the 3060 under 2.5 GB RSS). Not done: making the swap pass skip frames absent from the plan - its per-frame fallbacks (nearest-centroid re-association, the 0.75 distance fallback) can still swap a face whose track was left without a source, so a skip changes swap rate and needs the regression benchmark.
  *Checked:* `test_range_seek.py` 26 (timestamp grid, policy against a recording fake, real CFR and VFR files, the pipe reader's position; the two VFR tests fail with the old always-seek behaviour, off by exactly one frame), `test_stage2_range_tracking.py` 9, `test_stage2_plan.py` 14, `test_face_sanity.py` 17; the 285 existing tracking/temporal/sparse/NVDEC tests; full suite 6091 passed / 3 failed (one pre-existing `test_ui_zoom_pan`; the exception-visibility ratchet caught two bare handlers of mine, now routed through `_swallowed`; affected files rerun green, whole suite not re-run after that); regression benchmark PASS (300/300, face SSIM 0.9906, unchanged); end-to-end Love 240f with `sparse_auto_range` ON executes `[Track] positioned 1 active range(s): 1 seek(s), 0 walk(s)` and the `[Stage2]` line (details in `RECODE_STATUS.md`). *Not done:* RTX 3060; multi-range seeking on a real swap (the Love scan found one range); a VFR clip with a real face (the VFR proof is on frame-numbered fixtures, not on a swap); the stitch over-claim above.

- **"Stage 1: sparse auto-range pre-scan and multi-worker detector pool hardening" brief: the pre-scan (`c01d767`, `3ec3906`) is rebuilt around ONE sequential demuxer, an fps-derived stride with cut/midpoint inspection, relaxed target-aware gates and dilated, coalesced ranges. Measured on six real clips against dense production detection it missed 0 frames in both modes (one clip: 2 "misses" that are a rock, viewed) where the old scan missed up to 13; building it exposed three recall holes the brief did not name, all fixed. `sparse_auto_range` is still OFF by default (owner's call, unchanged).**
  *What was wrong, with sites (old `sparse_range_scan.py`).* The reader seeked every sample (`CAP_PROP_POS_FRAMES`) in a thread of its own; its bounded `Queue(16)` was defeated by the consumer, which submitted every frame to an UNBOUNDED executor queue (every sampled frame of a long clip held in RAM at once - read, not measured). The fan-out was `pool*2` threads against a pool that only leases `pool` instances. Its stride was ~1 s (`max(15, round(fps))` frames), so an appearance shorter than that was stepped over (the misses in the table). The caller read `roop.globals.distance_threshold` rather than the swap's own `options.face_distance_threshold`; `api.py:4623/5059` set both from the same payload, so that is a coupling, not a measured defect (checked: it is NOT why targeted mode misbehaved). Samples ran the full `get_all_faces` (landmarks, 3-D 68, rescue ladder) and the first detections happened INSIDE worker threads.
  *Built (`roop/sparse_range_scan.py`, `face_util.py`, `procmgr_batch.py`).* **Barrier:** `FaceAnalyserProbe.prepare()` runs on the calling thread before any worker exists; `face_util.prescan_pool_size()` builds the pool, which runs the engine-hash-cached aux canary synchronously (`face_util._guard_aux_models`, called from `_build_face_analyser`), then one warm pass per pool instance. `cudnn_algo.probe` is only reached for `SUSPECT_MODEL_KEYS` (enhancers) via `precision_policy.py:744`, never on the detector path (`test_stage1_never_reaches_the_cudnn_probe`), so `core.py`, `trt_session_builder.py` and `ProcessMgr.py` needed no change. Workers = analyser pool size. **Detection only:** `detect_faces_for_prescan` runs the detector (`aux=False`) and, in targeted mode, the recognition model alone; no landmark_2d_106 / 3d_68 / genderage. The models stay LOADED - narrowing `g_desired_face_analysis` would rebuild the pool the pre-pass and the render lease next (twice), so latency drops but VRAM does not. **Demuxer:** one thread, `grab()` every frame, `retrieve()` the sample grid, one initial seek to `frame_start` (frame-exact on three H.264 files, mean |diff| 0.0), `Queue(maxsize=2 x workers)`, stop-aware puts so a dead consumer cannot wedge it. **Stride** `S = max(1, min(12, floor(0.20 fps)))`; **cut test** 64x36 luma, mean abs diff > 28.0 between consecutive samples inspects the midpoint `t - floor(S/2)`. A sequential decoder cannot go back for a frame, so every interval's midpoint is `retrieve()`d and held one deep (a deviation from "retrieve only on sampled indices": +0.3 s per 1200 frames at 720p, hidden behind detection). **Gates:** universal `det_score >= max(0.25, det_thresh - 0.15)`; targeted max cosine on w600k embeddings `>= max(0.15, tau_swap - 0.12)` with `tau_swap = 1 - face_distance_threshold`. **Ranges:** islands of positives, each padded `M = ceil(1.25 S) + 2`, ranges <= 45 frames apart coalesced. **Empty:** a CLEAN scan with no positive returns `[]` and an all-False mask (the whole clip passes through); an unclean one (stop, demux error, seek mismatch, detector/read errors with nothing found, nothing decoded) fails SAFE to the full range, as does a stopped or crashed scan even with partial positives.
  *Three recall holes found by measuring (`tests/probe_sparse_scan_stage1.py`, ground truth = production `get_all_faces` on every frame; "missed" = a truth frame outside the mask = rendered UNSWAPPED).* (1) **Inverted heads, targeted mode: 193 of 268 frames missed** - the 5 keypoints of a 180-degree head fit the ArcFace template badly (embedding cosine 0.023 against its own upright reading); fixed by embedding a face past `FACE_ROLL_LOWER` on the turned frame, reusing `face_down_axis` / `_action_for_down_axis` as `_upright_remeasure` does. (2) **Sideways close-ups: 3 of 381 missed** (a 700 px face the upright pass cannot see; only the cw/acw turns find it); the three cardinal turns are now tried. (3) **The rescue never ran when it mattered:** the relaxed 0.35 floor lets a junk 0.42 detection through, so the frame is non-empty and a rescue-on-empty rule (production's, at its 0.5) stays off while the real inverted face (0.93) is one turn away - frames 299-300 of `angle_inverted`. The turns are therefore tried when the frame is NOT POSITIVE, not when it is empty. Small-face / close-up / padded / CLAHE rescues are not run: on these clips they only produced a rock in a corner (Love f34, f39) and the back of a head (angle f281-285, which the ladder reports as a 1200 px face), both viewed.
  *Measured (RTX 4070, `retinaface_r50` + TensorRT, `face_detector_threshold` 0.5, `max_face_distance` 0.75; targeted = the biggest face of an early frame is the target).*

  | clip (frames, S) | universal: missed new / old, skipped new | targeted: missed new / old, skipped new | scan s new / old (univ / targ) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | angle (381, 6) | 0 / 0, 0.0% | 0 / 0, 16.5% | 1.1 / 0.4, 1.2 / 0.3 |
  | angle_inverted (381, 6) | 0 / 0, 0.0% | 0 / 0, 16.5% | 1.1 / 0.6, 1.8 / 0.6 |
  | angle rotated 90 (381, 6) | 0 / 0, 0.0% | 0 / 0, 16.5% | 1.0 / 0.5, 1.8 / 0.5 |
  | Love (1200, 4) | 2 (the rock) / 0, 3.4% | 0 / 11, 21.9% | 2.0 / 1.1, 4.9 / 1.0 |
  | Weeds (1770, 4) | 0 / 13, 13.2% | 0 / 13, 30.6% | 4.3 / 1.7, 7.0 / 1.5 |
  | Monica (2400, 6) | 0 / 0, 0.0% | 0 / 0, 71.1% | 2.9 / 1.8, 13.2 / 1.7 |

  The old scan was run with the same 0.75 distance. Its misses are the 1 s stride stepping over short appearances (Love 11, Weeds 13 target frames lost), and it skips MORE than the new one on Monica (77% vs 71%) because it looks at 5-6x fewer frames. On the three `angle` clips its 0 comes with a ~1 s dilation each side (7.6% skipped), not better detection. The new scan costs more (denser sampling, the turns on every non-positive sample: Monica 13 s for 2400 frames is the crowd-of-strangers worst case) and still skips 71% of that render. Raw numbers: `docs/perf/sparse_scan_stage1/*.json`. Decode of the 1200-frame Love window: grab only 0.16 s, + grid retrieve 0.41 s, + midpoints 0.74 s.
  *Checked:* `app/tests/test_sparse_range_scan.py` 48 (stride/pad/gates/ranges, demuxer driven by a recording fake capture - no seek, retrieve set = grid + midpoints, midpoint index, bounded queue, barrier ordering on thread names, fail-safes, a real MJPG file); the full suite and the end-to-end render are recorded in `RECODE_STATUS.md`. *Not done / open:* RTX 3060 (TensorRT not admitted, pool of 1 -> one worker; unmeasured); the scan does not honour pause; sub-S flashes that return to the same picture by the next sample are invisible to any sampling scheme (the cut test sees a CHANGE between samples, not a flash inside one interval); a face at 0.35-0.5 detector score that production's 0.5 would never swap still opens a range (only speed is lost); AdaFace-driven identity uses a different distance scale than the w600k gate here.

## 2026-10-19

- **"Stage 19: React UI target/bystander pinning controls and multi-face/object-crossing E2E stress suite" brief: per-face role badges + a one-click "Exclude (never swap)" are built, and they exposed three REAL bugs; the three synthetic scenes are built and graded; the pipeline meets the identity half of the brief and misses its alignment/pixel-exact thresholds (kept as strict xfails). The Z-order override toggle and the Stage 16/17 live dispatch were NOT built: there is nothing for them to drive.**
  *Premises, with sites.* `verify_integration.py` and `e02e2af` did not exist (created / absent). The "Stage 16 BystanderExclusionBank" veto is `ROOP_BYSTANDER_VETO`, default OFF because it was measured harmful (drops turned-away target frames); "Stage 17 ZDepthPriority" was never built (Z-depth isolation measured, no defect that size) and the weighted alignment lost end to end (`ROOP_FLOW_ALIGN` OFF). A Z-order toggle serialised into the checkpoint would therefore change nothing a render does, so it was not added; the same for a timeline keyframe lock-out. What already exists and is what the UI now drives: the per-person source mapping (`faceMapping` -> `target_person_source_mapping`, saved by `api._save_target_mapping_from_payload` into the target context, live without a restart).
  *UI (`react-ui/src/components/faceswap/faceRoles.js`, `InteractivePreview.jsx`, `FaceSwap.jsx`).* Every detected box on the Media Canvas now carries `data-face-role` = target / swapped / bystander, a chip saying so, and (for a captured person who is not the pinned target) an "Exclude (never swap)" / "Include again" button that writes an explicit `null` for that person. Clicking a box still pins it as the target. `faceRole()` shares `stableTargetSourceMapping` with the job payload, so the badge cannot say "bystander" for a face the job swaps (checked for every person in every mode, `face-roles-check.mjs`, 16 checks, mutation-checked). Only `FaceSwap.jsx` renders detected boxes (`InteractivePreview`); #/extras has none, so the brief's #/extras badge has nothing to attach to. The Face Manager control is the Person Groups dropdown, which now makes the same `excludePerson` call.
  *Three real bugs.* (1) **Skip did not skip.** `PersonGroups.setMapping` implemented "Ignore / Skip" by DELETING the person's key; in "Selected people" and "All faces" an absent key falls back to the rank default, so the skipped person was still swapped (`faceMapping {A: srcA}`, B deleted, 'Selected people' -> `[0, 1]`; with `B: null` -> `[0, -1]`). Fixed with `excludePerson`. (2) **Backend ignored an exclusion of the only mapped person**: `_save_target_mapping_from_payload` replaced the canonical mapping only `if stable:`, so `{B: null}` left B's old binding (`api.py`, `test_target_exclusion_mapping.py`, failed before / passes after). Needs a backend restart. (3) `Number(null)` is 0: a box with no person id was read as person 0 (hardened; `face-roles.spec.js` uses the backend's real convention, ids past the captured count).
  *Scenes (`app/tests/synthetic_scenes.py`, `occlusion_interaction.py`, `occlusion_gates.py`; `verify_integration.py`; `tests/test_occlusion_interaction.py`).* Drawn faces are not detected, so the faces are the app's own faceset photos feathered onto a seeded background; every sprite box and visible mask is known exactly. Renders go through `two_face_video.py` (realswap + RealityUX, tracking, 20 threads) with `ROOP_BENCH_CODEC=libx264 ROOP_BENCH_CRF=0` and the new `ROOP_BENCH_NO_TRIM=1` (the harness's `trim()` re-encoded the clip with lossy cv2 mp4v, which alone changes every pixel). The "did the bystander change" control is the same clip with the target sprite left out and the same pinned target: nothing can be swapped, so the pipeline passes every pixel through.
  *Measured (RTX 4070, 30 frames, sprite IoU peak 0.753).* Crossing, bystander in front: the target is hidden on 6/30 frames (the detector cannot see a face > ~55% covered); the 24 detected are all swapped; the bystander is swapped on 0 frames and its pixels are byte-identical to the control (MSE exactly 0). Target in front: target swapped 30/30, bystander swapped 0 frames, a faint halo on the bystander (max 5 levels, <= 42 px/frame over 8 frames, MSE 0.30). Bar over eyes/nose/mouth: the swap crop (`estimate_norm` of the 5 keypoints, vs the same frames without the bar) drifts up to **30.4 px / 33.0 deg** while the bar crosses the face (mean 5.1 px / 4.5 deg), because the detector's keypoints themselves move; swapped-skin second-order luma derivative 1.21 vs 0.54 without the bar; skin LAB dE vs the no-bar render mean 0.39, max 0.91. The brief's thresholds (0.75 px, 0.4 deg, dE 0.8, bystander MSE strictly 0.0 in both orderings) are met for "bystander in front" MSE and unmet elsewhere; unmet ones are `xfail(strict=True)` with the measured reason in `KNOWN_MISSES`, and 15 ratchets hold today's values.
  *Checked:* `face-roles.spec.js` 4/4 (Playwright), `face-roles-check.mjs` 16/16, `test_face_roles_js.py`, `test_target_exclusion_mapping.py` 3, `tests/test_occlusion_interaction.py`, `verify_integration.py`, `verify_pipeline.py` (see RECODE_STATUS for the exit codes). *Not done:* Z-order toggle, timeline lock-out windows, and any wiring to a Stage 16/17 parameter (see premises); the alignment gap is the open lead (Stage 17 tried weighting, reconstruction and flow and each lost on clean frames).

- **"Stage 18: flow-warped mask hysteresis, guided edge matting, pre-enhancer occluder shielding" brief: a REAL macro mask-switching defect found on a real hand-over-face stretch, but the brief's fusion did not improve the rendered output (end-to-end it was worse), so it ships OFF; shielding and the Lab freeze have no measured defect. Production behaviour unchanged.**
  *Premises, with sites.* Already present: a flow-warped mask EMA (`roop/occlusion_mask.py` `TemporalMaskSmoother`, global residual reset), the production mask stabilizer `one_euro.MaskStabilizer` (`ProcessMgr.py:1210`) with its own optional flow warp (`mask_flow_warp`, **false** in `config.yaml:78`) and an opt-in fast-restore policy, a guided-filter edge refinement (`procmgr_masking.py:246`, setting `mask_guided_filter`, off), an opt-in `TemporalOcclusionEngine`. The occlusion mask is applied AFTER the enhancer (`_composite_mask(_img_mask, aligned_img, enhanced_frame)`, `ProcessMgr.py` ~6210), i.e. the brief's final "restore the real object on top" already happens. What does not exist: per-pixel flow-consistency weighting, dual-threshold hysteresis, and any shielding of the enhancer's INPUT (`enh_input` is the swap crop, hand included, `ProcessMgr.py` ~6345).
  *The defect, measured on the production masks* (`tests/capture_masks_stage18.py`, single-thread render of Weeds 960-1170, the woman the only target, a hand over her mouth; masks recorded in the aligned crop, where the alignment already removes the face's motion). **22 of 191 frame-to-frame transitions (11.5%) are macro mask switches (swap-region IoU < 0.6)**, concentrated in the contact frames 132-157; the occluder-chain mask's swap area swings 13% -> 53% -> 29% -> 44% between consecutive frames (the occluder model alternately reads the skin-coloured hand as face and as occluder, so the swap lands on the hand on some frames and not others). The stabilizer in the capture raises the semi-transparent share of the swap area from 7.4% (raw) to 19%. Edge chatter by threshold crossing is a minor part (2.0% of the uncertain band).
  *The brief's fusion, built and tested* (`roop/mask_fusion.py`: `FlowHysteresisFuser` = per-pixel flow-consistency fusion + soft Schmitt ramp 0.60/0.38, `guided_matte`, `FusionMaskStabilizer` as a drop-in `MaskStabilizer`; 14 unit tests, each with its control). **Offline on the captured sequence it looked good:** macro switches 22 -> 12, area jitter 6.1% -> 3.8%, semi-transparent share 19% -> 7.1%, chatter 2.01% -> 1.43% (no parameter setting went below ~1.2-1.4%), at a cost of 0.3-0.4% ghost/lag pixels. **But that offline stand-in did not reproduce the production stabilizer (max mask difference 0.42; production applies it inside `process_mask` as well), so the offline win was not trustworthy - and end to end it reversed:** same clip, same flags, `ROOP_MASK_FUSION=1`: frame-to-frame change of the swapped area 1.93% -> 2.53%, macro jumps 1 -> 6, changed area +1.5 points (guided variant 2.44% / 6); in every 40-frame segment including the quiet ones. Viewed frame by frame (146-151) the fusion is not visibly better and shows faint hard-edged patches. **`ROOP_MASK_FUSION` (`1` / `guided`) is OFF by default.**
  *Enhancer and colour, measured and NOT built.* UltraMax vs no enhancer, same flags (`tests/probe_mask_flicker_stage18.py`; its noise floor is high - a plate-only edge map flips 9-13% under the same flow compensation): edge chatter 18.65% vs 18.46% (the enhancer is not the source), edge boil +0.33 grey levels (2.69 vs 2.36). Lab flash in the hand window: L excess mean 0.87, max 1.99 (UltraMax); the one big outlier (+19 L) is frame 15, before the hand arrives.
