No measured occluder-driven colour flash (same conclusion as the Stage 10 entry), so the occlusion-clamped Lab freeze is not built; pre-enhancer shielding is not built because no phantom-feature boiling at the object boundary was measured beyond that +0.33.
  *Checked:* `test_mask_fusion.py` 14 (x3 stable). One real design weakness the tests exposed and a bound fixed: dense flow can "explain" a patch that is simply different, keeping the old occlusion alive (`max_flow_frac`). *Not done:* a second clip (Weeds is the only footage with a real hand over the target's face and one captured person); the 3060; a way to read the production stabilizer's true input (so an offline A/B can be trusted); the occluder model's skin-coloured-hand confusion itself, which is the root of the area swings and which no temporal filter in this stage addresses.

- **"Stage 17: visibility-weighted affine alignment and 3D Z-depth non-target isolation" brief: the alignment defect is REAL and measured (an occluder drags the swap crop 2-34 px at 256), but every fix built here either loses to doing nothing or regresses clean frames; the Z-depth half has no defect of that size. Shipped as measured libraries and an OFF-by-default flow-alignment switch; production behaviour is unchanged.**
  *Premises, with sites.* Production builds M with plain `face_util.estimate_norm` (unweighted similarity of the 5 keypoints) in `face_analyser.canonicalize_face_alignment` (called `ProcessMgr.py:5487`). A weighted Umeyama exists (`face_analyser.py:236`, `geometric_alignment.py:441`) but nothing in the swap path calls it - so the brief's gap is real. For isolation: `face_overlap.build_regions` already gives SDF territories with a size-based depth bias (`DEPTH_BIAS`) and treats an unswapped bystander as already painted; `Mask_RealityUX._non_target_keep_mask` protects the neighbour's padded box through M (c72e97a). `enable_xseg_occlusion` is `false` in the live config, so no XSeg occlusion probability exists before alignment either.
  *Alignment under occlusion, measured* (`tests/probe_alignment_stage17.py`: real frames, REAL detector run on the clean and on the occluded frame, a skin-coloured occluder pasted over an eye / the mouth / half the face; truth = the clean detection's matrix; error in 256-px-crop px). Production's plain solve: eye 1.7-7.5, mouth 5.1-8.4, half 22-23 (s3/s6/s7). **Weighting the keypoints alone (the brief's w_k, oracle P_occ) is WORSE than doing nothing** (eye 7.5 -> 10.8, mouth 5.1 -> 12.6): the detector's occluded points are not systematically wrong, and with 1-2 of 5 points near zero weight the fit is badly conditioned. Rebuilding occluded keypoints from the prior frame through the visible dense landmarks: mouth 5.1 -> 4.5, eye 7.5 -> 7.7, s7 eye 1.7 -> 3.5, half worse; the dense landmarks shift with the occlusion too. A kinematic clamp helped only half-face on s7. **Optical flow from the last clean frame over the visible pixels carries the keypoints to 0.3-1.2 px** (single occluded frame, oracle mask) and, in `probe_alignment_chain_stage17.py` (occluder slides in over K frames), **needs no mask** (`flow_nm` = `flow`): s6 K=3: 2.2 -> 0.7 eye, 8.4 -> 0.7 mouth, 21.8 -> 0.8 half; but s7 eye is worse than the detector (2.4 -> 5.7).
  *Triggers, measured.* A detector-vs-flow DISAGREEMENT gate is unusable: on CLEAN frames flow disagrees with a good detection by > 0.05 x IOD in 23-81% of s5/s7 chains (p99 1.7-2.5 IOD), so it cannot tell a hijacked detector from wrong flow. The flow chain's own `survival` (fraction of the anchor cloud still rigid) works in the probe: clean median 0.84-1.00, < 0.7 in 0-4.3%; occluded median 0.23-0.71; flow where survival < 0.7 beats the detector in 17 of 18 cells (s7 K=8 eye the exception).
  *Production wiring (`ROOP_FLOW_ALIGN`, a second persistent chain per track in the pre-pass scan loop; `flow_coast.chain_policy`) then FAILED the end-to-end check* (`tests/probe_align_e2e_stage17.py`: clean clip vs the same clip with sliding occluders in known windows, through the real `_precompute_temporal`). Inside the windows s6 6.39 -> 4.92 px (eye 2.5 -> 1.6, mouth 6.6 -> 3.2, half none), s7 6.69 -> 6.92; **clean frames outside them s6 0.04 -> 1.19, s7 1.06 -> 3.08** (30 / 33 replaced). The first version (24-frame chains) replaced 99 of 156 clean s6 frames: a long chain loses points to blur/rotation until clean frames read as covered, and points lost under an occluder never come back when it leaves. Capping chains at the 8 frames the probe validated helped but did not remove it. **Default OFF** (`ROOP_FLOW_ALIGN=1` enables); `roop/visibility_alignment.py` (weights, weighted Umeyama, LMedS consensus, reconstruction, `KinematicClamp`; 18 tests incl. controls) has NO production caller.
  *Z-depth isolation, measured and NOT built* (`tests/probe_isolation_stage17.py`: Weeds 960-1170, the woman the only target, real production render; changed = mean-channel |out - plate| > threshold). In the unambiguous region (bystander hull minus the target's hull) swapped pixels average 0.0-0.7% for box IoU < 0.3 and 1.1% (thr 12) / 6.2% (thr 6) at IoU >= 0.3. The worst frames (16-45%) were viewed: the "bystander" hull sits ON THE WOMAN'S FACE (the junction detection of two touching heads, see Stage 16), the red change is her swapped face where it belongs, and on the man's real face there is a hairline along the contact seam and nothing else. Z-depth needs the bystander's own face detected, which is exactly what fails in a kiss; no signal here can be validated without ground truth for "who is in front". Not a measured defect of the size the brief assumes.
  *Checked:* `test_visibility_alignment.py` 18, `test_flow_coast.py` 34 (policy, survival, high-resolution, bridge), the survival/chain probes; the scan-loop ON path is covered by `probe_align_e2e_stage17.py` only (no unit test drives it); full suite below. *Not done:* RTX 3060; real (non-synthetic) occluder footage - every occluder here is pasted; SAM 2 / XSeg probability not used (not enabled, and the oracle arm already loses); heavy contact remains a recall problem.

- **"Stage 16: bipartite target-ID lock, bystander negative-exclusion bank, optical-flow coasting" brief: premises mostly wrong (the named file is not the live pre-pass; Hungarian + Kalman coasting already shipped), but two real gaps found by measurement and closed (the composite association cost, flow-bridged gap fill) and one feature MEASURED AND SHIPPED OFF (the bystander veto removed real target frames on real footage).**
  *Premises, with sites.* `app/roop/optimized_prepass.py` (`FacePrepass`) has no production caller (Stage 11); the live pre-pass is `procmgr_tracking._precompute_tracks._consume`. **Already existing:** Hungarian assignment at `procmgr_tracking.py` `_consume` ((1-IoU)+cosine), `tracker.py` `FaceTracker.update` (IoU+ArcFace), `temporal_state_machine.py:581`, `identity_manager.py` (module-level swapper path); Kalman coasting `tracker.py` `coast` + `_coast_track_gaps`; track hold with a looser gate `selected_routing.py:224-244` (`test_selected_track_hold.py`). **Did not exist:** any bystander bank (`grep bystander` = comments only); any face optical flow in the render path (`live_mode.py` only; DIS flow is for masks).
  *Differential margin census* (`tests/probe_bystander_stage16.py`; person A = target, B = bystander, truth = identity-clustered on clean frames carried across a contact run by x-order). d2/d3 are near-static (adjacent frames cos 0.97) and measure nothing; d4 margins are ±0.65. **Love, crop contamination >= 0.2 (n = 75 target / 154 bystander): margin 0.12 -> false-bind 0.0%, false-refuse 6.7%** (margin 0.00: 0.6% / 4.0%). Past 0.4 contamination (n = 6) the margin collapses (median -0.28). On CLEAN Love frames the true target clears the 0.42 lock only 79% and the 0.12 margin 85%, so the margin applied per frame would refuse ~15% of clean target frames - it needs the hysteresis it was built with.
  *Association replay* (`tests/probe_assoc_stage16.py`, the pre-pass `_consume` replayed on recorded detections): wrong-person assignment on Love at contamination 0.35-0.5 **4.42% -> 0.00%**, at >= 0.5 **22.70% -> 2.45%**; clean bands unchanged; Weeds 0 either way; d4 contact windows 0 of 1,848 detections assigned differently. Ablation: the **keypoint term does it** (kpt-only reproduces it; identity-only 14.7%, GIoU-only no change); pose is neutral. Viewed on pixels (Weeds f999/f1155/f1167): legacy hands the woman's box to the man's track. Not perfect: Weeds f1143 still gives her a track the man later takes.
  *Built.* `roop/association_cost.py` (`legacy_cost` lifted unchanged; `composite_cost`; default `ROOP_TRACK_COST=composite`, `legacy` restores); `roop/flow_coast.py` (pyramidal LK on a face-scale-normalised crop, forward-backward + patch-error + competing-motion + closing-anchor guards; `ROOP_FLOW_COAST=0`); scan-loop chains + `_flow_bridge_geometry` in `procmgr_tracking.py` (flow supplies the shape of the path, the closing detection pins its end; also fills holes up to `ROOP_FLOW_MAX_GAP`+1 = 13 frames, past `gap_max`, ONLY when verified); `roop/bystander_bank.py` (banks, gate, hysteresis `TrackLedger`) and the veto in `_assign_track_sources` (**default OFF**, `ROOP_BYSTANDER_VETO=1` enables; see below).
  *Flow, measured.* Isolated (`probe_flow_stage16.py`, ground truth = detector on undropped frames): flow+bridge is never materially worse than the Hermite fill and on fast footage far better (Love, 10-frame hole, clean: 21.1% -> 1.9% NME); anchorless flow LOSES to Kalman under a stationary occluder, so it is not used without a closing detection. **85% occlusion: flow refuses 70-100% of trials - the brief's 85% case is NOT met.** An early run reported flow failing on 4K clips because the benchmark downscaled to 960 px; native 4K needed the scale normalisation now in `FlowCoaster`. End to end through the real pre-pass (`probe_flow_e2e_stage16.py`, 23 dropout windows on Love): mean NME **14.54 -> 9.69 (6-frame holes), 19.86 -> 12.80 (10-frame)**; s6 (slow head) flow = interpolation within +-0.1. Pre-pass fps flow on/off, A B B A, d4 700 frames: 43.9 vs 44.6 (-1.6%), outputs identical.
  *Checked:* `pytest` of the new files (bank 23, cost 16, flow 23, gap-fill 12, veto 7) and 121 existing tracking tests; the closing-anchor guard mutation-checked; `test_exception_visibility` (4 silent handlers caught and narrowed); regression benchmark **PASS** (decided 300/300, changed 300/300, face SSIM 0.9906 vs golden).
  *The bystander veto, run on real footage (`probe_bystander_veto_stage16.py`: one person captured, veto on vs off through the real `_precompute_temporal`) - NEGATIVE, default OFF.* d1 / d2 / d5: 0 fires, identical bindings. d3 / d6: auto-capture timed out (90 s budget), not measured. **Love: veto fired on 1 track, 35 target face-frames lost (13 -> 12 bound tracks). Weeds: 1 track, 39 frames lost (8 -> 7).** Rendering the vetoed tracks (`veto_inspect`): both were the REAL TARGET turned away (Weeds f206: the woman, hair and ear only, man in frame as the bystander; Love f1172: the man in dark profile), bound at a weak 0.71 against the 0.75 gate. A turned-away head's embedding is noise and a relative comparison of two noisy readings is noise; the margin census above covered frontal/contaminated faces, not these. The veto is therefore OFF by default; do not enable it without a pose-aware evidence rule and a run that loses 0 target frames on Love and Weeds.
  *Not done / unverified.* `TrackLedger` (0.42/0.22 hysteresis with flow consistency) is a tested library with NO production caller - the existing track hold (`selected_routing.py:224-244`) already keeps a bound track through weak frames at a looser gate. 3060 unmeasured. Heavy contact is mostly a recall problem (the detector often returns ONE box on the junction of two heads); nothing here fixes recall.

- **`benchmark_pipeline.py` no longer defaults to `D:\k1.mp4`: it resolves its clip like the other harnesses, and reports PENDING (exit 2) instead of dying or substituting.**
  *Defect.* The root-level `benchmark_pipeline.py` wrote `r"D:\k1.mp4"` in its docstring, `BenchmarkSuite.__init__` and `--video`. There is no such drive on the 3060 laptop, so the documented command died; on the 4070 it silently depended on one machine's layout. `test_fixture_paths.py` could not see it: it scans only `app/tests/*.py` and only for `X:\pinokio` literals.
  *Fix.* `app/tests/fixtures.py` gains `resolve_target(arg, default)` beside `clip()`. The default is now the NAME `k1.mp4` (`DEFAULT_CLIP`), searched in `$ROOP_CLIP_ROOT`, then `<PINOKIO_HOME>/roop-keep` / `roop keep`, then the same beside the launcher; `--video` takes a name to search for or a path. Two rules kept apart on purpose: a named clip is searched (flat or in a category folder); a path the operator typed is used exactly as given, and if it is missing the run FAILS rather than falling back to a same-named clip under the root, which would run a different workload under the chosen name (the substitution `fixtures.fingerprint` documents). The clip is resolved first in `BenchmarkSuite.__init__`, before the model registry is built; `main()` turns `FileNotFoundError` into a `[PENDING]` message listing every root searched, on stderr, exit code 2 (the regression benchmark's "could not run" convention), with no results file written.
  *Checked by running it:* default on this machine (`k1.mp4` is not here) -> `[PENDING] ... Searched: G:\pinokio\roop-keep, G:\pinokio\roop keep, ...`, exit 2, `benchmark_results.json` untouched; `--video X:\nowhere\d4.mp4` -> "an explicit absolute path is used as given and is not searched for", exit 2; `BenchmarkSuite("double/d4.mp4")` -> resolved to `G:\pinokio\roop-keep\double\d4.mp4`, probe OK (1280x720, 13,305 frames).
  *Tests:* `app/tests/test_benchmark_clip_resolution.py`, 11 tests: the resolver (flat, category folder, explicit path wins, missing explicit path does not fall back, unfindable clip raises naming the roots and `ROOP_CLIP_ROOT`, never returns another clip), the construction order and exit code, and a static scan of the benchmark harnesses that live outside `app/tests` (`benchmark_pipeline.py`, `tests/benchmark_pipeline.py`, `run_stage0_benchmark.py`, `pinokio_batch_runner.py`, `verify_pipeline.py`) for any drive-lettered string literal. Mutation-checked: with the original `benchmark_pipeline.py` swapped back in, 4 of the 11 fail and the scan reports exactly its two `D:\k1.mp4` literals; with the fix, 11 pass; `test_fixture_paths.py` still passes.
  *Not fixed (found while scanning, outside the ask):* `tools/acceptance_real_clip.py:78` enumerates `Path("D:/")` for its target clip - the same class of defect. *Still not run on this machine:* `k1.mp4` is not in any clip root here, so the benchmark matrix itself is PENDING until it is placed in `roop-keep` or `ROOP_CLIP_ROOT` is set; `--video` with any available clip runs it, but a different clip is a different baseline.

- **"Stage 15: 60 FPS zero-lag React workstation (OffscreenCanvas), binary frame streaming, PERFORMANCE_BASELINE.md lock" brief: one real fix (the 3.5x Lens no longer re-renders the stage 58 times a second), one new guard (`frame-budget.spec.js`), and the rest measured as already built or not worth building. The numbers it asks for are only partly recorded: the rows that were not run are named.**
  *Already built, so not rebuilt.* Binary transport: `GET /api/target/preview` (JPEG/WebP bytes with a weak ETag over file identity + every encoding parameter, `app/api.py:2555-2679`), `preview_grid` and `preview_seq` (length-prefixed octet-stream, no Base64), `GET /api/live_frame` (`image/jpeg`). Off-thread decode: `decoder.worker.js` + `createImageBitmap`, `usePlaybackBuffer.js` (ArrayBuffers, not blob URLs), `FastCanvasPlayer.jsx` + `fastPlayer.worker.js` (an `OffscreenCanvas` transferred to a worker, rAF on the worker, the page only hands over ArrayBuffers), `frameSocket`. Transient telemetry: `store/telemetryStore.js` keeps per-tick fields OUTSIDE React state and `LiveText` / `LiveBar` / `LiveValue` subscribe through `subscribeThrottled` at `UI_HZ = 10` (the file's header describes the exact thrash the brief names as already fixed). Brush and timeline pointer work is already rAF-coalesced.
  *Base64: one endpoint left, and it is cheap.* Only `POST /api/preview` (the swapped one-frame preview, `{"image": dataURL, faces, kps, pose}`) still returns Base64, JPEG q95 (`api_media._bgr_to_preview_dataurl`). In Chromium: JSON.parse 0.1 / 0.2 ms; the app's decode (`atob` + byte loop + Blob, `objectUrls.dataUrlToOwnedBlobUrl`) 0.7 / 3.0 ms; the binary alternative (bytes -> Blob) 0.4 / 1.1 ms, for a 1080p (~0.28 MB, 365 KB as JSON) and a 4K (~1.1 MB, 1,432 KB as JSON) frame. Binary would save ~0.3 ms (1080p) / ~1.9 ms (4K), once per user-triggered preview, never per playback frame; loopback absorbs the 33% size. Far below one frame, for an API contract change. Not built.
  *Measured* (`react-ui/e2e/frame-budget.spec.js`, new; headless Chromium with software rasterisation on the production build against the mock server, so a MAIN-THREAD jank budget, not a GPU frame-rate claim; rAF deltas, `longtask`, React commits through a stub DevTools hook, and Chromium's own task / script / layout / style durations): all 9 tabs idle 59.6-59.9 fps, 0% of frames over 20 ms, 0 long tasks, 1.7-2.5 commits/s; a running job (mock 4 Hz telemetry) 59.4-59.8 fps, <= 0.4% over 20 ms, 4.2-4.7 commits/s - the feed costs ~2 extra commits/s, i.e. the transient-subscription design works.
  *The fix: the 3.5x Lens.* `InteractivePreview.handlePointerMove` called `setLensPos` on every pointermove (two `getBoundingClientRect` reads and a React render of a 1,373-line component) and positioned the glass with `left`/`top`. 600-pointermove flood vs a no-tool control (2.2 commits/s, 0% frames over 20 ms): 58-59 commits/s. Now the handler records the geometry in a ref and one `requestAnimationFrame` per frame writes `transform` (and the image size only when the picture's box changes) straight to the two elements; React renders the glass once when the tool is switched on. Counterbalanced A/B, original vs new, source state asserted before every run, 600 pointermoves, main-thread ms (control 2,350): commits 58.6 / 59.3 -> 2.1 / 2.1; script 326 / 327 -> 170 / 170 ms; layout 84 / 84 -> 6 / 6 ms; task 4,012 / 3,981 -> 3,333 / 3,328 ms (the lens's own added work -40%). `will-change: transform` was tried and dropped (no measurable difference, avoids a 2240x1260 GPU layer). Behaviour change: after the tool is toggled off and on the glass stays hidden until the first pointermove (it used to reappear at its last position). Correctness: the existing `canvas-tools.spec.js` lens test (sample point within 0.004 of the picture at 100% and at 2.5x stage zoom) and the other 6 canvas-tool tests pass.
  *Not fixed, measured.* **Frame-drop share is too noisy to rank anything here:** the same original code scored 4.1%, 5.0% and 12.1% of frames over 20 ms in three runs, so "the lens drops fewer frames" is NOT claimed - the stable signals are main-thread time and commits. **Brush:** 600 pointermoves = 10-12% of frames over 20 ms against 0% for the control, but only ~45 ms of the extra ~485 ms of main-thread task time is script (~75 us per event) - software canvas rasterisation in headless, which a main-thread change does not move; not rewritten. **Timeline drag:** ~94 React commits/s (about 1.6 per frame) of the panel that owns the playhead while frames stay at 59 fps against a trivial mock; real previews are not in this measurement. Left open.
  *A measurement that measured nothing, caught.* The first timeline run reported 59.7 fps and 2.5 commits/s and looked healthy; the playhead had not moved (frame 1 -> 1) because the track sits at y = 1742, below the 720 px viewport, so the mouse events landed nowhere. The scenario now scrolls the track into view and FAILS if the frame number does not change.
  *Guard.* `frame-budget.spec.js` asserts ceilings taken from the measured numbers: idle <= 5% frames over 20 ms, 0 long tasks, <= 6 commits/s; job running the same with <= 10 commits/s; lens <= 10 commits/s (the original fails it at 58.8; the new code passes at 2.1). The brush and the timeline are reported, not asserted.
  *Gate run (RTX 4070 box):* `npm run lint` 0; `lint:unreachable` 0 of 122; `lint:api-timeouts` OK; build OK; `npm run test:e2e` **96 passed, 38 skipped** (the opt-in theme runs; was 80 passed before this stage's 16 new tests), axe clean on every tab; `npm run test:render-checks` pass (13 s); `verify_pipeline.py` exit 0 (h264 / yuv420p, HTTP 206). **Not run:** `benchmark_pipeline.py` (defaults to `D:\k1.mp4`, absent here, a drive letter in a tool; a one-frame preview loop over swapper x mask, no enhancer arms; its `sam2_tracked` arm needs the checkpoint that was never downloaded), the Pixel-Boost / SAM 2 / GPEN Realistic / RF++ per-model end-to-end rows, and `verify_integration.py` (does not exist). `PERFORMANCE_BASELINE.md` ("Final verified numbers") and `OPTIMIZATION_PLAN.md` carry what WAS measured and name what was not. Rebuild the UI (`npm run build` in `react-ui`) for the lens change to take effect; `react-ui/dist` is gitignored. RTX 3060: not measured.
  *Open (owner):* the brush's per-event cost on a real GPU-backed browser, and the timeline drag's 94 commits/s with real previews (both need a headed run); (`benchmark_pipeline.py`'s `D:\k1.mp4` default was fixed right after this stage; see the entry directly above.)

- **"Stage 14: SAM 2 memory ring-pruning and ROI-gated enhancer dispatch (RF++, UltraMax, GPEN Realistic)" brief: NO product code changed. SAM 2's attention window is already 6+1 and its cost is flat; the named wrapper never calls SAM 2; the small-face gate exists, and turning it on buys +11% on small-face footage and nothing on most clips - the default is left for you to decide.**
  *SAM 2 cost does not grow with the clip* (`app/tests/probe_sam2_scaling_stage14.py`: the app's own `Mask_SAM2.precompute` over 600 real 1280x720 frames, tiny model built with no checkpoint - compute, memory and the attention window do not depend on the weights, so no download was needed and the masks are meaningless): 47.2 -> 49.1 ms/frame (last window / first = **1.04**), CUDA allocated 0.47 GB flat (peak 0.68 GB). The model already attends to `num_maskmem: 7` = the prompt frame + the 6 previous (`sam2_base.py:28`), which is the brief's "6 + 1 anchor". **What does grow, linearly, is host RAM:** `SAM2FrameBuffer` holds the whole trimmed clip as float32 at 1024x1024 = **12.6 MB/frame** (7.55 GB for 600 frames, allocated up front from `frame_count`, `Mask_SAM2.py:53-69`) plus ~1 MB/frame of tracker state in `non_cond_frame_outputs` (RSS 9.35 -> 9.86 GB over 500 frames; back to 2.30 GB after). That is ~13.6 MB/frame: ~136 GB for 10,000 frames and roughly 2,000 frames on a 31.7 GB machine (extrapolated, not run). Not built: SAM 2 is not selected here (`mask_engine: RealityUX`), its checkpoint was never downloaded and no log shows it running, and a windowed pre-pass (re-seeding each window from the previous mask) cannot be validated for seam quality without real weights. Cheaper options if long SAM 2 clips matter: store the buffer as uint8 and normalise on fetch (4x less RAM, bit-identical by construction), or window the pass.
  *"SAM 2 trigger on XSeg entropy / 4% disagreement": not built.* The production SAM 2 is a whole-clip, sequential, frame-0-seeded pre-pass (`procmgr_tracking.py:144`), not a per-crop refiner; skipping frames breaks its memory chain.
  *`face_engine.pipeline.hybrid_masker.HybridVideoMasker` - the "SAM 2 occlusion wrapper" - never runs SAM 2.* `step()` builds the "SAM 2 face hull mask" with `cv2.ellipse` from landmarks (`generate_hull_mask`); `_sam2_predictor` is constructed in `initialize()` (only if the checkpoint exists) and not touched again; `reset_stream` calls `reset_state(None)` inside `except: pass`; the "strict 16-frame FIFO window" is a `deque` of frame INDICES. Nothing outside `face_engine/tests/test_modern_stages.py` calls the class. Pinned by `app/tests/test_stage14_hybrid_masker_sam2_unused.py` (3 tests: zero predictor calls over 40 steps, mask identical with or without a predictor, FIFO holds ints only) - it fails the day `step()` starts using the predictor. There is no memory bank in it to prune.
  *Small-face enhancer gate: exists, off by default.* `roop/enhance_gate.py` (shorter box side, hysteresis x1.15), setting `enhance_min_face_px` (default 0; React "Skip restorer below face size (px)", env `ROOP_ENHANCE_MIN_FACE_PX`), wired at `ProcessMgr.py:6229` with a per-render summary line. Census of the roster clips (`probe_enhancer_face_sizes_stage14.py`, frames 0-600 every 10th, 1,087 faces): **13 of 17 clips have no face under 128 px**; share under 80 / 100 / 128 px (shorter side): Love 45.5 / 49.5 / 50.5%, Monica Bellucci 42.8 / 51.0 / 69.0%, b1 17.1 / 30.3 / 32.9%, d4 1.4 / 4.3 / 5.7%; all faces 16.5% under 128 (15.5% on max side <= 128, the brief's definition).
  *A/B on Love, first 600 frames, live config (UltraMax / realswap / RealityUX, 20 threads), `ab_enhancer_gate_stage14.py`, A B B A in one process, gate 0 vs 128:* **9.40 -> 10.45 fps = +11.2%** (A 9.56 / 9.24, B 10.48 / 10.43). The gate's own counters prove it ran: restorer skipped on **124 of 343 faces (36.2%)** / 123 of 343, smallest face 43 px. Swap rate identical in all four runs (322 swaps, 34.7%); the two A renders are pixel-identical to each other (mean abs diff 0.0000 - not the 0.71/255 floor `AGENTS.md` quotes for other configs). A vs B: whole-frame mean abs diff 0.61/255, 542 of 600 frames hold a pixel > 8 off (lossy HEVC spreads a changed block's rate-control to neighbours, so this overstates the face change); isolating the connected change cluster (414 frames, median 137 px across) the mean abs diff inside it is 1.5 / 1.5 / 2.2 (p10 / p50 / p90) and the Laplacian-variance ratio B/A is 0.80 / 1.00 / 1.02 - most faces are indistinguishable, the worst tenth lose ~20% fine-detail energy. A side-by-side sheet is in the session scratchpad only. Caveats: one clip; look judged on three examples; the faceset (`akansha`) is not the person in the clip, which does not affect timing or the swap-rate guard. The fps divides 600 by render time while the render processed 459 frames; both arms alike, so the ratio stands and the absolute does not.
  *Why the default was NOT flipped.* `config.yaml` already stores `enhance_min_face_px: 0`, and `Settings.save()` writes defaults byte-identically to user values, so changing the default in `settings.py` would not reach this install or any that has saved (the "derived default that outlives its rule" trap in `AGENTS.md`); reaching them needs the provenance-stamp mechanism `_THREAD_RULE` uses. It is also a look change on the 3 of 17 clips that have small faces and a no-op on the other 13. The control already exists in the UI: setting it to 128 reproduces the A/B. This is also the one lever that recoups the RF++ FP32 pin (`PRECISION_POLICY.md:154`, owner instruction, +82% enhance-stage cost, 21.63 -> 39.31 ms/face); `7a15446` itself is the FP16-vs-FP32 island study, not the pin.
  *Batched multi-face enhancer: already measured, not repeated.* RestoreFormer++ is flat at 24.4 / 25.6 / 25.2 / 25.0 ms/face for B = 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 (`tools/bench_restorer_batch.py`, 10-03); UltraMax's CodeFormer fp16 is 4-6% SLOWER batched on the shipped profile (25.6 -> 26.8 ms/face) and -12% only at exactly B=2 on an opt=2 engine that is 6.6% slower at B=1 (`tools/bench_codeformer_batch.py`, 10-13). The dynamic profile the brief asks for already ships for UltraMax (`1x3x512x512 .. 8x3x512x512`, `PRECISION_POLICY.md:191`). **GPEN Realistic was not probed.** No 4x4 Pixel-Boost applies at the default `realswap` 256 (Stage 9).
  *Verified:* `test_stage14_hybrid_masker_sam2_unused.py` 3 passed. Full suite not run (no product code changed). RTX 3060: not measured. Added `probe_sam2_scaling_stage14.py`, `probe_enhancer_face_sizes_stage14.py`, `ab_enhancer_gate_stage14.py`.
  *Open (owner):* set `enhance_min_face_px` to 128 if the small-face look is acceptable (+11% on Love/Monica/b1-type footage), or ask for a default flip with a provenance stamp so it reaches saved configs; uint8 / windowed SAM 2 buffer if long SAM 2 clips matter; GPEN Realistic batching probe.

- **"Stage 13: pinned-memory FFmpeg zero-copy pipes, NVDEC/NVENC hardware queues, multi-GPU GOP worker scaling" brief: NO product code changed. The pinned-buffer premise does not describe this pipeline, the no-face passthrough already exists (now pinned by a test), and the vendor-codec and multi-GPU asks cannot be verified on this machine.**
  *Where the frame goes.* Production decode (main pass) is `video_stream.open_video_capture` -> `video_stream.NVHardwareVideoReader` (`-hwaccel cuda ... -pix_fmt bgr24`, pipe `bufsize` = 2 frames, `readinto` into a NumPy frame array via `_read_into`, `video_stream.py:210`); the tracking pre-pass uses `nvdec_reader.FFmpegVideoReader` (pooled pre-sized buffers, prefetch depth 2 on a >= 7 GB card, `nvdec_reader.py:162-236`). Both already `readinto`; there is no per-frame `bytes` copy. The frame then stays a HOST array through cv2/NumPy and goes back out through a host pipe; the only device transfers are the 256-512 px crops ONNX Runtime stages itself. A full-frame pinned H2D/D2H copy stream has nothing to carry. Pinned-ring machinery exists where a device boundary does (`hardware_streamer.py`, used only by the dormant `vectorized_pipeline`; Stage 3's `ZeroCopyBufferPool` for enhancers). **Do not create `app/roop/core/`**: `core.py` is a module and a `roop/core/` package shadowed it once (`import roop.core` broke, fixed `5609800`).
  *Measured* (`app/tests/probe_media_io_stage13.py`, RTX 4070, 300 frames, rounds 2-3 of 3, ms mean/p50/p99): **pinned vs pageable** - pipe `readinto` 1080p 6.4 vs 6.3 ms, 4K (26.5 MB/frame) 166 vs 166 ms (ffmpeg-side, unchanged; the two 4K rounds disagree on sign); host->device copy 1080p 0.34 vs 0.28 ms, 4K 1.46 vs 1.13 ms - **0.06-0.33 ms per frame, on a copy the pipeline does not make at frame size.** **Decode** via the production reader, consumer idle / paced at the 70 ms/frame a stabilised render consumes: 1080p 5.6 ms (177 fps) / 5.3 ms wait; 4K 57 ms (17 fps) / 56 ms wait. **Encode** `FFMPEG_VideoWriter.write_frame` (`hevc_nvenc`): 1080p 2.4 ms idle / 0.8 ms paced; 4K 9.2 ms idle / 2.3 ms paced. The brief's 1.5 ms line is met by paced 1080p encode only. These are reader/writer-thread pipe latencies (the main-pass reader is synchronous and its wait is the pipe copy at ~1.2 GB/s at 1080p, ~0.5 GB/s at 4K, not NVDEC); a worker waits only if that thread cannot keep up, and its capacity (177 fps at 1080p, 17 fps at 4K) is above what a render consumes (13.6 fps at 1080p in the 600-frame regression render). That render's monitor still printed `decode-starved 27%` (input queue empty in 27% of samples) on the stabiliser's chunked queues; I did not isolate the cause, so I am not calling decode healthy or starved - open. Unexplained: the pinned probe's own 4K pipe read (163 ms) is 3x slower than the production reader's (56 ms) on the same file; its ffmpeg command differs (`-hwaccel cuda` without `-noautorotate -vsync 0`), and I did not chase it.
  *FFmpeg flags.* NVENC already runs `-rc vbr -cq <crf> -preset p5 (video_stream.py: p4) -tune hq` (`ffmpeg_writer.py:250-258`, `video_stream.py:521-632`); NVDEC is `-hwaccel cuda` (equivalent to `h264_cuvid`/`hevc_cuvid` for this purpose, which the repo does not use); the final-render audio is already a stream copy (`util_ffmpeg.restore_audio`); `-c:a aac` appears only in the writers' `audiofile=` path. `-thread_queue_size 512` was NOT tested. AMD/Apple: `ffmpeg_writer.py:164` knows `*_amf` only as a software-fallback key and the repo has no VideoToolbox; this machine has one NVIDIA GPU, so AMF/VideoToolbox cannot be verified here and nothing was built.
  *No-face passthrough: exists.* `ProcessMgr.swap_faces` (~3743-3752): with the pre-pass cache covering a frame, a missing entry means nobody was there, so no detector, matcher, swapper, mask or enhancer runs and the frame is returned. `app/tests/test_stage13_noface_passthrough.py` drives it (2 tests; a covered range of 0 makes the same call trip the 'stage ran' guard; past the covered range detection still runs).
  *Distributed render.* `distributed_render.py` already cuts at keyframe/GOP boundaries (`plan_chunks`, ~10 s) and every GPU worker pulls the next chunk from a shared queue (`tasks.get_nowait()`, `:303-342`) - it is not an equal static split. Ordering that queue by pre-pass face count would only shorten the tail on a multi-GPU box; this machine has one GPU, so not built and not measurable here.
  *Benchmark.* `benchmark_pipeline.py` has no decode/encode-wait metric, and the regression benchmark's "raw decode fps" comes from `benchmark/regression.py` `FrameReader` (software decode, `stdout.read`), not the production reader - quote neither as NVDEC. Added `app/tests/probe_media_io_stage13.py` (decode / encode / pinned) and `test_stage13_noface_passthrough.py`. Full suite not run (no product code changed). RTX 3060: not measured.
  *Open (owner):* the cause of `decode-starved 27%` on the stabilised path (needs queue-wait timing inside `_run_stab_parallel`); a multi-GPU run of `distributed_render` with face-count-ordered chunks.

- **"Stage 12: multi-stream CUDA graph execution, pure-GPU affine warping, zero host-sync (`optimized_processor.py`)" brief: NO product code changed. The classes it targets have no production caller; the fork-join overlap is real but production already takes it, and the GPU ports were measured or rejected before.**
  *Premise.* `GpuFaceSwapProcessor` (`optimized_processor.py:2213`) and `MemoryStreamingProcessor` (`:2405`) are instantiated nowhere in `app/roop`, `app/tests`, `tests` or `tools`; `vectorized_pipeline.py` shares `CudaAffineBatch` / `CudaFrameBridge`. What `optimized_processor` does ship in production is the Stage 3 `ZeroCopyBufferPool`, `VramGovernor` and `release_zero_copy_pools`. The per-face loop that runs is `ProcessMgr` on 20 worker threads (`alignment` `:5482`, `swap` `:6004`, `mask` `:6179`, `enhance` `:6334`, `blend` `:6890`), each leasing its own TensorRT context. `ROOP_STAB_STREAMING=1` (the "single CUDA owner" the brief builds on) is the one-thread path measured 3.88 vs 13.38 fps on 09-24.
  *Fork-join XSeg ∥ swap, measured* (`app/tests/probe_stream_overlap_stage12.py`, RTX 4070, real `realswap` + fp32 TRT XSeg, 6 s arms, rounds 2-3, spread ~3%): one pair at a time serial 62.3 → fork-join 72.2 pairs/s (**+15.9%**, overlap efficiency 1.46 of 2.0 - the device does overlap them). With several frames in flight: plain frame workers T=2/3/4/6 = 82.9 / 96.1 / 100.5 / 105.9 pairs/s; each worker forking its swap and XSeg (2T ops in flight) T=2/3/4 = 85.0 / 99.0 / 102.7 (**+2.2-3.0%, inside the spread**). The brief's best case (72) is below plain T=3 (96). Production had 6.2 frames in flight in the render below. Not built.
  *Where the time is* (regression render, live config incl. UltraMax, 600 frames, 20 threads, `ROOP_PROFILE=1 ROOP_PROFILE_DETAIL=1`): 7.74 fps end to end (77.5 s wall), timed render phase 13.58 fps, GPU util mean 48% / peak 93%. Summed thread time: `frame_total` 276 s; `enhance` 99.8 ms/call (11.0%), `swap` 97.0 (10.7%), `stabilize` 12.1 (8.0%), `mask` 35.2 (7.7%), `blend` 37.1 (4.1%), `lighting` 7.2 (1.6%), `alignment` 2.9 (0.3%). The cv2/NumPy compositing stages (`blend` + `lighting` + `alignment`) are 33.9 s = 12.3% of `frame_total`: an upper bound for every GPU port in the brief, and thread time is not a speedup budget (`AGENTS.md`).
  *GPU ports, already decided.* `warpAffine` -> `grid_sample`: `CudaAffineBatch` (`optimized_processor.py:1732`) exists and is dormant; resampling cannot match OpenCV's fixed-point warp (3 grey levels) and global TF32 makes `affine_grid` far worse; a June 2026 pre-reset `gpu_math.py` port was benchmarked 1.1-40x slower per call and reverted. `dilate`/`erode` -> `max_pool2d`: a square vs the shipped `MORPH_ELLIPSE`, error up to 1.0 per pixel (`tools/bench_mask_gpu_morphology.py`); the real cost was the full-frame call, already fixed bit-identically by `mask_roi.py`. CUDA graph around compositing: the compositor is NumPy/host (decode arrives and encode leaves as host pipes), and the one graph this repo captured (GPEN 256 Pro filter) was 23% slower (`CUDA_EXECUTION_POLICY.md`). `phase6_cuda_graph_ab.py` covers the provider-level graph.
  *"cv2 forces cudaStreamSynchronize": not demonstrable.* OpenCV's CPU ops act on host arrays and touch no CUDA stream; the syncs are the device-to-host copies at model outputs. `ROOP_PROFILE_DETAIL` cannot show them: `gpu_event_ms` covers the PyTorch stream only (ORT/TRT run on their own) so each stage's CPU and GPU-event times come out equal, and ORT transfers are reported opaque. An `nsys` capture is the missing instrument.
  *Verified:* `test_cuda_affine_batch.py` + `test_mask_roi.py` 150 passed; `test_zero_copy_buffer_pool.py` + `test_enhancer_zero_copy.py` + `test_runtime_optimizer.py` 76 passed. Full suite not run (no product code changed). Added `app/tests/probe_stream_overlap_stage12.py`; `CUDA_EXECUTION_POLICY.md` gains a "Stage 12" section. The regression benchmark recorded a new baseline for the 600-frame clip under `.roop/regression/nvidia-geforce-rtx-4070_9d7be205ba/` (gitignored; the older baselines are untouched). RTX 3060: not measured.
  *Open (owner):* an `nsys` trace of a real render is the only way to see the host-sync stalls the brief assumes; the one-owner path would gain ~16% from a fork-join if it were ever made the default, but it is 3.4x behind the threaded path.

- **"Stage 11: hyper-fast pre-pass (`optimized_prepass.py`), batched NVDEC downscaling, adaptive keyframe striding" brief: NO product code changed. The named file is not the production pre-pass, and none of the four asks can reach 2.5-4x on it; the one lossless lever measured is worth <= 8-10% on two of six clips.**
  *Wrong file.* `optimized_prepass.FacePrepass` is imported only by `optimized_processor.py:38` and `vectorized_pipeline.py:54` (grep over `*.py`, no other caller). The live "Analyzing faces" pass is `ProcessMgr._precompute_temporal` -> `procmgr_tracking._precompute_tracks` (`procmgr_tracking.py:200`) + `temporal_tracker.TemporalFaceTracker`. `benchmark_pipeline.py` has no pre-pass stage, so its 2.5-4x criterion cannot be run as written; `app/tests/probe_prepass_stage11.py --section baseline` drives the production path instead.
  *Baseline (RTX 4070, d4 `double/d4.mp4`, frames 0-600, `ROOP_PROFILE=1`, `retinaface_r50`, 3 repeats in one process).* 22.9 s / 26.2 fps (first arm: engine build) then **13.9 s / 43.2 fps and 12.5 s / 48.1 fps** (null spread ~10%); identical output every repeat (503 frames with a face, 740 faces, 705 detected). Schedule: **179 full, 421 ROI, 21 ROI-fallback-full, 0 coast**. STAGE TIMING, repeat 3: `track_detect` 24.09 s / 600 calls / 40.15 ms (40.8% of summed thread time), `track_wait` 10.61 s, `track_consume` 0.17 s, **`track_decode` 0.13 s = 0.21 ms per frame (0.2%)**. Counters: 1431 detector invocations for 600 frames; 736 recognition / 106 / 68 calls.
  *Ask 1, decode at detection resolution (scale_cuda / pinned bilinear): not built.* The pre-pass decode wait is 0.2% of thread time here (it was 11.5 ms/frame only on one grain-heavy clip, `prepass-decode-bound`, already fixed by readahead). The pass also keeps every Face it detects for the render (`collect_obs=True`), so the 106/68 landmarks and the 5-point kps would come from a downscaled frame - the opposite of "no loss of landmark precision". `NVHardwareVideoReader` already decodes on NVDEC (`nvdec_reader.py:250-285`).
  *Ask 2, adaptive stride + ROI propagation: ROI exists; coasting is a measured negative.* Full detect every 8 frames, union-ROI between, scene-cut forced full detect, velocity prediction: `temporal_tracker.plan` / `get_all_faces_in_roi` (`face_util.py:836`), tests `test_temporal_tracker.py`, `test_shot_boundaries.py`, `test_tracker_coasting.py`, `test_stage8_roi_derotation.py` (77 passed with the small-card tests). Coasting on a stride was measured 6.3% mean / 13.9% p95 of interocular error on 35-60 deg heads and it is why ROI cadence 2 went back to every frame (`ROOP_TEMPORAL_ROI_INTERVAL`) and `ROOP_TEMPORAL_STEP` stays opt-in. The "0 faces -> stride to every 3rd frame" half is new for the 4070 (the 3060 has it as `RescueBackoff`, `face_util.py:1818`, `procmgr_tracking.py` small-card branch): see below.
  *Ask 2b, the empty-frame rescue ladder, measured (`probe_prepass_stage11.py --section ladder`, 600 consecutive frames, plain pass vs full ladder per frame, then an offline replay of stride policies).* On d4 the 1431 detector invocations are 55% rescue passes (rotated 495, upscaled 143, CLAHE 132), so it looked like the lever. Replay: **with rewind (a plain-pass face ends the gap and the skipped frames get their ladder) it is lossless on all six clips (0 lost of 23 / 5 / 0 / 18 / 0 / 1 ladder-only frames) and costs 1.00x on d4, b1, Weeds, s1, s3 and 0.90-0.92x on Love** - faces reappear too often for a streak to build. The policies that do save (0.68-0.81x on d4 / Love / b1) lose faces: k=2 N=3 no rewind loses 5 of 23 (d4) and 5 of 18 (b1); k=2 N=8 loses 10 of 23 (d4). Not built: the lossless version buys <= ~10% on footage with long faceless stretches and nothing elsewhere, for a rewind inside the pipelined pool dispatch.
  *Ask 3, batched ArcFace / 2d106det / 1k3d68 across a micro-batch: built before and removed.* `aux_batch.py` (f11c8c2, removed e2b69f5, report `docs/perf/aux_batch_2026-10-05.md`): 1.36 faces per batch, per-face 61-67 fps unbatched vs 50 fps batched. Today's counters are the same shape (736 aux calls over 600 frames = 1.2 faces per frame, spread over 2 detector workers). Not rebuilt.
  *Ask 4, lazy ArcFace embeddings (track start / every 5th frame / pose delta > 10 deg): not built.* Ceiling from the documented in-pipeline cost (`face_util.py` `detect_boxes_in_roi` docstring: recognition 2.11 ms per face) x 736 calls = 1.55 s of 24.09 s detect thread time (~6.4%) - an upper bound, derived not re-timed - and the embedding is what Re-ID, the contamination check and the AdaFace cache read (`_cache_identity_embeddings`, `procmgr_tracking.py:403`). Adjacent-frame embedding cosine is 0.913 mean, so "carry the rank ID" needs the tracker to be right about identity, which is what the embedding is for.
  *Verdict on 2.5-4x.* Detect is 41% of thread time and the pass is detection-bound; the asks remove decode (0.2%), aux (<= ~16% at the very most and already measured slower batched), and ladder passes (<= 10% lossless). The rest requires not detecting, which is the measured-bad coasting. Added: `app/tests/probe_prepass_stage11.py` (baseline + ladder replay) and `app/tests/test_prepass_stage11_policy.py` (5 tests pinning the replay's accounting). Nothing to restart.
  *Open (owner):* if the pre-pass matters on a clip with long faceless stretches, `RescueBackoff` with rewind on the pooled path is a <= 10% lossless change; not worth the dispatch complexity on the clips measured.

- **"Stage 10: mask-weighted LAB colour transfer / working-tree hygiene / final verification gate" re-issue: the colour brief was measured and NOT built (its premise did not hold on real faces); the scratch-log hygiene defect was real and is fixed; the full verification gate passes.**
  *Premise check:* `a564e17` is the 10-14 changelog-only commit. `86ace34` is real: it committed `scratch_capture.log`, `scratch_eval.log`, `scratch_probe.log`, `scratch_tf32.log` at the repo root together with `app/tests/trt_matrix.py` and a `test_swap_batch_equivalence.py` edit. `verify_integration.py` does not exist (as in Stage 8).
  *Colour transfer - measured, NOT built.* `probe_skin_tone_transfer.py` (91 real single-face frames from Love / Weeds / Monica Bellucci / b1, `realswap`, CIE76 distance between the mean skin colour - BiSeNet skin+nose, eroded - of the output and of the plate, in the aligned crop): transfer off median **1.30** dE (p90 2.11, none above 4); production `lct` **1.80** (p90 2.42, none above 3); a skin-masked Reinhard candidate built offline 1.06 (p90 2.14) but with 2 faces above 4 dE (max 6.93). The just-noticeable difference is ~2.3. The brief's hypothesis - whole-crop statistics skewed by hair / background / glasses / hat - predicts the error rises with the non-skin share of the crop; the correlation is **-0.00** and `lct` leaves no face above 3 dE. Temporal pumping (mean |frame-to-frame change| of that error, 91 consecutive frames): off 0.27, `lct` 0.20, skin-masked per frame **0.68**, skin-masked + EMA 0.15 **0.44** - segmentation-driven statistics are noisier than whole-crop ones and the EMA does not bring them back. All of it is below the JND, so there is no colour defect to fix, and the design would add outliers and pumping. What the brief asks for mostly exists: skin-masked OKLab statistics (`compositing_engine.SkinPhotometricMatcher`, opt-in via `ROOP_COMPOSITING_ENGINE`, default off), a Laplacian multi-band blend in the default composite path (`temporal_compositing.composite_multiband`, called from `procmgr_masking.py`) and `TargetAppearanceStabilizer` for temporal appearance; a Poisson pass stays rejected (`temporal_compositing.py`). Side observation, NOT acted on (owner call): the shipped `lct` slightly RAISES skin-mean error versus transfer off (1.80 vs 1.30 median, both inside the JND) while removing a mean-lightness bias (+0.79 L on a 13-face sample) and costs two LAB passes per swapped face.
  *Hygiene - fixed.* `git rm --cached` the four root scratch logs (files stay on disk; history keeps them); `.gitignore` gains `/scratch_*` and `/*.log`, anchored to the root so the six `docs/perf/**/*.log` evidence logs the changelog cites stay trackable; `.githooks/pre-commit` now refuses staged `scratch_*` / root-level `*.log` (added or modified only, so deleting one is always allowed) before it regenerates the manifest, with a `ROOP_PRECOMMIT_GUARD_ONLY=1` test seam; `trt_matrix.py` and the test edit stay. The hook is opt-in per clone (`git config core.hooksPath .githooks`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`), so CI also asserts the result: `test_no_scratch_output_is_tracked_at_the_root` (git only, runs in the light profile). `app/tests/test_precommit_scratch_guard.py` (12) runs the real hook in a throwaway repo; mutation-checked (neutering the guard fails 3, dropping the ignore rule fails 1). `tools/gen_update_manifest.py --check` still passes - neither file is manifest-hashed.
  *Verification gate (2026-10-19, RTX 4070):* `pytest app/tests tests` **5807 passed, 16 skipped, 2 xfailed** (13 min; includes `test_standalone_install.py`, the Stage 7 RealityUX tests, the `roop.core` import guard, segment concatenation, the 8662077 gate tests and the Stage 6-9 additions of today). `python verify_pipeline.py` exit 0, no albumentations output. `npm --prefix react-ui run build` exit 0. `npm --prefix react-ui run test:e2e` exit 0: **80 passed, 38 skipped** in 5.8 min; the 38 are the opt-in per-theme contrast sweep, run separately (`npm --prefix react-ui run test:e2e:themes`): **38 passed**, 4.8 min. Axe: 12 route entries pass (the 9 top-level routes - home, faceswap, batch, processing, facemgr, extras, gallery, history, settings - plus 3 batch sub-routes) with an empty `axe` allowlist; `themeContrast` is 0 for every theme. The allowlist is not empty in full: `tabStops.faceswap` = 258 is a ceiling on a count, not a violation (kept 10-18). Both Playwright runs end with a vite / mock-server `ECONNRESET` stack after the last test (line 168 of 171) - the preview process being killed at teardown, exit code 0, not a mid-run crash.
  *Not run:* `face_engine/tests` (its own tree and compiled engines; CI runs it, not part of this gate), `benchmark_pipeline.py`, and the regression benchmark (no product code changed in this stage).

- **"Stage 9: swapper (RealSwap) / ArcFace frontal-pose gate / batched Pixel-Boost" re-issue: NO product code changed. The three asks were measured against real heads and each is either already present or makes things worse; the numbers and the probe that produced them are recorded.**
  *Premise check:* `4b67811` is the 10-14 changelog-only commit and `8662077` is the smudge fix (it exists). The gate it added lives in `ProcessMgr.py` / `procmgr_runtime.py` / `face_util.py`, not `optimized_processor.py` or `face_engine/`. `D:\out.mp4`, the one clip its thresholds were tuned on, is not on this machine, so the smudge frames could not be re-rendered and no new threshold could be validated against the failure.
  *Ask 1 - joint geometric pre-gate (|yaw| > 72 with a 58-72 roll-off, |pitch| > 55, convexity, IOD >= 12% of box width, score >= 0.55, XSeg skin >= 15%): measured and NOT built.* `probe_pose_gate_and_source_embedding.py --section gates` on the 36 real studio banks (180 faces, pose read the way the pipeline reads it): of the 71 real profile faces (|yaw| >= 72) **all 71 are refused** - 66 by the yaw cutoff alone, 63 by IOD < 12%, because a profile head's IOD / box width has median 0.070 (one eye is hidden; frontal is 0.472). None of the 109 faces under 58 degrees is refused by anything; pitch and score refuse nothing; the cross-product rule flags 2, both past 88. Those same plates are swapped well today: `tests/angle_bench.py --swap-model realswap --rolls 0` on two real pairs, +-90 plates (5-point yaw ~84): 10/10 detected, feature drift at most 0.087 interocular distances, identity to the source 0.64-0.76 against 0.67-0.79 frontal. A hard refusal in that range deletes good swaps - the "whole-face refusal IS flicker" lesson that already removed two such gates (09-21). XSeg skin visibility was not measured: it needs a per-face XSeg call on the swap path and the gates above already fail without it.
  *What already protects the two smudge causes (8662077), unchanged:* past `VERIFY_AWAY_YAW` (88 degrees, jaw-free, `procmgr_runtime.py:951`) the outcome guard `swap_moved_the_face` judges at 0.5 / 0.3 instead of 1.0 / 0.75 and discards the swap when the face moved (`ProcessMgr.py:7134`); a locked face under `LOCK_MIN_DET` 0.85 (`procmgr_runtime.py:970`, `ProcessMgr.py:4345`) goes to per-frame matching. Those measure the OUTCOME (does the swapped result still sit where the face was), which separates a smudge from a clean profile; a pose or landmark-geometry threshold cannot, because they read the same numbers. Its 10 tests are intact. Still open from 10-14: both thresholds were set on one clip; before widening either, render a second two-face clip.
  *Ask 2 - pose-adaptive ArcFace embedding (SLERP between the bracketing bank photos, L2 re-normalised): built as an experiment, measured, NOT shipped.* Real yaw-sweep plates, 60 swaps, `realswap`, scored with AdaFace (independent of the swapper's own embedding), leave-one-out so the photo at the target's pose is held out of every arm and is the reference: production mean 0.627, SLERP 0.603, nearest-pose 0.575. Paired: SLERP - mean -0.024 (19/60 wins), nearest - mean -0.052 (13/60), SLERP - nearest +0.028 (37/60). By target yaw SLERP is +0.016 at frontal, -0.010 at 45, -0.058 at 90 degrees. So interpolating by pose is better than hard nearest-pose, and worse than the centroid the product uses (`AverageEmbeddings` / the V2 stored centroid). V2 banks that HAVE the pose-matched photo still select it with hysteresis and cross-fade (`pose_source_selector.select_pose_aware_source`, `temporal_identity.propose_source`); this changes neither. An earlier run of this experiment showed three identical arms: the swapper caches its latent ON the source face (`_latent_<model>`, `FaceSwapInsightFace._compute_latent`), so mutating `face.embedding` is silently ignored after the first swap. The probe deletes it per arm; anything else that edits a source embedding in place must do the same.
  *Ask 3 - vectorised Pixel-Boost with a 16 px-overlap Hann fold: premise is wrong; the batched path already exists.* Pixel-Boost here is an INTERLEAVED subsample - `implode_pixel_boost` reshapes the aligned crop to `(model, total, model, total)` so every tile is the WHOLE face at 1/total resolution on a different pixel phase, and `explode_pixel_boost` interleaves them back (`procmgr_tiling.py:101`). There are no spatial tiles to overlap or window; swapping contiguous 128 px pieces of a face with one identity latent would not produce a face. The tiles already run as one batched call (`RunBatch`, bounded chunks, `ProcessMgr.py:6046-6090`; cross-frame `RunBatchMulti`) behind `ROOP_BATCH_SWAP` (`test_swap_batch_equivalence.py`). It is off by measurement: `inswapper_128` is exported with a STATIC `[1,3,128,128]` input so TensorRT cannot take `[1..16,3,128,128]` without a re-export, and a dynamic-batch swapper was measured slower (10-11). The configured swapper (`realswap`, 256 px native) has `subsample_total == 1`: no tiles, so nothing to vectorise on this machine's default path.
  *Reproduce:* `env/Scripts/python.exe tests/probe_pose_gate_and_source_embedding.py --section gates|source` (needs the `faceset-v1-backup-2026-09-03` banks under the clip root). Product code, tests and suite untouched; the suite was not re-run for a probe that is not collected.

- **"Stage 8: detection prepass / rotation-aware ROI / out-of-frame guards" re-issue: the 10-14 "everything exists" was half right. The rescue ladder, ROI pads and reflect padding existed; what was missing - a rolled track's ROI stood upright BEFORE the detector looks at it - is built and measured. The padded border rescue was measured and NOT shipped.**
  *Premise check:* `366a356` is the 10-14 changelog-only commit. `e02e2af`, `ba3cff7` and `verify_integration.py` do not exist in this repository (`git cat-file`, `--all`, `find`); `verify_pipeline.py` exists and is the codec / faststart / HTTP 206 check, unrelated to detection.
  *Finding that makes the rolled-face fix necessary:* the rescue ladder in `get_all_faces` runs only when the first pass returns NOTHING. On real frames rolled 180 degrees, 13 of 76 full-frame detections missed the face and **11 of those 13 were not empty**: the upright detector returned confident faces (score 0.78-1.0) that were not the face (IoU 0), so the rotated rescue never ran; calling `_rescue_rotated` directly found the real face in 11 of the 13 (IoU 0.54-0.96).
  *Built - rolled tracks get an upright ROI first:* `TemporalTrack.roll` / `roll_action` (`temporal_tracker.py`, `_note_roll`, `orientation.roll_from_face` + `action_for_roll` with its hysteresis band); `DetectionPlan.rotation_action` is set on an ROI plan only when EVERY live track agrees on one quarter turn; `face_util.get_all_faces_in_roi(rotation_action=)` tries the derotated crop first (`_roi_faces_derotated`: aux models run on the upright face, results un-rotated to frame coordinates) and falls through to the unchanged upright path if nothing comes back that rolls to the same quarter turn; `procmgr_tracking._precompute_tracks` passes `detect_plan.rotation_action` to the ROI detector. `ROOP_ROI_DEROTATE=0` restores the old order for an A/B.
  *Measured (real Love / Weeds / Monica Bellucci / b1 frames, retinaface_r50, RTX 4070):* single frame, 76 faces per roll, ROI path as it was -> derotated with the true roll: +90 69 -> 75, -90 67 -> 74, 180 70 -> 75 (oracle upper bound). Then the real `TemporalFaceTracker` over 150 consecutive frames per clip with the hint taken from its own history, counterbalanced off/on/off/on (880 ground-truth frames per cell): recall **-90: 89.5% -> 96.1%, +90: 91.4% -> 95.9%, 180: 82.7% -> 92.0%**, and 10-27% less wall time (fewer frames fall through the rescue ladder). Control without rolling: Love, Weeds, Monica get no hint at all; `b1` is hinted on 33 ROIs because its head really is rolled (-87 to -56 degrees in its first ~40 frames) and recalls 438/440 with and without.
  *Limit, stated:* the hint is validated by rolling the found face back and requiring the same quarter turn. That rejects a face with real sideways geometry (a wrong hint on a normal detector mostly returns nothing and falls back); it cannot reject a hallucinated upright-LAYOUT face, whose un-rotated roll equals the hint by construction (pinned by `test_hallucinated_upright_geometry_is_NOT_caught_by_the_roll_check`). Quarter turns only: +-60 degrees was already recalled 93-100% at 0.017 normalised keypoint error, so arbitrary-angle derotation has nothing to fix.
  *Out-of-frame guards - existed, now pinned:* `_roi_window` clips to the frame (never a negative origin or empty slice); `align_crop` fills with `BORDER_REPLICATE` (its comment records a 34% black wedge with the default border; nothing tested it - now `test_the_border_pixels_are_the_frame_edge_not_black`, mutation-checked); the multiscale detector reflect-101 pads 64 px (`face_detector.apply_context_padding`, `MIN_BORDER_PADDING_PX`); similarity alignment removes +-60 roll exactly and does not shear; pixels the alignment invented map outside the frame canvas on paste-back, so they cannot be written. `align_crop` was NOT switched to reflect: no measurement says the swap models do better with a mirrored border than a replicated one.
  *Measured and NOT shipped - a larger padded-frame detect for clipped faces:* with 40% of the face beyond each of the four edges (76 faces per edge, 304 cases), production recall is 271/304 (89%); 33 misses, 28 of them empty. A plain re-run recovers 0 of 33 (so it is not detector noise). A 25% padded detect recovers 11 (black border, what `_rescue_padded` does, skipped today for multiscale engines) or 14 (reflect; 13 of the 28 empty ones) - but fires on 9 / 13 of 78 frames that contain NO face, the worst kind of error for a swapper. Gated on "the face is >= 10% beyond the frame and score >= 0.8" it is 6 of 13 true recoveries at 0 / 78 false positives. That is ~2 points of clipped faces for one more full detect on every empty frame, and 0 / 78 only bounds the false-positive rate below ~4%. `app/tests/probe_edge_roll_recall.py --section edges` re-runs it.
  *Verification scripts:* there is no CI path that fails on warnings (`pytest.ini` has no `filterwarnings = error`, `ci.yml` no `-W error`), so albumentations' "new version available" UserWarning was noise, not an exit-code failure. It comes from an import-time update check that also makes a network call; the launchers already set `NO_ALBUMENTATIONS_UPDATE=1`, a bare `pytest` or `verify_pipeline.py` did not. Both now do (`conftest.py`, `verify_pipeline.py`). `python verify_pipeline.py` exits 0 with no albumentations output.
  *Not built:* 4K "dynamic ROI windowing" (the temporal tracker's union ROI is exactly that, pads 0.55 / 1.35); the brief's `R(-theta_roll)` for arbitrary angles (see above); `verify_integration.py` (does not exist, not invented).
  *Tests:* `app/tests/test_stage8_roi_derotation.py` (14; breaking the plan, the ROI pass or the pre-pass plumbing each fails tests - 4 / 3 / 1) and `app/tests/test_stage8_border_and_roll_geometry.py` (10: 40% clipped at all four edges, +-60 roll). Full suite `pytest app/tests tests`: 5795 passed, 16 skipped, 2 xfailed. `--benchmark-mode regression` PASS (6.42 fps; 6.58 / 6.63 earlier the same day, inside the machine's ~5% spread; face SSIM 0.9906, 300/300 swapped) - one upright face, so it does not exercise the hint. Backend restart needed.

- **"Stage 7: surgical mouth dynamics / non-target isolation / occlusion governance" audit of 9b18807 + c72e97a: the red tests, unwired settings and RealityUX pixel-space defects the brief names were already fixed on 10-14; ONE real defect remained (the UltraMax hook) and is fixed. SAM 2 fusion and the seven invented settings are not built.**
  *Premise check:* `surgical_mouth_enabled`, `mouth_aperture_threshold`, `teeth_preservation_weight`, `non_target_isolation_enabled`, `isolation_margin_px`, `occlusion_governance_mode`, `xseg_sam2_fusion_weight` exist nowhere in the repo. The real settings are `restore_original_mouth`, `mouth_open_threshold`, `enable_xseg_occlusion`, `occlusion_erosion` (non-target protection has no setting; its pad is the `_NON_TARGET_PAD` 0.10 constant). Stage 7 tests were not red at HEAD: `tests/test_stage7_*` 59 passed before any change (c72e97a cleared them).
  *Already correct, verified:* RealityUX composites in the aligned crop and maps neighbour polygons and landmarks in with `M` (`Mask_RealityUX.py`, c72e97a; 100 real faces below). Its kernels are stated at a 256 px reference and scaled by the mask grid (`_REF_MASK_PX`). The brief's `s = sqrt|det M|` was NOT applied: the alignment already pins the face's size in the crop, so crop-relative IS face-relative, and a det(M) factor would correct a second time. Settings flow: React (`FaceSwap.jsx`, `Settings.jsx`, `defaults.js`) -> payload -> `api._apply_surgical_settings` (preview `api.py:4604`, render `:5130`, tests `test_realityux_production_space.py`, `test_settings_wiring.py`) -> `roop.globals`; `project_checkpoint.new_project` stores and fingerprints the whole payload, so resume replays all four.
  *Defect fixed — the UltraMax hook drew FRAME-pixel lip landmarks into the crop.* `Enhance_UltraMax` -> `PipelineManager.process_enhancer_crop` -> `AnatomicalEngine` -> `MouthDynamicsEngine.process` took `get_inner_lip_points` (detector coordinates, e.g. x=900,y=500) as crop coordinates, so the polygon was clipped onto the crop edge and the cavity mask was empty - the same bug c72e97a fixed in RealityUX, left as an owner item in RECODE_STATUS 10-14. Measured on 100 open-mouth faces from Love / Weeds / Monica Bellucci / b1 (real detector, `canonicalize_face_alignment`, BiSeNet class 11 as ground truth): mean mask inside the cavity **old 0.000 (0 of 100 faces non-empty) -> new 0.259**; mask >7 px from the cavity 17.2% -> 3.0%. Fix: `mouth_dynamics.frame_pts_to_crop` (one mapper, `Mask_RealityUX._frame_pts_to_crop` now delegates), `MouthDynamicsEngine.process(M=...)`, `AnatomicalEngine` reads `target_face._aligned_M`, which `ProcessMgr` stamps beside `aligned_img` (`M`, or `_M_e` when the enhancer re-aligns) and clears after `p.Run`. The 4 px feather is now stated at 256 px and scaled to the crop (same seam fraction at 256 and 512). Live only with `restore_original_mouth` on and UltraMax as the enhancer; default off.
  *Not built:* SAM 2 / high-frequency-residual fusion into occlusion governance. `Mask_SAM2` is a whole-clip, video-tracked pre-pass whose cached masks are warped into the crop with `M`; it is not a per-crop promptable model, and a per-face-per-frame SAM 2 call adds GPU work to a GPU-bound pipeline (AGENTS: only removing work per face moves the clock). No named occlusion defect on a rendered clip to design against. `isolation_margin_px` as a setting: no defect, pad is a measured constant. Non-target protection in the UltraMax hook: not needed, the hook returns a crop that is pasted through the RealityUX matte, which already carries the protection.
  *Still open (owner):* the restore uses only the bright cavity pixels (0.26 coverage), not the whole inner-lip polygon - needs a rendered A/B. `api.py:4722` defaults the ProcessOptions `restore_original_mouth` to False when a payload omits the key while `_apply_surgical_settings` falls back to config; React always sends it, so latent.
  *Tests:* `tests/test_stage7_ultramax_hook.py` (11; mutation-checked: dropping the `M` pass-through fails 3). Full suite 5771 passed, 16 skipped, 2 xfailed; `--benchmark-mode regression` PASS (6.58 fps, face SSIM 0.9906) - that run has mouth restore OFF and does not exercise the hook.
- **"Stage 6: temporal stabilization / 3D head pose normalization / landmark tracking" re-issue: the 10-13 "not built" verdict was half wrong. Two real pose-convention defects on the multi-face (V1 .fsz) source-bank path fixed; the filters, cut reset and HUD asked for already exist and are now pinned by tests that drive them.**
  *Premise check:* commit `429e4ff` ("handle mixed source pose tuple formats") is not in this repository (`git cat-file`, `git log --all`); the defect it names was found independently below. `7c8029e` is the 10-13 changelog-only commit.
  *Defect 1 — `canonical_face_index` ranked the PROFILE photo as most frontal.* `FaceSet.ensure_face_poses` stores EPnP angles (`face_3d_recon.decompose_yaw_pitch`), where a frontal head reads yaw ~ +-170 and pitch ~ -50; `min(yaw^2 + pitch^2)` therefore picked a profile. Measured on all 36 V1 banks in `roop-keep/faceset-v1-backup-2026-09-03`: old rule agreed with the frontal photograph (index 2, `solve_pose_5pt` yaw |x| <= 8 in every bank) in **0 of 36**. The test fixture (`test_angle_flicker_fix`, "frontal 2.6") was in `solve_pose_5pt`'s convention, not what production computes, so it passed. Fix: `FaceSet.canonical_poses()` (`FaceSet.py:236`, solves each bank face from its own keypoints; V2 sets use their metadata poses unchanged) and `canonical_face_index` (`FaceSet.py:292`) ranks on that. **Averaged V1 sets (`AverageEmbeddings`, which `source_gallery` runs on every multi-face archive) return 0** — face 0 carries the identity MEAN by design; the old rule reached it for 22 of 36 banks by numerical accident and picked a single profile photo's own embedding for the other 14 (13 at index 4, 1 at index 3). So the default swap source CHANGES for those 14 V1 banks (a single profile photo's embedding, mean 0.82 cosine to the set's identity centroid and 0.47 for `ashna` -> the mean embedding). Embedding distance only; NOT render-A/B'd. V2 sets are unaffected.
  *Defect 2 — the tracking pre-pass asked the V1 bank for the wrong pose.* `procmgr_tracking.py:2918`: `getattr(target_p, 'yaw', 0.0)` on `temporal_state.pose`, which is a dict (`TemporalIdentityStabilizer.update_pose`), is always 0.0 -> every frame requested a frontal face when `ROOP_TEMPORAL_IDENTITY=1`; and with it off the target (`solve_pose_5pt`, frontal 0) was compared with the bank's EPnP angles (frontal ~170), selecting the profile. Fix: `normalize_pose_tuple` + `FaceSet.select_reference_index_canonical` (`FaceSet.py:316`). The swap-time lookup in `ProcessMgr` is unchanged (EPnP on both sides, self-consistent). Live only with `use_source_bank` (off in `config.yaml`); the pre-pass result overrides the swap-time one.
  *New `roop/head_pose.py`:* `HeadPose` (yaw, pitch, roll, confidence, landmarks_5pt; iterates/indexes as `(yaw, pitch, roll)`, string keys, `pitch_up`), `normalize_pose_tuple(raw, order)` (HeadPose / dict / `PoseEstimate` / 2-, 3-, 4-sequences / ndarray; a bare sequence must declare `order` — `CANONICAL_ORDER "ypr"` or `INSIGHTFACE_ORDER "pyr"` — it is never guessed from magnitudes; unusable input is `None`), `head_pose_from_kps`. The two inline pitch/yaw permutations (`faceset_v2._pose`, `pose_source_selector.estimate_target_pose`) now go through it. Convention is `solve_pose_5pt`'s (+yaw = viewer's right, +pitch = DOWN), which `angle_portfolio` and Stage 2 pin — the brief's "right-handed convention" was NOT applied because it would flip every gate keyed on it. No copy in `face_engine/`: it never imports `roop` and has no mixed-format site.
  *Already existed, now verified by tests that execute them (`app/tests/test_head_pose.py::SmoothingBehaviour` drives `_build_temporal_faces`):* velocity-adaptive coupled 5pt + 106pt smoothing `temporal_smoother.py:149` (wired `procmgr_tracking.py:2687`; stationary jitter std 1.0 px -> < 0.6x, 15 px/frame translation lag < 3 px); One-Euro `one_euro.py:273/306`, Kalman option `one_euro.py:395` (`procmgr_tracking.py:2684`); guarded Kalman coasting of 1-3+ frame detector dropouts `procmgr_tracking.py:2361`; shot cuts: `ContentAwareSceneDetector` in the scan loop (`procmgr_tracking.py:990`) -> smoother/filter reset at the cut (`:2690`, test: output at the cut frame equals the raw detection; without the cut 30+ px drag), tracks retired and not stitched/coasted/gap-filled across it (`test_shot_boundaries.py`). A separate "IoU < 0.25" gate was NOT added: tracks associate on predicted-box IoU + Re-ID, so a jump that low is a new track, and a fast same-track move already runs the smoother at `beta_fast` 0.90.
  *Not built:* smoothing of `landmark_3d_68` (1k3d68) — no default-path consumer (`ProcessMgr.py:1252-1300`: loaded only for 3D recon / source bank / frontalization / refine / autorotate) and no named jitter defect; a coupled smoothing of it would need its own measurement. HUD: `#/faceswap` already shows per-face `(yaw, pitch, roll)` from `solve_pose_5pt` (`api.py:4653-4668` -> `FaceSwap.jsx previewPose` -> `InteractivePreview.jsx` "D"); `#/facemgr` shows no pose and was not changed.
  *Tests:* `app/tests/test_head_pose.py` (30). Mutation-checked: reverting the whole pre-pass fix fails the frontal and turned-target pre-pass tests, reverting only the dict read fails the identity-on test. Full suite `pytest app/tests tests`: 5760 passed, 16 skipped, 2 xfailed (13 min); `--benchmark-mode regression` PASS (6.63 fps, face SSIM 0.9906, swapped 312/312) — that clip is single-person with a single-photo source, so it does NOT exercise the V1 bank path.

## 2026-10-18

- **"Stage 5: React UI workstation, API deadlines, telemetry, Playwright + axe" brief: the Stage 4 status channel now has its React consumer; four real defects found on pixels and fixed; the theme-contrast allowlist went from 36 non-zero entries to zero; `tabStops` stays (a ceiling, not a defect list).**
  *Deadline:* `src/pipelineStatus.js` follows `WS /ws/v1/pipeline/status`. When a request's 15 s deadline fires, `api.js` asks it: `STAGE_COMPILING_TRT` / `STAGE_RUNNING_CANARY` active -> the request stays in flight and gets a FRESH deadline when the stage ends; otherwise it times out as before. A busy claim is re-confirmed by a 2 s GET and expires after 6 s without one, so a backend that dies mid-build still fails requests. `ApiError` gains `endpoint`, `elapsedMs`, `suspendedMs`. `components/CompileBanner.jsx` shows model + FP32/FP16 + elapsed as a non-blocking status strip (backend: `pipeline_status.session_precision`, read from the session's TensorRT options, added to both stages). `useApi`: `scope: 'job'` requests (the shared `/api/queue` read) survive route changes; `abortJobRequests()` is the explicit cancel.
  *Frame retry:* now deterministic 250 / 500 / 1000 ms, three retries (4 attempts), no jitter, replacing 0.5-8 s / 5 attempts (a786b33); applies to the stage frame and to playback chunks, checked at 0.25x-4x.
  *Defects found by pixel tests:* **alpha blend ignored its percentage** (`drawTop` overwrote `globalAlpha`: Blend 25% showed the swap at 100%); **Erase left a ghost** on the visible mask (`destination-out` with the 0.45-alpha tint thinned by 45% per pass; the export was clean); **every HUD button (Lens / Mask Brush / Erase) was dead while the stage was zoomed** (pointer-down started a pan and took pointer capture, so `click` never fired); **the brush mapped wrong on letterboxed media** (anamorphic SAR/DAR: the canvas box is not the picture; `maskBrush.js`). Also the new Enhancer settings had unlabeled sliders and sub-AA text (axe + contrast red on Settings).
  *Themes:* `postcss-ink-floor.js` gives every `text-white/N` and `text-<hue>-<200..400>(/N)` a per-theme floor / lightness window (`--ink-floor`, `--hue-min`, `--hue-max`, set per mode at the end of `index.css`); accent-fill ink threshold 0.62 -> 0.565 (white on mid-tone accents was 3.7-4.1:1); dark panels written as `bg-slate-9*` / `bg-neutral-9*` are treated as dark surfaces on light pages. The default theme is unchanged. `e2e/allowlist.json` `themeContrast`: every theme 0. `tabStops.faceswap: 258` remains: it is a count of Tab presses, not a defect.
  *Tests added:* `compile-banner`, `canvas-tools` (brush / blend / diff / wipe / Lens on real pixels), `offline` (no foreign origin, bundled font loaded, UNVERIFIED offline) specs; playback-at-every-speed in `frame-unavailable`; `brush-mapping-check.mjs`; suspension, `elapsedMs` and job-scope checks in `render-api-client.mjs` (mutation-checked: stubbing `suspendWhileBusy` fails them); `StagePrecision` in `test_pipeline_status.py`.
  *Not done / unverified:* backend restart + UI reload needed (precision field, banner); not exercised against a real cold TensorRT build, only against the mock's replay of the channel; the persistent cross-fade ramp has no pixel test; the 3060 is unmeasured.

- **"Stage 4: pipeline governor, streaming queue, segment concat, core namespace consolidation" brief: the segment-overwrite bug was real (two causes) and is fixed; the status channel and the governor's absolute VRAM floor were missing and are added; the namespace was already fixed (5609800) and a single CUDA owner thread is declined on measurements.** Detail and numbers: `docs/perf/stage4_segments_governor_2026-10-18.md`.
  *Segment overwrite:* (1) a slice's final name came from the output template alone, so any template without a time token put every slice on one path (default `{file}_{time}` collides within a second); (2) **`clear_output` defaults ON and `_run_swap` removes the whole output folder before every run, so each slice deleted all earlier slices**. Now: `roop/segment_output.py` names slices `{stem}_segment{idx:04d}_{start:06d}_{end:06d}.mp4` (`core.final_video_destination`), a slice never clears the folder (`api._clear_output_folder`), and when every slice of a Batch Matrix split is COMPLETED the queue verifies them (exists, non-empty, decodable video), writes `{stem}_concat_list.txt` and stream-copy joins to `{stem}_joined.mp4` with the slices kept. `stageSegments` stamps `segment {group,index,count}`; unmarked ranged jobs on one file are named as slices too. `render_distributed` already had unique chunk names and kept its workspace; it now also verifies the chunks before writing its join list.
  *Namespace:* `roop/core.py` stays one module (5609800 removed the shadowing `roop/core/`); making it a package would recreate the break. New `tests/test_core_import_surface.py` fails on any name the repo imports from `roop.core` that it lacks.
  *Governor / status:* `UnifiedRuntimeScheduler` gets an absolute free-VRAM floor (`ROOP_GOVERNOR_MIN_FREE_GB`, 1.2 GiB, 0 = off), a rate-limited cache flush under it and a `VRAM_GOVERNOR_THROTTLE` event; it only acts in the one-owner stream, which stays opt-in (`ROOP_STAB_STREAMING=1`) because it measured 3.4x slower than the threaded path. New `GET /api/v1/pipeline/status` and `WS /ws/v1/pipeline/status` carry STAGE_COMPILING_TRT (`predictor.warmup_session`), STAGE_RUNNING_CANARY (`aux_canary.check_session`), VRAM_GOVERNOR_THROTTLE, SEGMENT_PROGRESS and SEGMENTS_JOINED. `PROCESSING` and `PAUSED`/`PAUSE_REQUESTED` are now written to `queue.json` when entered (all 10 `JOB_STATES` are on disk).
  *Not done:* a React consumer of the status channel (the 15 s deadline still fires on a request that blocks through a cold build); `b900deb` is not in this repository's history; the 3060 and a real VRAM squeeze are unmeasured. Backend restart and a UI reload needed.
  *Tests:* `test_segment_output.py` (real ffmpeg), `test_queue_segments.py` (real runner loop, `clear_output` ON; three mutants each fail it), `test_pipeline_status.py` (through `api.app`), `test_core_import_surface.py`, `test_distributed_render.py` (+2), `batch-matrix-check.mjs` (+4). Found while testing: `snapshot(events=0)` returned every event (`[-0:]`).

- **"Stage 3: decoupled UltraMax / GPEN Realistic execution, zero-copy tensor handling" brief: the ring is built and wired for GPEN Realistic and RF++ (byte-identical, default ON) and for UltraMax (TensorRT opt-in); the stated race did not exist in the code, and end-to-end fps is NOT improved (+1.2%, unresolvable).** Numbers: `docs/perf/stage3_zero_copy_ring_2026-10-18.md`; rules: `CUDA_EXECUTION_POLICY.md` "Stage 3".
  *Premise check:* no enhancer reused a static output `OrtValue` (ORT allocated per run). The binding "Torch-owned output corrupts" is true only for CodeFormer's dynamic output; GPEN-512 and RF++ have static outputs and a pointer-bound output measured max |diff| 0.0 against the host path under TensorRT.
  *Built:* `ZeroCopyBufferPool` / `ZeroCopySlot` (`optimized_processor.py`): 4-slot ring of contiguous input+output tensors per resolution, a lock + `torch.cuda.Event` per slot, a CUDA stream per worker thread, VRAM-margin slot reduction, allocated/peak accounting, `scope()` and `release()` (called from `core.release_resources` on job end and cancel; waits for in-flight leases). ORT runs on its own stream so the ORT side is ordered by a host wait + `synchronize_outputs()`, not `wait_event` (documented). `Enhance_GPENRealistic` and `Enhance_RestoreFormerPPlus` normalise on the GPU into the slot, bind by pointer and finish on the GPU (finite check, uint8 cast, collapse test, luma recolour) with one uint8 download; `ROOP_ZEROCOPY_ENHANCE=0` restores the host path. New exact GPU ports in `enhance_common` (`model_output_to_u8_tensor`, `gray_u8_tensor`, `luma_only_recolour_u8_tensor`, `looks_collapsed_tensor`), pinned to cv2 by `test_enhance_common_gpu.py` (OpenCV 4.11 gray is 15-bit fixed point; `convertScaleAbs` is a fused multiply-add).
  *Found:* `Enhance_UltraMax._run_cuda_postprocess` has been OFF under TensorRT since 8c10e91, so on the 4070 UltraMax ran the 92.9 ms/face host path, and the test claiming to guard that case passed vacuously (empty call list); fixed to assert at least one `bind_output`. UltraMax now takes its input through the ring and on TensorRT it is opt-in (`ROOP_ULTRAMAX_TRT_RING=1`): 3,800 faces at 8/12/20 threads on the live engine, no hang, ring == old CUDA path bit for bit; 40.0 -> 30.1 ms/face isolated; 600-frame counterbalanced A/B +1.2% (spread 6.7%). The CUDA post chain differs from the host chain by 0.29/255 mean (pre-existing, float vs cv2 fixed point).
  *Measured, not claimed:* GPEN Realistic 26.4 -> 20.7 ms/face, RF++ 49.1 -> 44.2, 8-thread throughput unchanged for both (GPU-bound). Not measured: the 3060, any render with GPEN Realistic or RF++ selected.
  *Correction (found 2026-10-18 by the Stage 4 full-suite run):* the ORT-owned output copy in `enhance_common.ort_cuda_output_to_torch` (`cudaMemcpy` D2D, no host sync) raced the ring's non-blocking worker streams: ~5% of UltraMax-ring calls read stale memory. Fixed (`cudaStreamSynchronize(0)` after the copy) with a regression test; the stress runs quoted here compared faces with their own first pass and could not see it. Details in the perf note.
  *Tests:* `test_zero_copy_buffer_pool.py` (ordering with a negative control; the `wait_event` and slot-lock lines mutation-checked), `test_enhance_common_gpu.py`, `test_enhancer_zero_copy.py`, `test_enhancer_ultramax.py` (+2).

- **"Stage 2: TensorRT profile shape binding, per-model precision policy, resilient fallback chaining" brief: three of its four asks already shipped, two real gaps found and fixed, one ask applied at a measured cost, one ask NOT applied because the data contradicts it.** Numbers and harnesses: `docs/perf/stage2_canary_cosine_2026-10-18.md` (+ `.json`, `tools/measure_canary_cosine.py`, `tools/measure_gpen_canary_controls.py`). Policy and enforcement sites: `PRECISION_POLICY.md`, "Stage 2".
