  *Already shipped (the brief cites the commits that did it):* xseg / 2d106det / 1k3d68 FP32 + the engine-hash-cached canary = 967e17e; RF++ island study (target unreachable) + restorer canary + opt-in native runner = 7a15446. `e8c89f8` is not in this repository's history; the "unbound profile crashes TensorRT" premise is not reproducible: `trt_shape_profile.resolve_profile` already binds explicit `trt_profile_{min,opt,max}_shapes` for every dynamic input (UltraMax `x:1x3x512x512 .. 8x3x512x512`).
  *Profiles the brief asks for that cannot exist:* GPEN-256/512, RF++, CodeFormer v0.1 and `inswapper_128` are exported with STATIC `[1,3,S,S]` inputs; TensorRT rejects a max above a static dimension, so `[1..4,3,512,512]` / Pixel-Boost `[1..16,3,128,128]` can only exist after a re-export (and a dynamic-batch swapper was measured slower). Pinned by `test_stage2_precision_profiles.py`; nothing was changed.
  *Real gaps, fixed:* (1) **the recorded downgrades were never reported.** `backend_manager.provider_degradations()` / `session_degradations()` had no caller, so a worker running on a worse provider than requested was visible only as one terminal line. `record_provider_degradation` now carries the model; `/api/runtime/state` -> `sections.PROVIDER.values.downgrades` lists them (newest 50); canary-driven steps (mixed -> TRT FP32 -> CUDA/CPU, and native -> ORT RF++) are recorded too. (2) **`build_session_with_fallback` had no CPU rung for DirectML, CoreML or MIGraphX** (the CPU rung was built only for tensorrt/cuda/rocm chains), so a build that raised there ended the worker; fixed, tested for all three.
  *Applied at a cost, on the owner's repeated instruction:* **RF++ is FP32 by default** (`PRECISION_OVERRIDES["restoreformer_plus_plus"]`). Measured on the 4070, same process: 21.63 -> 39.31 ms/face (**+82% on the enhance stage** of every RF++ / Restore Ultra render) for cosine 0.9997 -> 1.0000 and SSIM 0.978 -> 0.9999 on the worst of 8 inputs. End-to-end fps NOT measured. Verified on the real processor: TensorRT, `trt_fp16_enable=0`, cache namespace `..._restoreformer_pp_fp32`, canary OK. Revert: delete the table entry or `ROOP_TRT_MODEL_PRECISION="restoreformer_plus_plus:global"`. The 3060 is unaffected (TensorRT is not admitted there).
  *Canary:* every spec now also carries a cosine floor on the raw output: xseg / 2d106det / 1k3d68 >= 0.998, RF++ / UltraMax >= 0.995 (healthy engines score >= 0.9996). The verdict identity names the TensorRT version and the GPU compute capability (`runtime_stamp`) beside the engine hash, ONNX digest, precision and profile shapes it already keyed on, so every cached verdict is re-checked once. The opt-in RF++ native engine, which skipped the canary entirely, is now checked (`NativeSession.run`), and a failing one is dropped for the ORT engine and recorded.
  *NOT applied: GPEN at cosine >= 0.995 with an FP32 fallback.* Healthy shipped GPEN FP16 engines score cosine 0.9922-0.9978 against FP32 (SSIM 0.85-0.89); 0.995 fails GPEN-256's own canary input (0.99417) and 3 of 8 inputs on GPEN-512, so the rule would rebuild the 4070's tuned GPEN look as FP32 on engines that are fine. GPEN Realistic gets a **collapse** canary at cosine >= 0.97 instead (wrong face <= 0.84, 24 px shift <= 0.92, grey 0.0, NaN fail; it does NOT see blur, sigma-5 scores 0.991-0.995). Real-hardware check: with the floor forced to 0.9999 the real GPEN-512 processor failed the canary, rebuilt on the TensorRT FP32 engine, and the step appeared in the runtime state. Only GPEN Realistic is wired; `Enhance_GPEN`, `Enhance_GPEN256Pro` and `Enhance_GPENUltimate` build their sessions separately and are NOT canary-guarded.
  *Rules:* `AGENTS.md` gains "Never mark a stage 'not built' or 'already exists' on a reading" (a named site and a passing test, or say "unverified"); `CLAUDE.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `QWEN.md` point at it.
  *Test changes:* `test_aux_canary.py` replaced `test_gpen_is_deliberately_not_covered` (GPEN now has a cosine-only spec) and one landmark test used an all-zero reference (cosine undefined there). New: `test_stage2_precision_profiles.py`; downgrade/native/identity tests in `test_aux_canary.py`, `test_runtime_state.py`, `test_execution_providers.py`.

- **"Stage 1: Pinokio provisioning, lockfile repair, model registry, standalone install guards" brief: the reported root causes did not reproduce on this checkout; the real gap was that a failed build could still be committed as a finished install. Fixed that, and filled the registry's real gaps.**
  *What did not reproduce:* `react-ui/package-lock.json` exists, is tracked, is `lockfileVersion` 3 and is in sync (`npm ci --dry-run` = "up to date", no `package.json` dependency disagrees with the lock), so nothing was regenerated. `logs/api/install.js` holds no `EUSAGE` or "'vite' is not recognized", and its latest run committed. `app/env`, `app/models`, `app/facesets` are real directories (`test_standalone_install.py` passes); `link.js` (`fs.link venv`) links the package folders INSIDE site-packages, never `app/env` itself, so it does not conflict with that test and was left alone. The `conda_hook & conda deactivate ...` chain is generated by Pinokio for every `shell.run`; `conda: { skip: true }` is the only lever and was NOT used, because it would also drop the conda `bin/` PATH that macOS/Linux get and only Windows could be tested.
  *The real defect:* Pinokio keeps running a script after a command in a shell exits non-zero. So a failed `npm ci` (EUSAGE reproduced here with no lockfile) was followed by `npm run build`, then by `install_state.py commit`, which published `.pinokio-install-complete.json` without a UI. Also, `start_react.js` matched `npm ERR!`, which npm 11 no longer prints (`npm error`).
  *Fixed:* `install.js`, `update.js` and `start_react.js` run `npm ci --no-audit --no-fund || npm install --no-audit --no-fund --legacy-peer-deps` (the fallback was run under `cmd.exe` with no lockfile: EUSAGE, then exit 0), then `react-ui/scripts/verify-build.mjs vite` before `npm run build` and `... dist` after, and break on its `[build-verify] FAIL` marker. `install_state.py commit --require-react-build` (passed by install.js and update.js) refuses to publish the manifest when `react-ui/dist/index.html` is missing or empty and records `failed_stage=react_build`. `verify_ort.py --require-extras` (install.js only; start-up verification is unchanged) makes the manifest depend on `sam2` and `onnxconverter_common` importing, since their `--no-deps` installs fail silently. NumPy 1.26.4 was already gated by `verify_numpy`.
  *Registry:* the model facts already lived in `face_engine.models.zoo` (sha256, size, inputs, licence), `app/model_manifest.json` and `precision_policy.PRECISION_OVERRIDES`; no new registry was created. `ModelSpec.default_precision` is new, validated, and in `catalog()` with sha256/size/licence/inputs. Added the entries the zoo lacked: `2d106det`, `1k3d68` (digests = the Hugging Face `X-Linked-ETag`, equal to the local files) and `codeformer_fp16` (UltraMax; digest from the manifest). Declared FP32: `xseg`, `dfl_xseg_v2`, `2d106det`, `1k3d68`; FP16: `codeformer_fp16`, `gpen_bfr_512`, `restoreformer_plus_plus`. `default_precision` is a declaration, not a control: `app/tests/test_model_registry_precision.py` is the only reader and fails when it disagrees with `PRECISION_OVERRIDES` (checked by mutation: flipping `2d106det` fails it), and when the zoo and the manifest disagree on a shared file's sha256/size.
  *Not done, owner decision:* the brief asks for RF++ at FP32. It is declared FP16 because that is what runs: the 2026-10-10 measurement put FP32 at 31.6 vs 21.75 ms/face and found the FP32-island target unreachable. To flip it, add `restoreformer_plus_plus` to `PRECISION_OVERRIDES` and change the zoo; the test then requires both. Also noted: the zoo's `gpen_bfr_512.onnx` (facefusion, sha `d5f066b9...`) and the manifest's `GPEN-BFR-512.onnx` (CountFloyd, sha `0960f836...`) are two different files for the same model; not unified. `app/tests/test_launcher_e2e_harness.py` cannot be collected (`import fixtures`; `tests/fixtures/` is gitignored and absent), unchanged by this work.

## 2026-10-14

- **Two women, two facesets: one swapped cleanly, the other smudged (`D:\out.mp4`, harjot + ashna). Two separate causes, both "a frontal face painted onto a head that shows none"; both fixed, measured on that file only.**
  *Reproduced* with `tests/two_face_video.py` on the live config (realswap, UltraMax, RealityUX, TensorRT, 20 threads): wrong faceset on 0 of 664 swaps (the identity routing was never the problem). Per-person grading cannot see a smudge, so the output was read frame by frame.
  *A. frames 340-447, person 0 (head down, turned away).* The 5-point solver reads 90.3-92.5 deg of yaw (past profile: the detector's keypoints are degenerate, the swap crop lands on the ear), and the render shows a doubled eyebrow, a nose past the head outline and a smeared mouth. Ruled out by re-rendering a 40-frame window with each stage off: enhancer off, stabilizer off, mask engine off, hififace band alpha 0 - the smudge is identical in all four, so it is the swapper's own output. The outcome guard (`swap_moved_the_face`) measured it at displacement 0.65-0.88 / shape 0.40-0.54, under both shipped tolerances (1.0 / 0.75), so it passed. **Fix:** past `VERIFY_AWAY_YAW` (88 deg, jaw-free yaw) the guard judges at `VERIFY_AWAY_MOVED_TOL` 0.5 / `VERIFY_AWAY_SHAPE_TOL` 0.3 (`shape_tol` parameter on `swap_moved_the_face` / `_verify_after`). 71 of the 73 faces reading >= 88 deg are discarded (the other two read 70.8 deg and were clean); every other face of the clip reads <= 85.4 deg and scores <= 0.30 / 0.19. The discarded face shows the ORIGINAL, not a swap: nothing swaps a head that is turned away.
  *B. frames 448-479, person 1 (the other woman turns into an out-of-focus back of a head).* The detector reports a "face" on the ear at 0.64-0.82, her embedding is 0.89-0.96 from her own identity (gate 0.75), and the identity lock swaps her anyway: a whole ghost face on the ear. The outcome guard cannot see it (displacement 0.3-0.4 on half of those frames; the readings of 1.0-1.4 on the rest are detector instability on a non-face, not a swap). **Fix:** `LOCK_MIN_DET` 0.85 - a locked face under that detector confidence loses the lock and goes to per-frame matching, which swaps it only if THIS frame's embedding is inside the match threshold. Every other swapped face of the clip scores >= 0.84 except three transition flashes, which were poor swaps anyway. A man's face in a phone photo (frame 80) that the lock had been swapping with a female face is now left alone.
  *Result (same command before / after):* guard discards 16 -> 86 (the 70 extra = cluster A), lock-floor skips 0 -> 35; only frames 340-447 and 80 change strongly, the profile swaps (frame 146) and the clean swap on the other woman in the same frames (350, 420) are unchanged; 9.5 fps after (GPU not shared). Tests: `tests/test_verify_away_tier_and_lock_floor.py` (9 of 10 fail on the old code), `test_verify_tol.py` updated for the new keyword; full suite 5569 passed.
  *Not done / limits:* both thresholds were set on ONE clip - d2/d5/d1 renders were started for a second population and stopped when the owner said to test `D:\out.mp4` only. `ROOP_VERIFY_AWAY_YAW=0` and `ROOP_LOCK_MIN_DET=0` restore the old behaviour. The head-down woman is now unswapped for ~110 frames rather than swapped properly. Separate, not touched: strangers (the man in the phone photo, the poster man at frames 660/700) are swapped by the lock when `max_face_distance` admits them. **Backend restart needed** (api.py is a non-reloading uvicorn thread).

- **"Stage 10: multi-modal masking stack (CLIPSeg / BiSeNet) + gradient colour harmonization" brief: NOT built, no code changed. CLIPSeg, the fusion chain, the box mask and the LAB transfer exist; Poisson blending was evaluated and deliberately left out of the hot path.**
  *Where each ask lives:* text-prompted masking = `Mask_Clip2Seg` (CLIPSeg via `clip.clipseg.CLIPDensePredT`, weights `models/CLIP/rd64-uni-refined.pth`; prompts from `mask_clip_text`, default "cup,hands,hair,banana"); XSeg / XSeg-3 / BiSeNet face parser / occluder / SAM2 / FastSAM engines, fused for the default by `Mask_RealityUX` (XSeg authoritative, BiSeNet subtracts hair/ears/cloth/hat, geometric glasses-frame protection, neighbour and mouth protection; see the RealityUX audit above) and chained through `mask_engine` / `mask_engine_2` plus `occlusion_mask.inject_occlusion_engine`; user rectangular/elliptical box = `mask_offsets` (top/bottom/left/right, ellipse in `paste_upscale`) with `mask_blur` feathering; ROI-limited morphology = `mask_roi.py`; LAB statistics transfer = `procmgr_color` (`color_transfer_mode`: `lct` covariance-whitening in LAB, the default, and `rct` Reinhard mean/std; the brief's formula is `rct`), plus `frequency_split.reinhard_lab` and `enhance_blend` for the enhancer.
  *Why the specifics are rejected:* (1) `roop/compositing/`, `roop/masking/` would duplicate `compositing_engine.py`, `temporal_compositing.py`, `mask_roi.py` and the processors above, and `roop/enhancers/anatomical_engine.py` is the Stage 7 file with the frame-space defects fixed today. (2) `clipseg.onnx` does not exist; the shipped CLIPSeg is the PyTorch model, and an ONNX export would need the same output check the other masks got. (3) Adaptive Otsu on a CLIPSeg heat-map: the heat-map is a soft prior, and binarizing it is the hard-edge/flicker path the project removed ("mask the object, never refuse"); the temporal mask stabilizer needs the soft values. (4) Poisson/gradient-domain blending is documented as deliberately not used: `cv2.seamlessClone` is CPU-only, allocates a full patch, and did not improve the boundary metric enough to justify its cost (`temporal_compositing.py` docstring). (5) GPU-resident morphology: `torch max_pool2d` is not `cv2` ellipse dilation, and cv2 single-threaded is cheaper than the transfer (matte-ops note, 2026-10-03). (6) Per-channel mean/std remap of the swapped face to the boundary statistics: measured on Taras (2026-10-13), the colour transfer is a verified no-op for the face because the crop is mostly the plate's own surround, and a face-ellipse LCT built to test it measured identical and was reverted; the "less yellow" was lips/makeup/blush identity, not a transfer failure.
  *What would reopen it:* a clip + frame range with a visible seam or colour step at the neck/hairline, with the mask and colour-transfer lines from the log.

- **"Stage 9: modular swapper registry, ArcFace 512-d centroid, Pixel-Boost before UltraMax" brief: NOT built, no code changed. The registry, the embedding, the centroid and Pixel-Boost all exist; the `roop/core/` path is unavailable; the HF-guidance and batch-profile asks are already measured and rejected.**
  *Where each ask lives:* swapper registry = `roop/processors/frame/model_registry.py` (name -> class and name -> `SWAP_MODELS` key) over `onnx_swappers.py` / `swapper_base.py` and the `SWAP_MODELS` table in `FaceSwapInsightFace` (inswapper_128, reswapper_256, hyperswap 1a/1b/1c, ghost 1/2/3, simswap 256 + unofficial 512, blendswap, uniface, hififace, instyleswapper A/B/C, cscs, realswap = the live default); the `swap_model` flag switches it; 512-d ArcFace = `w600k_r50` inside the buffalo_l analyser, guarded by an engine-hash canary (`aux_canary`, cosine >= 0.99); multi-image centroid = `FaceSet.AverageEmbeddings` / the V2 faceset's stored normalized centroid with median-centroid outlier rejection (`faceset_v2`); Pixel-Boost = `implode_pixel_boost` / `explode_pixel_boost` with `subsample_size` (the swapper runs on tiles of a larger aligned crop, the FaceFusion mechanism); swap -> restorer hand-off at 256 -> 512 with a GPU post path (`Enhance_UltraMax._run_cuda_postprocess`).
  *Why the specifics are rejected:* (1) `roop/core/model_registry.py` is the `roop/core/` package that shadowed `core.py` (5609800); the registry is `processors/frame/model_registry.py`. (2) `inswapper_256.onnx` is not in the SWAP_MODELS table and no such file is shipped by the sources this app uses; reswapper_256 and instyleswapper A/B/C are the 256 inswapper-family models that exist. (3) Dynamic batch 1/2/8 profiles: `tools/convert_dynamic_shapes.py` verifies batched == per-sample, and swap batching measured SLOWER on two-person footage (realswap d4 0.913 ON/OFF, avg batch 1.3-1.6 of 8); inswapper FP16 gives wrong colours and INT8/FP8 were rejected. (4) "Inject high-frequency edge guidance before the restorer" is the removed UltraMax texture restore: the face is already ~155% of the plate's texture, and UltraMax now takes the LOW band from the swap (`ultramax_detail_weight` 0.75) for exactly the opposite reason. (5) "Zero-copy swapper -> UltraMax": the swap output is already host-composited per face; Torch-owned output buffers corrupted faces when tried (Stage 3, 2026-10-13), and a render is GPU-bound at the network floor.
  *What would reopen it:* a named swap model that fails to load, or a clip + frame range where the swap output is visibly softer than its native resolution allows.

- **"Stage 8: multi-engine detection, 4-way auto-rotation, temporal ROI cache, dual-stage landmarks" brief: NOT built, no code changed. Every ask maps to shipped code; the two `roop/core/...` paths cannot be created.**
  *Where each ask lives:* detector registry = `face_util._HYBRID_ENGINES` + `detector_engine` setting (`scrfd` via insightface, `yoloface` = `roop/yoloface.py` + `yoloface_8n.onnx`, `yunet` = `roop/yunet.py`, `retinaface` 10g / `retinaface_r50` (the live default, chosen on measured recall) / `retinaface_r50_gpu`), with `adaptive_detector.py` and `session_pool` pools; TensorRT precision per model in `precision_policy` (`PRECISION_OVERRIDES` already pins `2d106det`, `1k3d68`, `xseg`, `resnet18` to FP32); rotation = `_rescue_rotated` (clockwise, anticlockwise AND 180 = all three turns, accumulated, coordinates un-rotated by `_unrotate_face_coords`) plus a post-detect rotation check (`face_rotation_action`, `rotation_improves_upright`); temporal ROI = `temporal_tracker.py` (stable pad 0.55, uncertain 1.35 of the box) with `get_all_faces_in_roi`, default ON (`temporal_detection: true`); coarse-to-fine alignment = 5-point `align_crop` then `refine_landmarks` with the 106 and 3d68 regressors.
  *Why the specifics are rejected:* (1) `roop/core/face_util.py` and `roop/core/video_processor.py` would recreate the `roop/core/` package that shadowed `roop/core.py` and broke the app at 9b18807 (fixed in 5609800; `tests/test_core_module_not_shadowed.py` now fails if the directory returns). (2) `roop/detectors/*` would be a second copy of five modules that have tests, pools and a lifecycle. (3) "Retry rotations when confidence < 0.65" is the wrong gate: on a frontal head rolled 180 the detector reports 0.98 confidence with crushed keypoints (documented in `face_util`, interocular 177 -> 136 px), so confidence never fires; the shipped gate keys on eye->mouth roll. (4) The "60% lower per-frame latency" has no budget behind it: the detect stage reads 42% of thread time and making it cheaper bought +1% (render is GPU-bound), and `temporal_detection` measured neutral end to end. (5) A 20% ROI margin is tighter than the shipped 0.55/1.35; it has not been A/B'd and a tight crop is what loses fast-moving faces, so it is not a free win.
  *What would reopen it:* a clip, frame range and the `[Detect]`/swap-audit lines showing faces the current chain misses.

- **RealityUX mask engine audit: four defects fixed, every one measured on real footage; and the app itself was unrunnable at HEAD.**
  *HEAD was broken first.* `9b18807` (Stage 7) added a `roop/core/` package beside `roop/core.py`. A package beats a same-named module, so `import roop.core` became an empty package: `live_swap`, `batch_process_regular`, `get_processing_plugins`, `decode_execution_providers` ... all gone, and every lazy `from roop.core import ...` (api.py, bench.py, the benchmark runners) failed at call time. The `__path__` line added to `core.py` to "allow submodules" can never execute (the package shadows the module that holds it), and the Stage 7 tests only imported the submodule, so the suite stayed green. `PipelineManager` now lives in `roop/pipeline_manager.py`; `tests/test_core_module_not_shadowed.py` fails if `roop/core/` returns. (`inference_engine.py` already warned about this exact trap.)
  *1. Neighbour protection erased the target's own face (`Mask_RealityUX`).* The neighbour box was mapped frame -> crop with `M` and then drawn into the mask at crop scale, but with BiSeNet on (the 4070 default) the mask is the parser's 512 map over a 256 crop, so the polygon landed at half scale, up-left of where the neighbour is. Real footage (d4.mp4, 112 faces, real detector + real XSeg/BiSeNet, protection on vs off): **3.38% of the target's swap region lost on average, 13/112 faces lost >10%, worst 53.6%, several with no neighbour in the crop at all.** Now drawn in the mask's own pixels: **0.02% mean, 0/112 over 10%, worst 1.5%**, the two faces above 1% having a real neighbour in the crop (loss ~ overlap). Also: the unwarped-box path (`ROOP_NONFRONTAL_MASK`) passed `M` for a crop that is not in `M`'s space; it no longer passes `target_face`/`M` to RealityUX there.
  *2. Arbitration guessed a target (`OcclusionGovernor.arbitrate_multi_face_tracks`, shared by the compositor-level protection too).* When `target_face` was not among the detections (a rebound/rotated copy, a coasted track) it fell through to "the first face is the target" and filed the REAL target under non-targets, protecting its own face. Now: same object, else best box overlap >= 0.5, else **protect nobody**. A box overlapping the target >= 0.5 is the same face detected twice, not a neighbour.
  *3. Mouth restore was empty on every real face.* `landmark_3d_68` is in FRAME pixels; `generate_inner_mouth_mask` draws into the crop, so the polygon was clipped to the crop's corner. Landmarks now go through `M`. 153 open-mouth faces, mean mask value inside the true lip polygon: **3060 profile (no parser) 0.000 -> 0.309; 4070 profile 0.336 -> 0.438.**
  *4. `compute_mar`'s 106-point branch was unreachable and, reached, wrong.* A `>= 68` test caught every 106 array. The formula, measured on 298 real faces: median 0.41 vs 0.18 for the 68-point reference, **100% of mouths over the 0.18 threshold**, correlation -0.05; the best 106 index block reaches r=0.49 held-out. It now returns 0.0 (unknown -> no restore). The production signal is sound: 68-point MAR vs BiSeNet cavity height r=0.74; threshold 0.18 precision 0.81, recall 0.93 (0.22: 0.95 / 0.81).
  *Also:* pixel kernels (occlusion erosion, mouth and neighbour feathers) were written in mask pixels, i.e. twice the crop-relative size on the 3060 (256 mask) as on the 4070 (512); stated at a 256 reference now (edge-band disagreement 0.071 -> 0.033; thresholded area unchanged, so this is small and on an opt-in feature). `_last_telemetry` was one dict shared by all workers; now per thread.
  *Stage 7 leftovers that were already red at HEAD (4 suite failures, verified identical with my changes stashed):* (a) **the Surgical mouth & occlusion controls never reached the engine**: `restore_original_mouth`, `mouth_open_threshold`, `enable_xseg_occlusion`, `occlusion_erosion` were saved to config and read off `roop.globals`, which only the `/enhancer` route wrote; new `api._apply_surgical_settings` (the `_apply_eye_restore_settings` pattern, clamped, called by preview and render) feeds them; their four `ROOP_*` env entries had no reader and are gone (`LIVE_ENV_SETTINGS`, schema regenerated); (b) the three controls were missing from `FACESWAP_DEFAULTS`, so Reset skipped them; (c) `OcclusionGovernor` built two raw `InferenceSession`s, one a silent CPU fallback; now `create_strictly_accelerated_session`, and a failure is reported, not hidden; (d) one silent `except Exception` in the governor. **Consequence to know:** with `restore_original_mouth` on, RealityUX's mask-level mouth restore now actually runs beside the legacy cutout restore (same flag), where before it was dead.
  *Not changed, with the evidence:* (a) the mouth mask keeps only the cavity pixels brighter than ~0.7x the cavity median, so the dark interior (tongue shadow) stays the swap's: only 0.31-0.44 of the polygon is restored. Whether restoring the whole inner-lip polygon looks better needs a rendered A/B, not a mask statistic. (b) `enable_xseg_occlusion` is a hardening of XSeg's own 0.4 level set (outside-the-face reads high too), not a foreign-object detector; harmless, left. (c) The parser's single-thread executor serialises BiSeNet across workers; the render is GPU-bound, so left. (d) `Enhance_UltraMax`'s Stage 7 hook passes `target_face.aligned_img` (absent -> falls back to the swapped crop as "original") and the same frame-space landmarks: same family as #3, not RealityUX, not touched. (e) ProcessMgr now gates the LEGACY mouth restore on MAR >= 0.18 (Stage 7); a behaviour change for anyone with `restore_original_mouth` on. `test_lipsync_audio` searched the old source text and was updated.
  *Tests:* `tests/test_realityux_production_space.py` (9, production shapes: 512 labels over a 256 crop, frame-space landmarks, a real `M`; 7 of 8 mask tests fail on the pre-fix source), 2 MAR tests rewritten, `test_core_module_not_shadowed.py`. Full suite: 5558 passed, 16 skipped, 2 xfailed; one `WinError 145` temp-dir cleanup race in `test_project_io_nle` under load (7/7 alone, three times).

## 2026-10-13

- **"Stage 6: temporal stabilization, anti-flicker, 3D yaw pose matching" brief: NOT built, no code changed. Every ask maps to shipped code, the two named files would duplicate it, and `roop/core/` cannot be created.**
  *Where each ask lives:* 1-Euro on landmarks = `roop/one_euro.py` `OneEuroFilter`/`KpsStabilizer` (+ `AdaptiveLandmarkSmoother`, the live default, in `procmgr_tracking`); affine M smoothing = `processors/frame/temporal_stabilizer.py` `TemporalMatrixStabilizer`, wired through `onnx_swappers` (its docstring has the measured table: plain EMA is WORSE than no filter on a moving head, the shipped Holt-trend + snap rule is better on all four motions); enhancer colour EMA and boundary-mask smoothing = `one_euro.EnhancerStabilizer` / `MaskStabilizer`; scene-cut reset = `scene_detector.ContentAwareSceneDetector` -> `flush_pipeline_temporal_buffers`; solvePnP pose = `pose_quality.estimate_head_pose`, `face_util.solve_pose_5pt`, `face_3d_recon`; yaw-routed multi-angle source bank = `FaceSet.select_pose_aware_reference` + `pose_source_selector` (FaceSet V2, `docs/POSE_AWARE_SOURCE_SELECTION.md`); profile retargeting = `face_frontalize` / `face_3d_recon` plan (yaw shear bounded, flip only when both views are strongly off-axis); a TPS already exists in `ProcessMgr` (jaw).
  *Why the specifics are rejected:* (1) `roop/core/` as a package would shadow `roop/core.py` and break `import roop.core`; `roop/processors/frame/face_enhancer.py` and `roop/core/video_processor.py` are not where the render loop lives (`ProcessMgr` / `procmgr_*`). (2) Scene cut at "histogram diff > 0.35": the repo already measured a fixed 0.32 gate firing on 0 of 12839 adjacent pairs on a visibly cut clip (`ContentAwareSceneDetector` is floor 0.045 + 4.5x rolling median for that reason); a different statistic, but no measurement supports a bare 0.35. (3) Defaults min_cutoff 1.0 / beta 0.05: `one_euro` time is per-frame, and the shipped 0.05-0.1 / 0.02-0.1 are calibrated; cutoff 1.0 per frame is alpha 0.86, i.e. nearly no smoothing. (4) "<0.5 ms per frame": `TemporalMatrixStabilizer.update` is six numbers on the host; not the bound (the pipeline is GPU-bound, see AGENTS.md). (5) Face-wide TPS "pose normalisation before enhancement": `frontalize` is default off and was net-negative until the 7070422 mirror fix and is still below off on every band; no named defect on a rendered clip argues for another warp in front of the restorer.
  *One real finding:* `roop/geometric_alignment.py` (Stage 4 "Face Alignment and Geometric Stability Engine", with its own One-Euro landmark and matrix filters) has NO production caller: only `tests/test_stage4_geometric_alignment.py` and `tools/benchmark_alignment_stability.py` import it. Anything that reads its report as the live smoother is reading dead code; the live one is `TemporalMatrixStabilizer` / `KpsStabilizer`.
  *What would reopen it:* a rendered clip with a named defect (frame range, plus the `[Stabilize]` and swapped-count lines) that the current chain does not already address.

- **"Stage 4: multi-threaded decoupled pipeline + hardware saturation" brief: NOT built, no code changed. Every component it names already exists under another name, and the bound it targets is already measured.**
  *Where each ask lives:* 3-tier bounded producer-consumer = `pipeline_concurrency.py` (seven bounded `queue.Queue` stages decode -> preprocess -> detect -> swap -> restore_mask -> composite -> encode, `BoundedSemaphore` in flight) plus the render's worker pool; NVDEC decode = `nvdec_reader.py` / `video_stream.NVHardwareVideoReader`, default on (`perf_nvdec`), no PyAV / PyNvVideoCodec in the env so no in-process CUDA surfaces (10-02: decode is 0.7% of summed thread time, 185 fps raw vs 6-14 consumed); NVENC + audio remux = `ffmpeg_writer.py` / `hardware_streamer.py`; pinned host staging = `optimized_prepass.PinnedPrepassCache` and the `hardware_streamer` ring (a 512 crop is ~0.8 MB fp16, ~0.03 ms over PCIe against a 25 ms network); CPU pools and affinity = `runtime_optimizer.apply_cpu_affinity` (measured-policy only, "auto" never pins; threads, contexts, queues and affinity are Gate E dead ends: 0.7% across a 5x thread range); VRAM safety = `vram_governor.py` (NVML first, torch fallback; `vram_safety_margin_gb` default **1.5** = the brief's 1,500 MB; steps swap batch cap 8 -> 4 -> 2 -> 1, then pool widths, then GPEN size; learns peak/estimate per settings signature) plus `render_guard`; lifecycle = `core.py` SIGINT handler -> `destroy()`, and each ffmpeg reader/writer terminates then kills its child (`video_stream`, `nvdec_reader`, `ffmpeg_writer`, `hardware_streamer`). `roop/core/` is not a package here and `pipeline_manager.py` / `video_processor.py` / `memory_governor.py` were not created. A second governor would disagree with `vram_governor` about the budget. *Why more orchestration will not help:* the render is GPU-bound at the network's own floor (1 TensorRT context saturates the card, 40 -> 36 faces/s as contexts go 1 -> 6); host-side redistribution was neutral end to end three times. *Not verified, honestly:* SIGTERM is not handled separately (Windows delivers no catchable SIGTERM; Pinokio stops the app by process kill), and I did not fault-inject an orphaned ffmpeg; if a hang on stop is actually observed, the log and the reproducing step are the deliverable that would justify code.

- **"Stage 3: zero-copy memory, dynamic shape binding, async CUDA streams" brief: NOT built. Zero-copy input already exists, the output binding it asks for corrupts faces, batching was measured here for the first time on CodeFormer and does not pay on the shipped profile, and the streams are what the 10-20 worker threads already are. New tool: `tools/bench_codeformer_batch.py`.**
  (1) *Zero-copy buffers*: `Enhance_UltraMax._run_cuda_postprocess` already binds a Torch CUDA input by `data_ptr()` and keeps chroma, low band, sharpen and eye ops on the GPU (isolated 92.9 -> 29.6 ms/face, but 600 frames end to end 4.61 -> 4.62 fps, +0.2%, not resolvable). It is deliberately OFF under the TensorRT EP (multi-worker external binding can deadlock the driver), and the ORT-owned output it uses is deliberate: binding the OUTPUT into a Torch allocation (the brief's `OrtValue`/`data_ptr` wrap) is what produced finite, striped, ghosted faces on ORT 1.23 / TRT 10 (`test_model_output_is_never_bound_into_torch_owned_memory`). (2) *Profile min 1 / opt 2 / max 8 and dynamic batch pooling*: the shipped engine already has a dynamic batch axis, profile 1 / 1 / 8 (`trt_shape_profile._BATCH_BAND`), and the ONNX is batch-invariant (CPU EP: batch vs single 1e-5; TensorRT: bit-identical, max|d| 0.0). ms per face on the 4070, TensorRT mixed, real crops: **prod 1/1/8: B=1 25.6, B=2 27.1, B=4 26.9, B=8 26.8 (batching 4-6% slower); the brief's 1/2/8: B=1 27.5, B=2 22.5, B=4 25.1, B=8 24.4.** The opt-2 engine is 12% faster at exactly B=2 and 7% slower at B=1 (most of a single-person clip); the second run on the same cached engines reproduced to <1%, a fresh engine build was not repeated (engine-to-engine spread ~15%). Ceiling if true: 12% of a ~26 ms/frame stage on frames holding two faces at once, ~2% of a render, below what the 4070 resolves (~5%); and getting pairs together across worker threads needs a cross-frame batcher, the thing swap pair batching already tried (avg batch 1.5, slower). RF++ was flat at B=1..8 (10-03). (3) *Three CUDA streams with events*: the render already runs 10-20 workers each doing pre -> infer -> post, and one TensorRT context already saturates the card (1 / 2 / 3 / 4 / 6 contexts = 40.0 / 38.4 / 37.4 / 36.6 / 36.0 faces/s, i.e. the network's own 25 ms); streams would add scheduling, not throughput. (4) `roop/core/trt_pipeline.py` and `roop/enhancers/zero_copy_worker.py` were not created. **Trap found while measuring:** `astype` on a transposed array keeps the transposed layout, and a raw `data_ptr()` bind reads it as contiguous - my first probe reported a batch-vs-single mean error of 0.285 that was entirely this; every tensor must be `.contiguous()` before it is bound.

- **"Stage 2: anatomical feature isolation + multi-scale frequency decomposition" brief: NOT built. Its core was already measured and removed; the regions it names mostly have no class or no measured defect; no code changed.**
  (1) *Re-inject the input crop's high-frequency skin texture into the restorer's low band, strength 0-2*: this is UltraMax's removed texture restore (`Enhance_UltraMax` docstring section 2; last code d995920): skin texture 156.8% -> 156.7% of the plate (paired t -0.7), flicker worse (t +4.6), 2.49 ms/face. The swapped face carries ~155% of the plate's micro-texture, i.e. it is OVER-textured, so a `skin_texture_strength` above 0 adds to a face the user already called "too sharp". The Gaussian "Original - Low + 128" split also leaks structure across every strong boundary (why `frequency_split.py` uses a guided filter), and the dual-stream variant was 3.1x the cost and not sharper (section 3). (2) *Eye / brow / lip / feature micro-sharpening*: eye protection and detail rebalance already run inside UltraMax (`_protect_swapped_eyes_gpu`, `_rebalance_eye_detail_gpu`, GPU port 3.14x/face, end-to-end neutral); the clarity engine and feature-targeted micro-sharpening shipped earlier (SESSION_LOGS). (3) *Teeth*: a restore that pasted the original's blurry teeth over the swap's clean ones gave grey, doubled teeth (CHANGELOG, earlier); there is no measured "solid-white smear" defect to threshold against. (4) *`bisenet_face_parser.onnx` classes*: the parser is `resnet18.onnx`, CelebAMask-HQ 19 classes: skin, brows, eyes, nose, mouth (11, inner mouth incl. teeth), u_lip, l_lip. There is NO oral-cavity-vs-teeth class, NO pupil/cornea/iris class and NO nasal-ridge class; those would need new landmarks or a new net. (5) `roop/enhancers/anatomical_engine.py` / `frequency_separation.py` do not exist and `roop/enhancers/` is not a package here. (6) The pipeline is GPU-bound: a per-face parser pass plus per-region ops adds GPU work per face, and every stage-level change that only redistributed work measured neutral end to end. **To do any of it:** name a defect on a clip (which region, which frames); measure it against the plate with the geometric-window instrument, not Laplacian variance, then build the smallest operator that moves it.

- **"Stage 1: registry / hybrid-precision engine / canary / locking" brief: most of it already existed under other names; three real gaps filled, nothing parallel built.**
  **Premises that were false:** `roop/enhancers/ultramax.py` and `roop/core/model_registry.py` do not exist (the registry module is the swapper registry); there is no `ultramax_512.onnx` (UltraMax runs `models/CodeFormer/codeformer.fp16.onnx`) and no `bisenet_face_parser.onnx` (the parser is `resnet18.onnx`); no download URL for the former exists to write down. **Already shipped, not rebuilt:** SHA-256 + size + URL-fallback verified downloads (`model_manifest.json` / `model_integrity.py`); FP32 for `xseg` / `2d106det` / `1k3d68` (`PRECISION_OVERRIDES`, 10-10); engine caches namespaced by precision, GPU, compute capability, CUDA, driver, TensorRT and ORT version (`backend_manager.cache_namespace`, under `models/trt_cache`); an engine-hash-keyed startup canary with a TensorRT FP32 -> CUDA/CPU rebuild chain (`aux_canary.guard`); a build-once session factory that refuses a silent CPU session (`strict_session`); locked session pools. **Not built:** a native-TensorRT first tier (RF++ native measured 21.11 ms vs ORT TRT 20.44; `ROOP_RESTORER_NATIVE_ENGINE` stays opt-in); a second cache manager (it would disagree with the first about what an engine's identity is).
  **Delivered.** (1) *UltraMax had no startup canary* (`Mask_XSeg` and RF++ do). `aux_canary.SPECS["codeformer.fp16"]`: the two noisy-skin inputs (fp16 pixels + the float64 fidelity `w`, exactly what `Run()` binds) through the live engine and a one-off reference (the same graph on the CPU EP at ORT_ENABLE_EXTENDED, because ALL trips `SimplifiedLayerNormFusion` there and the CUDA EP's HEURISTIC conv algo cannot run it). `Enhance_UltraMax.Initialize` runs `aux_canary.guard` on the primary session and rebinds `session_providers` so pool extras are built on whatever the primary ended on. Floor SSIM 0.96, measured on the 4070: healthy engine min 0.9798 / mean 0.9938 on 32 real crops, 0.9847 / 0.9880 on the canary inputs; on those inputs smear 0.918, another case's output 0.910, grey 0.005, non-finite fails outright. Cost: 16.5 s once per engine (CPU reference), then 1.6 s for a whole `Initialize` in a fresh process ("cached by engine hash"). Skipped off TensorRT, i.e. on the 3060. `Initialize` / `Release` now serialise on one `RLock` (`Initialize` is the wrapper; the old body is `_initialize`). (2) *Face Parser (`resnet18`) joins the FP32 table* on the owner's instruction, NOT on a measurement of its mixed-vs-FP32 error (not captured; cost expected ~1 ms). Verified through the real loader: TensorRT, `trt_fp16_enable=0`, FP32 cache. The swapper-registry `parser_session` (`onnx_swappers.py`) builds from `resolve_providers`, bypasses the precision table, and is not the render path. (3) *`codeformer.fp16.onnx` and `xseg.onnx` are in `model_manifest.json`* (`required: false`, matching their loaders' own offline policy; hashes equal the host's X-Linked-ETag and the local files). `model_integrity.lookup` now matches a sub-folder entry by its relative path, otherwise a download into `CodeFormer/` was never held to its hash. `1k3d68` / `2d106det` ship inside `buffalo_l.zip` and are not individually listed. `aux_canary.build_feeds` takes extra scalar inputs and declared dtypes; verdict keys of existing models are unchanged (the new fields enter the identity only where set). **Not done:** `Enhance_CodeFormer`'s fp16 tier uses the same graph and would pass the same canary, but was not wired.

- **"Enhancer optimization" brief (7 items): 1 delivered (UltraMax dead-code removal, pure), 6 NOT built because the premise was already shipped, already measured and rejected, or false.** No speed claim: the enhancer stage is at the network's own floor and the render is GPU-bound.
  **Delivered - item 3.** Deleted UltraMax's opt-in dual-stream engine (`ROOP_ULTRAMAX_DUAL*`, `_init_detail_stream`, `_detail_stream`, the GPEN-512 detail session/pool) and texture restore (`_restore_texture*`, `ROOP_ULTRAMAX_TEXTURE[_SIGMA]`, `_TEXTURE_GAIN`), -495 lines in `Enhance_UltraMax.py`, plus their tests and `tests/calibrate_ultramax_texture.py`. `roop/processors/frequency_split.py` stays (the merger and XSeg optimiser use it). The measured numbers moved into the module docstring (section 2 texture: 156.8% -> 156.7%, t -0.7; section 3 dual-stream: 125 ms vs 40, softer than CodeFormer at gain 1.00) with the last commit that had the code (d995920). **Verified pure:** `Run()` of `git show HEAD:` vs the new file on one fake session output is byte-identical in 24 configs (host post and CUDA post-ops x 512/256 crop x default / low band off / `CHROMA=1` / eye ops off / sharpen 0 / `CUDA_POST=0`, same scale, same counters). `cost_summary()` no longer prints "texture restored on N" or a dual-stream line. Tests: 74 in the three edited files, 444 in every other test file that mentions UltraMax plus `test_enhancer_pool` / `test_fixture_paths` / `test_phase_harness_contracts`. **Full suite NOT run** (a render was live: GPU 83%, 8.5 GB).
  **Not built, with the evidence (do not retry without new numbers):**
  (1) *GPEN 256 Pro "move the filter to the GPU, make `ROOP_CUDA_GRAPH_FILTER` default"*: the GPU filter IS the default whenever torch sees CUDA (`ROOP_GPEN256PRO_FILTER=cpu` is the only way to the host path) and measured 1.67 ms; the graph version measured 2.06 ms (+23%, host<->device copies dominate; `CUDA_EXECUTION_POLICY.md`). 600-frame s7 A/B GPU vs CPU filter: 7.19 / 7.01 vs 7.24 / 7.13 fps. The "19 ms" figure is the module docstring's CPU-filter table, now labelled as such; a ~19 ms reading means the host path ran.
  (2) *GPEN Realistic -> 256-net + scale-2*: that is GPEN 256 Pro, which exists. The premise "the 512 detail is the paste, not the net" is contradicted by the measured detail at the paste (swap input 2.67, GPEN-256 2.82, CodeFormer-512 4.11, GPEN-512 5.14): the 256 net adds almost nothing, Pro's detail is the host texture injection. The pool is already tiered by detected VRAM (`Enhance_GPENRealistic.Initialize`: 1 context under 11.5 GB, at most `min(pool_size, 4)` on 11.5-15.5 GB, so the brief's "caps at 1 on 11.5-15.5 GB" is not what the code does; the 3123 MiB / 6.60-vs-10.50 fps measurement in that file was an UNCAPPED pool, and nothing here re-measured the current tier). "FP8 on a 4070 / Blackwell": the 4070 is Ada; TensorRT 10.9 ran 0 FP8 layers there and was 3.5x slower (swap INT8/FP8 rejection, 09-25).
  (4) *CodeFormer per-fidelity io_binding / sessions*: the binding is already cached per session (`sess._cached_io_binding`); only the 1-element `w` array is rebound per call. The comments that said "fresh io_binding per call" were stale and are fixed. A session per fidelity value would add ~530 MB of engine per value for nothing.
  (5) *RestoreFormer++ native engine*: the "30% faster" compares the whole Run() (31 ms) with a bare engine; engine-to-engine ORT TRT 20.44 vs native 21.11 ms (09-28), and the 10-10 work measured native FP16 composite SSIM 0.98542 vs 0.98618 shipped, with builder level 4/5 10-12% faster as an engine (18.5 / 18.2 vs 20.6 ms) at the same crop SSIM, not tested through ORT. `ROOP_RESTORER_NATIVE_ENGINE` exists and stays opt-in; ~2 ms of a ~140 ms frame is below what this rig resolves.
  (6) *Sampling `looks_collapsed` 1 in 10*: it costs 0.318 ms/face (not 6.6 ms/frame; ~1 face/frame, spread over 8-20 workers, and thread time is not a speedup budget). The guard compares each output with its own input, so a collapse is input-dependent; sampling would paste a flat grey face on 9 of 10 faces during a bad stretch (GFPGAN FP16 did exactly that).
  (7) *Remove the enhance-stage `_gpu_guard`*: already a no-op for every `self_excluding` enhancer (`ProcessMgr.py` `_enh_excl`); every processor the brief lists declares it. What remains is the fallback for processors with neither attribute.
  (8) *GPEN 256 base*: nothing to do (the brief's own conclusion).

## 2026-10-12

- **Second pass on the Taras.mp4 flicker: three of the six items were my measurement or identity content, not defects; two were fixed (plate-adaptive sharpening, cheap ordered smoothing, now ON); pop-outs and mask patches are characterised, not fixed.**
  **Corrected claims (measured, so the earlier entry should not be read as saying otherwise):**
  (1) *Chroma flicker* averages 0.53 LAB units/frame (median run 0.50, worst run 1.65; 6 of 76 runs above 1.0): below the ~2.3-unit just-noticeable difference, and identical with colour transfer off (0.391), `rct` (0.386) and `lct` (0.379). Not a defect.
  (2) *Texture-energy fluctuation*: the PLATE's own face texture energy changes by 0.279 log-units per frame (motion blur, compression); every output arm changes by 0.295-0.297. The 0.22-0.24 I reported was the output following the footage imperfectly. Not extra flicker; the real finding is the LEVEL (face 2.9x the plate's Laplacian variance before this change).
  (3) *"Swap less yellow than the plate", attributed to colour transfer: wrong.* A face-ellipse debug at the transfer step shows swap crop, plate crop and result all within ~1 LAB unit; in the same crop space the final pre-paste face is L -1.2 / a -1.6 / b -1.7 from the plate (below the JND). The larger offset I quoted came from a frame-space box that includes lips, brows, eyes and the plate's makeup and blush (the source face has none), plus a small encode/decode matrix shift (background alone moves L -1.6 / a -0.5 / b -0.9). Colour transfer is a no-op for the face here because the crop is mostly the plate's own surroundings; a face-ellipse-weighted LCT was built, measured identical, and reverted. `merger_clarity`'s chroma soft-knee was suspected and ruled out (off: same offset). No colour change made.
  **Fixed:**
  (a) *Merger over-sharpening amplified shimmer 2.7x.* Whole merger chain off: excess shimmer 0.76 and face texture 1.5x the plate; on: 2.07 and 3.2x. `procmgr_merger.plate_sharpness_gate` scales sharpen + clarity by `headroom x plate detail / face detail` (both measured in the plate's crop size, over the aligned-face ellipse), clamped to [0.15, 1]; stateless, so it works in any worker order. Settings `merger_match_sharpness` (on) / `merger_sharpness_headroom` (0.7), `ROOP_MERGER_SHARP_MATCH` / `ROOP_MERGER_SHARP_HEADROOM`. Headroom sweep on 420 frames: off 2.09 / 3.2x, 1.5 2.05, 1.0 1.89, **0.7 1.54 / 2.3x**. Note the semantics: at 0.7, EQUAL detail gives 0.7 (the factor reaches 1 only when the footage is 1.43x as detailed as the face); first draft of the UI text overstated this and is corrected.
  (b) *Ordered smoothing was 31% slower for almost nothing.* Enhancer + mask smoothing cut shimmer (420 frames: 2.09 -> 1.13; 1500 frames: 2.07 -> 1.56) but the block path replayed a 7-frame warm-up per block. `_stab_warmup_frames` now gives the texture/mask/HF filters a looser seed bound (`_STAB_TEXTURE_EPS` 0.5, `ROOP_STAB_TEXTURE_EPS`) and keeps the 1% rule for geometric ones (a wrong seed there is a position offset; here it is a block-start frame at the un-smoothed level). 1500 frames, back-to-back: warm-up 7f 15.5 fps / 1.56; 3f 17.7 / 1.56; 0f 19.8 / 1.60; off 22.5 / 2.07. Block-start frames are no noisier than the rest (1.43 vs 1.62). Final code over 1500 frames: smoothing off + limit 1.46, smoothing on + limit **1.12** (warm-up 1f). `app/config.yaml` (gitignored, this machine) now has `stabilize_enhancer` and `stabilize_mask` TRUE; the code and React defaults already were. Cost on the stable series is ~12-20%; fps figures taken after that were unreliable (22.5 / 20.1 / 11.9 / 17.8 / 6.0 for near-identical work, GPU contention from outside), so no figure from that stretch is quoted.
  (c) *HF-texture filter said `active=True` while reaching 7.5-15% of faces.* The carry blends with the previous frame of the same track; round-robin workers rarely hold two consecutive frames. 85-92% in the ordered path. It now prints a note at start when smoothing is off. Its isolated effect is small (3.94 -> 3.55 at weight 0.5), which is why it was not made to force the ordered path.
  **Characterised, NOT fixed:**
  *Pop-outs* (133 frames in 35 events on the full clip). By cause: 29% blurred, 29% dark, 31% lit and sharp without an obvious cut, 11% one cut inside the gap. With the detector threshold dropped to 0.15 and an ROI around the interpolated lead position, a face exists on only 17 of the 133 frames (CLAHE: 18); the other ~85% the detector cannot see, so no lower-threshold rescue (swap-time or scan-time) helps. A person-level bridge between tracks of the same source was built, found nothing to join (4 of 62 tracks carry a source in the worst window; the one candidate pair crosses a cut 21 frames apart) and was removed; near-cut coasting gained 4 frames of 36 and was removed. Most remaining pop-outs are the identity gate refusing a blurred/dark face (`fallback missed (over match threshold)`), with crowd look-alikes at mean distance as low as 0.62 indistinguishable from her. The lever is `max_face_distance`, measured on frames 560-1000: 0.75 -> 36 real dropout frames, 332 swapped faces, 4 far from the lead identity (d>0.8); 0.85 -> 11 frames (-69%), 411 swapped, 33 far; 0.95 -> 5 frames (-86%), 428 swapped, 51 far (8 above 0.9). A sample of the extra 0.85 swaps looks mostly like the lead; a few are uncertain. Default left at 0.75 (this repo has reverted five identity-gate changes made on one clip's evidence).
  *Mask patches*: the "heart-shaped" cheek region in frames 884-891 is the dancer's own hand; XSeg alone (no occluder, no parser) reproduces it, so the mask is correct. The genuine mask-jump events (36 above 15% of the face between consecutive frames of a continuous shot) cluster in a few windows that include flashes and strobe alternation; enabling mask smoothing did not change them (5 -> 5 on 420 frames).
  **Traps recorded:** `paste_upscale` writes into `frame` in place, so a post-paste "plate" read of that buffer is the result; `rows.csv` "swapped / no detection" is the GRADER's detection, not the pipeline's (239 of 372 'gap' frames were filled by the pipeline); my cut helper normalised with L2 and under-counted cuts (94 at 0.35; 264 at 0.15 with a proper L1 histogram); `--set` in my driver reaches only CLI-visible settings, use `ROOP_BENCH_CFG_OVERRIDE` with every name the harness reads (`distance_threshold` as well as `max_face_distance`); a debug patch that raised was swallowed as "Face color transfer failed" and the render carried on without the transfer.
  **Tests:** `test_texture_warmup.py` (9, mutation-checked: 4 fail with the 1% bound restored), `PlateSharpnessLimit` in `test_merger_postops.py` (11, mutation-checked: 4 fail with the gate neutralised). Pre-existing: `test_stab_chunk_overlap::test_a_pause_with_two_chunks_in_flight...` is timing-flaky (fails 2 of 5 on the ORIGINAL code, 0 of 5 with these changes).

- **UltraMax now takes the face's low band from the swap (default 0.75 of the restorer's fine detail on top); two thirds of the swap-face shimmer on a hard dance clip was UltraMax re-drawing the face's shading and shape on every frame.**
  **Reported:** heavy flicker on swapped faces and across head angles. **Test:** `D:\Taras.mp4` (1280x720, 5004 frames, 30 fps, 128 hard cuts, dark/teal/strobe shots, one lead among dozens of dancers), `harjot` faceset, the live `config.yaml` (realswap, UltraMax, RealityUX, TensorRT mixed, 20 threads), lead captured at frame 660 with auto-angles (41 angles), through `baseline_snapshot.run_one` -> `two_face_video.py` (`sync_config=True`). Window arms: frames 1280-1700 with the captured fixture pinned (`ROOP_BENCH_FIXTURE_IN`).
  **What the baseline showed:** recognition is NOT the problem. Faces within 0.6 of the lead's identity are swapped 99-100% at every face size; the 0.6-0.75 band is look-alike dancers refused by the one-source-per-frame rule. 133 of ~3,500 swapped frames pop out (35 events, one per ~5 s): mostly strobe edits that alternate framing every frame, and dark/blurred frames the detector cannot see. The tracker's bridge guard (`_INTERP_MAX_TRAVEL` 0.5, `_INTERP_MAX_SCALE` 2.0) fails on 22 + 6 of those gaps, but the stretches checked by eye (801-804, 917-921) are strobe edits and a cut the histogram detector missed, so loosening it was NOT done: it would paint swaps onto the wrong place. UltraMax ran on 3742 of 3742 swapped faces, 0 collapse fallbacks, and its colour is identical to the no-enhancer arm (face-core LAB 106.9/16.0/22.4 vs 107.1/16.1/22.5): it is not pale; the swap is a little less yellow than the plate (b 22.4 vs 27.6) before UltraMax sees it.
  **Cause:** UltraMax pasted the restorer's whole output, including its low band. A codebook prior re-draws that every frame, so the face's shading/shape wobbled and drifted from the swapped identity (the same defect `roop/enhance_blend.py` measured on Restore Ultra on 2026-09-25 and fixed there; UltraMax never got the fix). Metric: temporal acceleration of the lead face (second difference of the output over the SAME box in three frames) minus the plate's own, so head motion cancels.
  **Changed:** `Enhance_UltraMax` recombines `blur(swap) + w x (restored - blur(restored))`, sigma 2 at 512 (the `enhance_blend` operator, on GPU tensors, with the shipped CPU function as fallback); applied before the eye operators; skipped in the `ROOP_ULTRAMAX_CHROMA=1` diagnostic mode. New settings `ultramax_detail_weight` (0..1, default 0.75) and `ultramax_swap_lowband` (False = the old paste), wired settings -> `roop.globals` -> both swap entry points in `api.py` -> React (Face Swap tab, shown for UltraMax). `ROOP_ULTRAMAX_DETAIL_WEIGHT` ('off' or a number) overrides both for A/B; `ROOP_ULTRAMAX_STRUCTURE_SHARPEN` and `ROOP_ULTRAMAX_EYE_OPS` exist for re-measuring. `cost_summary` reports the recombination count (3742/3742 on the full clip), and a failure prints once instead of silently passing the restorer through.
  **Measured (window, excess shimmer / face texture vs plate; lower = steadier):** no enhancer 1.52 / 1.3x; old UltraMax 4.57 / 6.4x; weight 1.0 2.99 / 4.9x; **0.75 2.09 / 3.2x (shipped)**; 0.5 1.36 / 1.9x; 0.75 with the unsharp off 1.87. **Full clip, shipped defaults vs the baseline render:** excess shimmer 4.93 -> 2.04 (-59%), worst 40-frame window 11.1 -> 6.6, face texture 7.4x -> 3.4x the plate, brightness flicker -13%, frame-loop 25.8 -> 26.6 fps (no cost), swap audit identical (17,777 faces, 3,478 + 264 swapped), 0 failed frames. Identity distance to harjot (window, 328 frames): old 0.481, 0.75 -> 0.431, no enhancer 0.413; closer on 298 of 328 frames.
  **Rejected on the same footage (so they are not retried):** CodeFormer fidelity 0.55 -> 0.8 (4.57 -> 4.68; the control works, it moves the output ~0.005); a whole-face gradient-agreement gate on the detail band (4.57 -> 3.98 at full strength, code removed); the HF-texture carry (3.94 -> 3.55 at weight 0.5 even when it runs); `ROOP_ULTRAMAX_EYE_OPS=0` (WORSE: 5.59); enabling `stabilize_enhancer` (4.57 -> 3.46 for -28% fps on the 420-frame window, 7.5 GB free RAM). **Found, not fixed:** with `stabilize_enhancer`/`stabilize_mask` off (the saved config) the HF-texture stabiliser reports `active=True` but runs on 5/67 faces (7.5%) because round-robin workers rarely hold consecutive frames; 87-92% in the ordered block path. `_rebalance_eye_detail` switches on at a hard 1.12 contrast-ratio trigger (gain steps 0 -> 0.107); small, left. Not touched: the 35 pop-outs, mask patches around occluders, chroma flicker (0.52 LAB/frame, identical with and without an enhancer), expression/wink following (a swapper limit).
  **Test edited:** `test_enhancer_ultramax.py::test_the_networks_luminance_is_what_survives` asserted the old contract (the network decides brightness); it is pinned under `ultramax_swap_lowband = False` and 11 tests cover the new one (default anchors to the swap, detail weight is monotone, weight 0 is the swap's own tone, legacy by setting/env, env precedence and clamping, the chroma=1 bypass, the summary reports what ran). Mutation-checked: flipping the shipped default to legacy fails two of them. `tests/config_sync.py` gained an opt-in `ROOP_BENCH_CFG_OVERRIDE` JSON lever so a bench can vary one setting without rewriting `config.yaml`.
  **Not measured:** the 3060 (CUDA-EP path `_run_cuda_postprocess` has the same recombination but was not rendered), other clips, an A/B of `stabilize_enhancer` + this together.

- **Stabilization pool is now persistent and chunks overlap (the end-of-chunk barrier is gone); a pause no longer writes frames out of order. End-to-end fps NOT yet A/B-measured.**
  **Found (live 61,400-frame 720p render, 4070 + i9-14900K, one face, `restore_ultra`, 8 workers, 17.6 fps avg):** GPU util swung 13-98% on a ~10 s cycle (avg ~60%) with clocks and power healthy, CPU sat flat at ~24% of 32 logical cores (= exactly the 8 workers), the GIL was held in 72% of py-spy ticks, and `run_with_iobinding` (all GPU calls) was only 31% of active thread time. The 15<->24 fps sawtooth in the `[Pipeline]` lines is a measurement artefact: frames are emitted in whole 192-frame chunks, so a 100-frame window mostly reports where the chunk boundary fell. The real signal was a `py-spy dump` once per second counting live `stab_proc` threads: 8 alive for ~16 of every 17 samples, then 1-4 for 2-3 samples, then 0 at the join - roughly 7-10% of worker time idle at every chunk tail, and that is where the GPU troughs line up. This render had `blocks_that_fit=15` (budget 1011.8 MB vs 1012.4 MB for 16 blocks), so the "two rounds" escape in `_stab_parallel_geometry` missed by 0.06% and it ran one block per worker, the worst case for the tail.
  **Changed:** `_run_stab_parallel` spawned `stab_width` fresh threads per chunk and joined them all. It now starts `stab_width` daemon threads once, over one FIFO queue of (chunk, block) items, with up to two chunks in flight (`_stab_overlap_depth`): a worker that finishes the last block of chunk N takes a block of chunk N+1. Chunks are still retired strictly oldest-first into the write queue. Block grid, warm-up, per-block filter state and the warm-up dedup cache are untouched (FIFO means every block of N has started before any of N+1, so a dedup wait cannot form a cycle; `drop_before` runs when a chunk is retired, as before). Each block clears its thread's `self._tls` first, which reproduces the fresh-thread state it was written for. Persistent threads also keep their per-thread buffers (restorer IO bindings, CLAHE/LCT scratch) instead of rebuilding them every ~10 s. Stays at depth 1 when `(live+1) x chunk` would exceed 92% of the RAM free at render start, so the 16 GB laptop keeps its footprint; `ROOP_STAB_OVERLAP=0` restores one chunk at a time.
  **Fixed (pre-existing):** on a pause every worker stopped at ITS next frame, so the interrupted chunk's results were a prefix of each block; the writer emitted all the prefixes and, after resume, the earlier blocks' remainders AFTER later blocks' prefixes - frames out of order in the video. The interrupted chunk is now trimmed to the run contiguous from its start before it is written. `tests/test_stab_chunk_overlap.py::test_a_pause_with_two_chunks_in_flight_...` fails on the previous code (frame 36 written where 35 belongs) and passes now.
  **Measured:** synthetic straggler workload (4 workers, one slow block per chunk) 2.99 s -> 1.56 s with overlap; strict output order, exactly-once, warm-up carried across the chunk boundary, pool threads gone after the render. **End to end (`tests/ab_stab_overlap.py`, s7 600f 720p, live config, 8 threads, ABBA x4, null control first):** overlap off 8.87 fps vs on 9.08 (+2.3%), inside the null control's 6.5% spread (8.80-9.38) - NOT MEASURABLE. Same 653 faces seen and 100% swapped on all 8 arms; arm logs confirm `chunk overlap ON|off` matched each label. This clip is a weak test of the claim: 600 frames is two 384-frame chunks (almost no chunk tail), RAM was free enough for 2 blocks per worker (the slow render had 1), and it renders at ~9 fps, not the render's 18. GPU util was not logged. Still to run: Love.mp4 (3456f) with `ROOP_STAB_CHUNK_MB` pinned to reproduce 1 block per worker, with GPU util sampled per arm. A running backend keeps the old code until restarted.
  **Test edited:** `test_stab_warmup.py::test_dispatch_uses_the_memory_derived_width` pinned the old `n = max(1, min(stab_width, ...))` line by regex; it now pins the pool being sized by `stab_width` (the invariant, not the text).

- **Canary gates brief (Stage 10): one real install-time hole closed (`verify_ort.py` accepted a CPU-only runtime on an NVIDIA machine); the fps / utilisation thresholds are not built because the record says they cannot pass.**
  **Fixed:** `verify_ort.py --manifest-out` (the last step of `install.js`, which gates `install_state commit`) rejected only `active_provider == "none"`. `gpu_preflight` reports `CPUExecutionProvider` when the CUDA/TensorRT session drops (a DLL that will not load raises nothing in ORT; the `except` path in the preflight also sets CPU), and on a card under 7 GB TensorRT is not required, so that machine was committed as `verified` on the CPU - the install-time form of "reports success while not running". `verify_active_provider(hardware, preflight)` now makes CPU fatal when `hardware.vendor == "nvidia"` and names the preflight stage and reason; CUDA-only (the legitimate sub-7 GB outcome, even with a failed TensorRT stage) still passes, and non-NVIDIA hosts are unchanged. 7 tests in `test_verify_ort.py` (all fail against the old file; one pins that `verify_environment` calls the gate). Real run on the 4070: `verify_ort.py --manifest-out` exits 0 with `active_provider = TensorrtExecutionProvider`, TensorRT session test passed. The app's strict session factory already refuses such a machine at first launch; this refuses it at install, where the message can still say why.
  **Already exists (not rebuilt):** provider-must-not-be-CPU at run time (`create_strictly_accelerated_session` raises `StrictSessionError`; `predictor.verify_built`; `tests/test_gpu_bindings.py` runs a fresh interpreter through DLL registration to a CUDA session with `disable_cpu_ep_fallback`); render fidelity / identity / A-V / leak regression on real production renders with per-GPU goldens (`tests/test_performance_regression.py`: PSNR, SSIM, LPIPS on the face crop, an identity gate that fails a render which swapped nothing); the FP16-vs-FP32 auxiliary fidelity checks (`trt_precision_fidelity.py`, `quality_harness.py`, `aux_canary.py`) - which is why xseg / 2d106det / 1k3d68 build FP32 (FP16 failed: xseg IoU min 0.81, 1k3d68 p95 3.2 px).
  **Not built, and why:** (1) *fps >= 24 (3060) / >= 35 (4070)*: the live config renders this repo's clips at 14.6 (d4) and 18.6 (s7) fps on the 4070 at 600 frames / 20 threads (`docs/perf/ui_poll_contention_2026-10-11.md`), so a 35 fps assertion fails by construction, and the 3060 is not on this machine. The regression suite deliberately never fails on fps (AGENTS.md: the 4070 resolves ~50% effects, not 5%, and 120-300 frames measure warm-up); a 10-second clip is 300 frames. (2) *GPU utilisation >= 80%*: this pipeline is GPU-bound in latency but not in SM occupancy (utilisation ~30-65% across the recorded sessions), so the gate would fail on a healthy render. (3) *host CPU <= 35%*: never measured here, no basis for the number. (4) *0 MiB paged to WDDM shared memory*: no counter for it exists in the repo; the TensorRT workspace cap (2 GiB) is already enforced at build in `core.py`. A gate that fails on a healthy run is worse than none: it teaches people to ignore it. If wanted, record those four numbers per GPU as a measured baseline first and gate on regression from it, as the goldens do. **Noticed, not changed:** the root `benchmark_pipeline.py` benchmarks the `face_engine` masker, not the app's render path, and hardcodes `D:\k1.mp4` as its default video (AGENTS.md: never write a drive letter into a tool).

- **Distributed render / GOP-aligned chunk workers brief (Stage 9): not built, recorded as the result; no behaviour changed (a docstring in `distributed_render.py` now says why).**
  `distributed_render.py` is an orchestrator, not a render loop: it plans GOP boundaries from packets, stream-copies each slice (`-c:v copy`), launches one `run.py --project --render` child per chunk and joins with `-f concat -c copy`. The children are the ordinary pipeline, so the premise that it "continues executing unbatched CPU-bound loops and bypasses Stages 1-6" does not hold: NVDEC decode, the configured NVENC encoder, `create_strictly_accelerated_session` and the cross-frame swap batcher all run inside each child. Item by item: (1) *session factory* - the module creates no sessions; importing it would be dead code. (2) *device pinning / isolation* - already the process boundary (`CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=<uuid>` + `--cuda_device_id 0`; tests `test_each_worker_is_a_separate_process_pinned_to_one_gpu`, `test_workers_never_outnumber_chunks_and_never_share_a_gpu`), and `backend_manager.cache_namespace` keys the TensorRT cache on GPU name + SM + driver. (3) *NVDEC/NVENC for slicing* - slicing is a stream copy with an IDR check; replacing it with decode + encode would add a generation of loss and invalidate the GOP guarantee the concat relies on (`h264_cuvid`, as in Stage 6, is the same NVDEC engine as the `-hwaccel cuda` pipe the workers already use). (4) *concat -c copy* - already how chunks are joined. (5) *micro-batch B=4/B=8 in the worker loop* - there is no worker loop here; the cross-frame batcher exists in the child, and measured ON vs OFF it is 0.913x / 0.982x / 0.956x (`docs/perf/swap_batch_render_2026-10-11.md`), not a speedup.
  **Not exercised:** any host with two GPUs (both development machines have one, so this renders one chunk at a time), and two identical GPUs building the same missing TensorRT engine into the shared cache directory simultaneously on a cold cache (unverified risk; warming the cache with one worker first would be the mitigation).

- **Weight caching / embedding memoization brief (Stage 8): one real inefficiency removed (a duplicate protobuf parse); the memoization it asks for already exists.**
  **Changed:** `FaceSwapInsightFace.Initialize` called `onnx.load(model_path)` twice for every `normed_emap` model (inswapper, reswapper, instyleswapper): once to read the 512x512 identity matrix, again for the graph transforms. It now parses once (`_parse_swap_model`) and reads the emap from the pristine graph before any transform runs. Measured on the shipped files: inswapper_128 (554 MB) 0.21 s of duplicate parse removed per render start (0.42 s cold), emap bitwise identical (float32, array-equal) to the old path. hyperswap_1a (0.18 s) and hififace (0.09 s) never took the emap branch, so they had one parse and nothing to remove. Real-app check: a 90-frame `--swap-model inswapper` render through `Initialize`: rc 0, 108/108 faces swapped, TensorRT active, no tracebacks. `test_swap_model_single_parse.py` (3: emap equals the two-step path on a synthetic graph with a decoy 512x512 initializer, exactly one `onnx.load`, `Initialize` holds no parse of its own). **This is not the default path**: `realswap` (hyperswap + hififace) uses no emap, and the saving is 0.2 s once per render, not an "initialization stall" (the session build and TensorRT engine load are tens of seconds).
  **Already true, not changed:** source identity vectors are memoized twice over - on the Face object (`_latent_<model>`) and in the global thread-safe LRU `HyperSwapSourceCache` (`hyperswap_optimizer.py`, 128 entries, keyed on model + mode + hash of the embedding bytes, so two sources with the same vector share an entry, which a source-image-hash + bbox key would not do). V2 `.fsz` archives store their normalised embeddings, so loading a faceset runs no recognition; a legacy zip re-embeds once at load. The converter MLPs (ghost/simswap/hififace) run once per face per model for the same reason. Nothing recomputes a source embedding per frame; target-face embeddings are per detection and necessarily per frame (tracking already reuses them).
  **Not done, with the reason:** an `.npy` emap cache on disk (it would save the same 0.2 s only on a cold process, and adds a stale-file failure mode; one parse is already the floor for the transforms), and binding the latent as a resident GPU tensor. `CudaOrtIOBinding` already keeps persistent per-session CUDA buffers and copies the 2 KB latent into them per call; a pre-uploaded latent would save a ~2 KB host-to-device copy against ~100 ms of GPU work per face, and the render is GPU-bound (only removing GPU work per face moves the clock).

## 2026-10-11

- **UI isolation / streaming stabilization brief (Stage 7): one real gap closed (`/api/preview` ran beside a render); the rest already exists or is contradicted by measurement.**
  **Closed:** `POST /api/preview` had no backend guard. Past the config check it set the process-global `roop_globals.is_preview` (`session_pool.pool_size()` returns 1 for every thread while it is set, the render's workers included), ran `core.live_swap` on the shared GPU, and ended in `torch.cuda.empty_cache()`. The React panel defers its own previews while `progress.processing`, but the comparison-grid loader (`useGridPreviewLoader`, 3 call sites, no check; each cell is a full swap), a second tab and a direct API client do not. It now returns **409 `{render_active: true}` before any state is touched**, on the same `_progress["processing"]` flag the other busy-guards read (`_render_active()` in `api.py`; no second flag - `roop_globals.processing` and `_progress` already exist). `test_preview_render_lockout.py` (4; the two admission tests fail without the guard). Grid cells fail silently on the 409 (blank), by the loader's existing per-cell policy; gating the grid in the UI is not done.
  **Already true, not changed:** during a render the live view is `/api/live_frame` - a JPEG encoded once by `live_preview.py` at <= 2/s, 960 px, 3.7 ms per publish, served as bytes; `/api/progress` inlines no base64 (`live_frame: ""`); target stills are ETag/304 cached. `capturer.py` runs no inference (it reads frames; the brief's "triggers GPU inferencing" premise does not hold). HTTP 206 is served by FastAPI (`routes_output.py`: RFC 9110 ranges, streamed in chunks, no base64); there is no Express service in the app, so "isolate it from Python worker threads" would mean a new process, built on an unmeasured premise.
  **Not changed, contradicted by the record:** `ROOP_STAB_STREAMING=1` as default. Measured 2026-09-24 (4070, d4, 600f, ABBA): streaming 3.88 / 3.88 fps vs the threaded path 13.38 / 13.03 (3.4x); it was the default once and that was the regression (`runtime_scheduler.frame_pipeline_allowed`). The streaming path already caps in-flight full-resolution frames at four (README). TensorRT workspace is already capped at 2 GiB (`core.py`, `ROOP_TRT_MAX_WORKSPACE_BYTES`); the 3060 derives 0.3 x 6 GiB = 1.8 GiB but TensorRT is not admitted there, so a 1 GiB cap would be inert and unmeasured.
  **Not built:** the `verify_pipeline.py` FPS gate (>25 fps on the 3060, >35 fps on the 4070). It is the codec/faststart/206 check; the shipped live config renders this repo's clips at 7-19 fps on the 4070, so the 35 fps assertion would fail by construction, and the 3060 is not on this machine. The poll-contention A/B was then run (below).
  **Poll-contention A/B (`tests/ab_ui_poll_contention.py` + `tests/ui_poll_launcher.py`, report `docs/perf/ui_poll_contention_2026-10-11.md`).** The real `api.app` runs on a thread of the render process (as `run_api` does); a separate process plays the browser. 4070, live config, `--threads 20`, 600 frames, chunk pinned, idle/ui/stress/stress/ui/idle, null control, every arm proven from its log and client counters. **Realistic UI traffic (progress 1/s, live JPEG on `live_seq` change, one 512 KiB range per second): d4 1.028x, s7 1.003x of idle - inside the noise (null 0.34% / 0.75%); not reported as a speedup.** A hostile client (~170-290 req/s: 4 progress pollers at 100 ms, 2 live-frame at 50 ms, 2 random 1 MiB range readers at 50 ms) costs **7.2% on d4 (0.928x) and 13.3% on s7 (0.867x)**, outside the null and the stress-stress spread. Request p50 stays 2-10 ms in every arm; the tail is one 0.2-1.3 s stall seen by all three endpoints at the same moment (also under the light load), i.e. the render holding the GIL, not request volume - not located (no per-sample timestamps kept). So the premise that routine UI polling contends with the render is not supported, and the stress cost cannot be attributed to range reads vs progress vs live frames, so the Express proposal has no evidence behind it. Not measured: the 3060, the server thread's own cost (up in every arm), per-endpoint attribution, the `/ws/frames` socket, other thread counts and clips.

- **NVDEC/NVENC video I/O brief (Stage 6): the render path already had both; the one software encode left, `finalize_web_video`, now tries `h264_nvenc` first.**
  **Already in place, not changed:** the main pass decodes through `video_stream.open_video_capture` -> `NVHardwareVideoReader` (an ffmpeg `-hwaccel cuda` rawvideo bgr24 pipe into the worker queue; `ROOP_NVDEC=0` and the sub-7 GB card policy turn it off, with a software-pipe then cv2 fallback), and the render writer is `NVHardwareVideoWriter` for `h264/hevc/av1_nvenc` (`-rc vbr -cq -preset -tune hq`, `+faststart`, fractional `-r`). `restore_audio` re-encodes nothing (`-c:v copy -c:a copy`), so there is no encode to move; making it NVENC would add a lossy pass. The `cv2.VideoCapture` sites left are tools/benches, the extras routes' per-frame `get_video_frame` seeks, and `post_swap` (upscaler-bound). `-c:v h264_cuvid` was not substituted for `-hwaccel cuda` (same NVDEC engine, no measured gain, and the cuvid wrappers are the ones ffmpeg is deprecating).
  **Changed:** `util_ffmpeg.finalize_web_video` (used by the extras edit/enhance routes) encodes `-c:v h264_nvenc -preset p6 -tune hq -rc vbr -cq 19 -b:v 0 -pix_fmt yuv420p -movflags +faststart`, audio `-map 1:a:0? -c:a aac -b:a 192k`, the fractional rate as an exact rational via `-r` (`frame_rate_arg`), and the unchanged `pad=ceil(iw/2)*2:ceil(ih/2)*2` filter. If NVENC exits non-zero (no NVENC, driver, a frame size it rejects) it prints one line and re-runs the previous libx264 command byte-for-byte (the caller's `crf`, no `-r`). `ROOP_FINALIZE_NVENC=0` skips NVENC. `test_finalize_web_video_nvenc.py` (6, subprocess faked) pins both command lines, the retry order, the muted case and in-place finalize.
  **Checked on the 4070:** 3 s 24000/1001 clip with AAC audio -> H.264 yuv420p, 72/72 frames, `r_frame_rate` 24000/1001, no fallback line; `tools/test_ffmpeg_robustness.py` (odd 321x241, muted 161x121) and the three `restore_audio` suites pass. **Not measured:** speed (a 3 s 360p clip is dominated by NVENC session start-up: 0.40 s NVENC vs 0.22 s libx264, so no speedup is claimed), the 3060, and the full suite. **`-shortest` replaced** by `-t <video duration>` (ffprobe, as `restore_audio` does; `-shortest` is kept only if the duration is unknown). The frame drop `restore_audio` had was NOT reproduced here, even with audio ending at 2.9 s against a 3.003 s video (72/72 on NVENC and libx264, before and after), so this is hardening, not a fix.

- **Micro-batched `ProcessMgr` pipeline with GPU-resident crops (Stage 5 brief): not built, recorded as the result.** No code changed. Batched swap (`perf_batch_swap='auto'`) and swapper/enhancer IOBinding already exist; restorer batching (flat ~25 ms/face), aux batching (slower, removed), GPU pre/post (slower, reverted) and a VRAM-resident pipeline (decode/NVENC are host pipes; masks and compositing are NumPy/cv2) were each measured or ruled out earlier, and the render is GPU-bound. The brief also assumed a 12 GB 3060 (it is 6 GB), a sibling work-in-progress repository that no longer exists, and consecutive frames per worker (dispatch is `frame % num_threads`). The one unmeasured question - batching the `realswap` pair - was then measured at the owner's request (`docs/perf/swap_batch_render_2026-10-11.md`, `tests/ab_swap_batch_render.py`): the pair batching already exists, and cross-frame batching ON vs OFF is 0.913x (realswap d4), 0.982x (s7, noise) and 0.956x (hyperswap d4) on the 4070 at 8 threads, average batch 1.3-1.6. Not a speedup; defaults unchanged, flip is an owner decision. Not run at 20 threads or on other clips.
- **Auxiliary models in FP16 (xseg, 2d106det, 1k3d68): measured, NOT shipped - FP32 stays.** `tools/convert_fp16.py` (FP16 weights, FP32 I/O, exclusion strategies; fixes the converter's silent 1e4 constant clamp and float16 graph outputs) and `trt_precision_fidelity.py onnx16` (CUDA EP vs FP32 on 500 real faces). No strategy passes: xseg IoU min 0.81, 2d106det p95 0.79 px, 1k3d68 p95 3.2 px / kps5 9.3% IOD; excluding Sigmoid/Gemm/normalisation moves nothing (error is diffuse). FP16 is slower than FP32 on the CUDA EP for xseg (+9%) and 1k3d68 (+34%). No setting added; `ROOP_TRT_MODEL_PRECISION` is the override. Report `docs/perf/aux_fp16_onnx_2026-10-11.md`. 3060 not measured.
- **Dynamic-batch ONNX conversion that proves itself (`tools/convert_dynamic_shapes.py`), a TensorRT builder that now builds engines, and a strict-TensorRT assertion that could never pass.**
  **Converter.** Writes `<name>_dynamic.onnx` (input dim 0 -> `batch_size`, outputs, leading-1 `Reshape` shapes where shape inference proves the data's dim 0 is the batch) and runs `onnx.checker` - but a batch-1 export passes the checker, loads, accepts a batch of 4 and computes the wrong thing, so a model is written ONLY if `converted(batch N)[i] == original(sample i)` on the CPU provider for N in 1, 4, 8; a failure writes nothing (`--force` overrides, loudly). Shipped models: `inswapper_128`, `det_10g` and the real `scrfd_2.5g_kps` convert with a worst relative error of exactly 0 (inswapper needs no Reshape edit at all); `restoreformer_plus_plus` 3.9e-5, verifying only its image output `2701` (the export leaks 15 outputs). **`gpen_bfr_256` / `GPEN-BFR-512` are not convertible by graph surgery** and the tool says so: batch 4 is rejected at the encoder's flatten into the final Linear; with that patched by hand, batch 2 is rejected in the generator's styled convolutions, whose per-sample weights `[B,256,512,3,3]` are reshaped to `[256,512,3,3]` for one `Conv` (undoing it needs a runtime `groups=B`; ONNX `Conv.group` is static).
  **Three things only the numeric check found.** (1) SCRFD folds the batch into axis 0 *interleaved per cell* (`index = (cell*N + sample)*anchors + anchor`, 2 anchors per cell), not batch-major - my first checker assumed batch-major and reported 87% error on a correct model; the checker now finds and prints the layout, which any decoder must unfold. (2) A graph's extra, statically-shaped outputs pin the batch: ORT treats a declared output shape as fact and constant-folds `Shape()` of it, so RestoreFormer++'s leaked `[1,64,512,512]` outputs made batch 4 fail in a group-norm reshape until every output's dim 0 was relaxed. (3) A blanket "leading 1 -> 0" rewrote GPEN's weight reshapes and broke batch 1; the rule is now gated on provable batch.
  **`tools/build_trt_engines.py`.** Models marked `dynamic` are built from the verified `_dynamic.onnx` with the profiles in the brief (inswapper `target:1x3x128x128,source:1x512` / `4x` / `8x`; SCRFD `1x3x640x640` / `4x` / `8x`); one that cannot be converted is built as static batch 1 and labelled so. Workspace 2048 MiB on the 4070 tier, 1024 MiB below 7 GiB (was 4096/1536).
